# Loop questions & assumptions

Format: `- [ASSUMED|BLOCKED|QUESTION] <item> · <decision or question> · why · how to revert`

- ASSUMED M0.1 · D6 default permission mode = `acceptEdits`, not `auto` · auto mode is gated by model (debug log: "model claude-haiku-4-5-20251001 does not support auto mode") and D11 only allows Haiku, so M0 could not prove auto works headless; the CLI silently downgrades an unsupported `auto` to `default` (which denies edits headless) · revert: once verified with a supported model, switch the default to `auto` and keep the `init.permissionMode` mismatch check (docs/spike-m0.md → Permission modes)
- ASSUMED M0.1 · Switchboard does not use `claude --bg` / `claude attach`; the live --bg round trip was not probed · the sandbox is untrusted and accepting the trust prompt writes ~/.claude.json (outside D12); --bg sessions are TUI-driven, so no stream-json/answers · revert: probe `claude --bg` in an already-trusted folder if bg/attach integration is wanted
- ASSUMED M0.1 · fixtures are byte-identical captures except the home dir (`/Users/<name>` → `/Users/dev`, `-Users-<name>` → `-Users-dev`) · keeps the developer's username out of committed files; NDJSON validity unaffected · revert: re-capture with `.spike/probe.mjs` and skip the scrub in `.spike/export.mjs`
- ASSUMED M0.2 · the default permission mode stays `acceptEdits`; auto is still unproven headless · `initialize.models[]` shows `supportsAutoMode` for default/opus/sonnet but not haiku, and `set_permission_mode auto` on Haiku returns `error_code: auto_mode_model`; D11 forbids probing another model · revert: at spawn send `initialize` → if the model `supportsAutoMode`, send `set_permission_mode auto`, stay on acceptEdits on any error (docs/spike-m0.md → M0.2 → D6)
- ASSUMED M0.2 · the `ctl-init` fixture replaces the account email and organization from the `initialize` response with `dev@example.com` / `Example Org` (on top of the M0.1 home-dir scrub) · keeps personal data out of committed files; NDJSON validity unaffected · revert: re-capture with `.spike/probe2.mjs` (PROBE_INIT=1) and skip the account scrub in `.spike/export2.mjs`
- ASSUMED M0.2 · (b) Agent SDK and (c) PreToolUse-hook bridge were not probed · (a) the native control protocol (`--permission-prompt-tool stdio`) worked end to end and the item says to stop at the first that works · revert: probe (b)/(c) in `.spike/` if a later CLI drops the stdio prompt tool
- ASSUMED M0.3 · transcript fixtures live in `tools/fake-claude/fixtures/transcripts/<scenario>.jsonl` (Claude's own extension) and are line-filtered: whole lines kept verbatim with the home-dir scrub, but the 12 `attachment` lines carrying the machine's CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md/memory, system prompt, account e-mail, org id and tool/skill/agent/MCP listings are dropped (manifest `transcripts` section lists them) · the raw transcript embeds the developer's instructions/memory and account identifiers; History needs none of them · revert: change the attachment allowlist in `.spike/export3.mjs` and re-export (the raw transcript is still in ~/.claude/projects)
- ASSUMED M0.3 · `usage-ctl`/`usage-turn` fixtures replace the money amounts in the `get_usage` response (`extra_usage.monthly_limit`, `spend.*.amount_minor`) with 0, on top of the home-dir scrub · account billing data, irrelevant to the meter; utilization values are kept · revert: drop `scrubMoney` in `.spike/export3.mjs` and re-export from `.spike/raw/`
- ASSUMED M0.3 · `/usage` and `/cost` slash-command outputs are documented but not exported as fixtures · their text includes a usage attribution computed from the developer's own sessions · revert: re-run `node .spike/probe3.mjs slash-usage -- -p "/usage" --output-format stream-json --verbose --model haiku --max-turns 1` and export with a scrub of the attribution block
- ASSUMED M0.3 · recommended usage source is the stdin control request `get_usage` (marked experimental in the SDK) plus each turn's `rate_limit_event`; anything missing/erroring → "unknown" · it is the only source that returns both windows as numbers without a model call, and it matched `/usage` and `rate_limit_event` exactly · revert: M9.2 can use `rate_limit_event` alone (updates only when a session makes an API call)
- ASSUMED M0.4 · the automated "terminal" steps use a text-mode `claude -p --resume <id> "<prompt>"`; interactive TTY behavior (entrypoint `cli`, id kept, picker hiding sdk-cli sessions) is taken from the 2.1.283 binary's loader code and left to the manual steps in docs/spike-m0.md → M0.4 · an interactive TUI cannot be driven headless, and a pty wrapper in the untrusted sandbox would hit the trust prompt, whose acceptance writes ~/.claude.json (outside D12) · revert: run the manual steps and record the outcome in docs/spike-m0.md
- ASSUMED M0.4 · the "attach while the terminal is open" probe (`handoff-conc`) used a live `-p` stream-json process as the stand-in for an open interactive terminal · same reason; the relevant behavior (in-memory chain + append to the shared file) is the same loader and writer · revert: repeat with a real `claude --resume <id>` left open in a terminal while the service attaches
- ASSUMED M0.4 · text-mode terminal runs are recorded in a new `textRuns` section of fixtures/manifest.json (argv, cwd, exit, `stdoutText`) instead of `<scenario>.ndjson` files · their stdout is plain text, not NDJSON, and the item requires every fixture to parse as NDJSON; keeping them out of `scenarios` also keeps a loader that expects a stdout file per scenario working · revert: re-run the steps with `--output-format stream-json --verbose` and export them as normal scenarios
- ASSUMED M0.4 · the handoff transcripts (`transcripts/handoff*.jsonl`) use the M0.3 line filter (instructions/memory/system prompt/account/listing attachments dropped, home dir scrubbed) · same privacy reason as M0.3 · revert: change the allowlist in `.spike/export4.mjs` and re-export from `.spike/raw/*.tx.jsonl`
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · D6 fallback applied: every spawn passes `--permission-mode acceptEdits`; the documented auto switch (`initialize` → `supportsAutoMode` → `set_permission_mode auto`) is not enabled (BACKLOG M2.1, ARCHITECTURE → Permission mode) · D6 makes auto conditional on M0 proving it headless, and M0 could not (model-gated, D11 allows only Haiku) · revert: enable the switch in M2.1 after verifying auto with a supported model; keep the init.permissionMode mismatch flag
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · M2.1 child env drops `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_*`, `CLAUDE_PID`, `CLAUDE_EFFORT` and keeps `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; normal runs pass no `--model`/`--max-turns`; `--forward-subagent-text` + `--replay-user-messages` are always on · the spike's recommended baseline; the extra-args env var covers the D13 Haiku smoke · revert: narrow the scrub list if a developer relies on a `CLAUDE_CODE_*` var; drop either flag in the supervisor argv builder
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · M1.2 fake-claude surface: scenario chosen by `FAKE_CLAUDE_SCENARIO` or a `[fake:<scenario>]` token in a stdin user message, `[fake:write <path>]` does a real Write under its cwd, `FAKE_CLAUDE_LOG` records argv + stdin, `hang`/`crash` scenarios, a live-process file `sessions/<pid>.json` under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` that `agents --json` lists; `auth status` is exit-code only (the real output was never captured); `--fork-session` is not needed (Switchboard never forks) · smallest surface that lets D13's real-path E2E (question → answer, pause/resume, detach/attach, Diff) run without the real CLI · revert: rename the env/tokens in tools/fake-claude and its tests
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · M3.1 a `multiSelect` question is answered with one option, because the locked contract carries one `answerIndex` per question; the CLI accepts one label · no contract change inside this item; answering a subset is valid input · revert: add an optional `answerIndexes[]` to `contracts/local-api.md` and send the labels joined with ", " (the format recorded in `ask-multiselect`)
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · M3.1 a question's source is always the session's main agent; no subagent attribution is parsed from `header` or the text · M0.2: subagents have no AskUserQuestion, so the main agent relays; inferring a subagent from free text would be a guess · revert: map a header/text prefix to an agent in M3.1 if the developer wants the prototype's per-subagent sources
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · M3.1 stale requests (cancelled by pause/detach or a dead process): a stale question batch stays answerable and its answers go to the session as a normal user message when it next runs; a stale permission item closes with no decision sent · M0.2: the CLI never re-asks after resume and a dead request must not be answered; keeping the answer avoids losing the developer's input, and nothing is allowed on their behalf · revert: close stale batches too (the developer then types in the chat)
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · M2.4 restart: a leftover child of a session (recorded pid, confirmed by `claude agents --json` with the same session id) is stopped with SIGINT → SIGTERM → SIGKILL before `--resume`; `run` sessions get "Switchboard restarted. Continue."; `need` sessions resume idle and get the note with their answers · M0.4: two live processes on one id fork the conversation; a crashed parent leaves children that keep working without a permission host; sending "Continue." to a session waiting for an answer would let it proceed without the answer · revert: wait for leftovers to exit instead of stopping them, and/or send "Continue." to `need` sessions as well
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · M4.1 Attach warns (does not refuse) when the transcript changed < 2 min ago (gap #5) **or** `claude agents --json` lists the id as live; the UI shows the warning before calling `/attach`, using liveness fields added to SessionDetail (an additive response field; the contract table is unchanged) · an open idle terminal does not touch the transcript, so mtime alone misses it (M0.3/M0.4) · revert: drop the agents check, or move the warning into a 409-and-confirm flow on `/attach`
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · M4.1/M4.3 the handoff copy stays `claude --resume <id>` (prototype), not `cd "<root>" && claude --resume <id>`; while detached, questions asked in the terminal are answered there (Switchboard owns no process), and the prototype sentence "Switchboard keeps showing notifications and syncs back when you attach" is kept verbatim (D10) · copy is the prototype's; sync-back happens on attach via the transcript · revert: change the copy, or tail the transcript while detached to notify about pending terminal questions
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · M5.2 the confirmed answers go only into the first stdin user message (task first, then the answers block); `--append-system-prompt` is not used; if the agent re-asks, the batch goes to the Inbox and is never auto-answered · the only probed channel; the router gives the developer the first move on task definition; auto-answering would answer on the developer's behalf · revert: also pass the block via `--append-system-prompt` after probing it; see decisions.md "Open for the developer" about a router amendment
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · M7.4 History lists Switchboard sessions + terminal-started sessions (`entrypoint:"cli"`, not in the DB); headless `sdk-cli` transcripts not in the DB and empty stubs are hidden · gap #5 names only terminal-started sessions; other headless tools' sessions are unspecified · revert: include `sdk-cli` rows with a "headless" mode line
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · M9.2 `usagePct` = the higher of the 5-hour and weekly utilization (the contract has one optional field and the footer one "Max" bar; Settings says "5-hour window and weekly limit"); warning once per window until its `resets_at`; readings via `get_usage` between turns ≤ 1/60 s, `rate_limit_event` per turn, and a short-lived poller only while a /hub client is connected and no session is live, ≤ once per 5 min · the spike's suggestion; the 5-min poller bound keeps idle cost low (each poller runs the user's SessionStart hooks, ~3.5 s) · revert: `usagePct` = 5-hour only (the wizard copy), or change the cadence constants
- ASSUMED M0-adapt · besides the items listed in the brief, M1.3 got a pointer to the M0 stored fields and ARCHITECTURE's "Usage meter (M0.3)" placeholder section was filled in; the data model section and `contracts/local-api.md` were not touched · M1.3 requires every field up front, and the usage section was explicitly an M0 placeholder · revert: `git revert` the M0-adapt commit's hunks for those two spots
- ASSUMED M1.1 · `@playwright/test` pinned to 1.62.1 (latest is 1.63.0) · its Chromium revision 1234 is the one already cached in ~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright (1.63.0 needs 1243), so no browser download; the item said not to run `npx playwright install` · revert: bump the pin to 1.63.0 and run `npx playwright install chromium` (D12)
- ASSUMED M1.1 · allowed Host = `127.0.0.1:<port>` / `localhost:<port>` only (name case-insensitive, port exact); allowed Origin = exactly `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` / `http://localhost:<port>`; `[::1]`, other 127.x, other ports (other local apps such as :13000) and `null` are foreign · the server binds 127.0.0.1 only, and SameSite ignores ports, so the Origin port check is what stops other loopback apps · revert: extend `LOOPBACK_NAMES` / `isAllowedOrigin` in src/server/security.ts
- ASSUMED M1.1 · `sb_token` = session cookie (`Path=/; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict`, no Max-Age/Domain/Secure), set again on every qualifying page load; a page load with no `Sec-Fetch-Site` header gets no cookie · gap #20 names only none/same-origin; a session cookie is the shorter-lived credential · revert: add `Max-Age` in `serializeTokenCookie`, accept a missing header in `mayIssueCookie`
- ASSUMED M1.1 · default-deny: every route (unknown paths included, 401 before 404) needs the cookie except routes marked `config.public` (only the UI page + static files), and `/api*` / `/hub*` stay protected even if marked public; guard errors are JSON `{error:"forbidden-host"|"forbidden-origin"}` (403) / `{error:"unauthorized"}` (401) · the contract defines no error shapes; default-deny keeps later routes safe without per-route opt-in · revert: src/server/security.ts
- ASSUMED M1.1 · the workspace-root variable (removed by D14) has no default (`null` = not configured) · deriving it from the repo location (`<workspace>/other/switchboard` → `../..`) would be an inference · revert: add a default in `loadConfig`

- ASSUMED M1.1 · Linux data dir honours an absolute `$XDG_DATA_HOME` before `~/.local/share/switchboard`; the token lives at `<dataDir>/sb_token` (0600, dir 0700) and a corrupt one is replaced · XDG default equals the documented path · revert: `defaultDataDir` in src/server/config.ts

- ASSUMED M1.2 · `rate_limit_event` is replayed where it was recorded (every multi-turn recording has it only in the process's first turn), not injected into every turn as the BACKLOG wording "a rate_limit_event per turn" suggests · fidelity to the real CLI: a supervisor that counted on one per turn would pass against the fake and fail against the CLI · revert: in tools/fake-claude/session.ts `runTurn`, append the process's first rate_limit_event line before each turn's result when the turn has none
- ASSUMED M1.2 · with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` unset the fake writes no transcript / live-process file (stderr warning) and refuses `--resume` (exit 1); it never falls back to `~/.claude` like the real CLI · a test that forgot the variable must not write fake sessions into the developer's real History · revert: default configDir to `~/.claude` in `Runner` (tools/fake-claude/session.ts)
- ASSUMED M1.2 · unrecorded exit/EOF combinations: EOF exits 1 iff the last `result` was an error (matches every recording; extends to aborted_streaming / error_max_turns in stream-json mode); EOF at a recorded wait point with no open request keeps waiting (a "running" tool or `hang` never finishes, so a dead supervisor leaves a live child for M2.4); EOF with a request open fails it with the M0.2 text `Tool permission request failed: AbortError: Stream closed` and ends the turn with the default reply; `--resume <unknown id>` prints `No conversation found with session ID: <id>` and exits 1 at startup (text/exit code not captured in M0) · smallest rules consistent with the recordings; no test can hang on EOF except the deliberate hang · revert: `pump` / `afterRequest` / `Transcript.open` in tools/fake-claude
- ASSUMED M1.2 · replies after a host decision: the assistant text after an answer is the recorded one (it does not re-read the chosen options); a decision that differs from the recording continues with the sibling recording (perm-allow ⇄ perm-deny, ask-interrupt answered → ask-delay) or else the default reply ("OK"); after a scenario's turns are used up every further message plays the default turn; `[fake:<scenario>]` switches the scenario for that and later messages · a replay fake has no model; siblings keep the stream coherent · revert: SIBLINGS / DEFAULT_FIXTURE in tools/fake-claude/scenarios.ts
- ASSUMED M1.2 · invented where M0 captured nothing: `auth status` prints nothing (exit code only), `agents` without `--json` prints `id status name cwd` lines and ignores `--all`, an unknown control subtype gets `{subtype:"error", error:"Unsupported control request subtype: <x>"}`, `[fake:write <path>]` writes `written by fake-claude\n` and exits 1 for a path outside the cwd, the synthetic `No response requested.` line follows either interrupt marker (recorded only after the "for tool use" one), `crash` leaves `sessions/<pid>.json` behind (SIGTERM removes it), live `name` = `--name` or the cwd folder name · none of these outputs is read by Switchboard; all are documented in docs/fake-claude.md · revert: the matching spots in tools/fake-claude
- ASSUMED M1.2 · the brief's "fail scenario (error result / non-zero exit)" is covered by the BACKLOG's `crash` (exit 1 unasked) plus `max-turns` / `--max-turns` (error result); no separate `fail` name was added · the BACKLOG item is the spec and names `crash` · revert: add `fail` to BUILT_IN_SCENARIOS in tools/fake-claude/scenarios.ts
- ASSUMED M1.3 · `node:sqlite` has only a synchronous API, so the repository methods return Promises over short synchronous calls (no worker thread); each call runs without yielding and is atomic, and multi-row writes use one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction with a synchronous callback · D4 mandates node:sqlite and AGENTS asks for async end to end; a Promise API keeps call sites async so the driver can move later · revert: put the Store behind a worker thread with the same Promise API (docs/database.md → Repository layer)
- ASSUMED M1.3 · schema beyond the ARCHITECTURE minimum so later lanes need no schema change: questions split into `question_batches` (verbatim input, state, delivery) + `questions` (a question's state = its batch's); permission requests and system items as their own tables (the Inbox merges them with open batches); new `pending_messages` outbox (stale answers, restart note), `usage_readings`, `history_cache`; extra nullable/defaulted columns (session task/cwd/stop_reason/schedule_id/detached_at/ended_at/last_activity_at, agent kind/status_text, event end_ts/uuid/message_id/tool_use_id, artifact path/url/data, loop label/breaker_state/next_fire_at/iterations/progress_path/note, tool description/position, worktree repo_path/base_ref/pr_url/pr_checked_at/removed_at) · the item requires every entity up front; nothing here is read by the UI yet · revert: a new migration dropping what no item uses
- ASSUMED M1.3 · CHECK constraints only on enums locked by the contract/ARCHITECTURE (session status, work_type, mode, phase, coordination, qa_stack, event kind, artifact type, question state; list columns must be JSON arrays); agent kind/status, permission/system-item states, schedule-run result/trigger, loop kind, usage source, pending-message kind are TS unions without a DB CHECK · later lanes can extend those without a migration · revert: add CHECKs in a new migration

## Wave 2 · lane w2-session (M4.1, M4.2, M4.3; merged 06fc930)
- ASSUMED M4.1 · The Attach warning is enforced by the service: `POST /api/sessions/{id}/attach` answers `409 {error:"attach-warning", message, reasons}` unless the (additive, optional) body is `{ "confirm": true }`; success still returns the contract's `{ resumeCommand }` · why: gap #5 says warn first, and a check made only in the UI could race with a terminal or be skipped by another client; a fork silently loses turns (M0.4) · revert: drop the `confirm` gate in `SessionSupervisor.#attachNow` and let the UI ask through a separate check.
- ASSUMED M4.1 · When `claude agents --json` cannot be read, the warning still shows (`liveness-unknown`) rather than assuming no terminal · why: the most conservative option against a fork · revert: remove that reason in `attachWarnings` (src/server/supervisor/attach.ts).
- ASSUMED M4.1 · Warning copy and look (the prototype has none): "The transcript changed n s ago. / A claude process (pid n) has this session open. / Switchboard could not check… Attaching while a terminal still has the session open forks the conversation. Close it there first, or attach anyway." with "Attach anyway" (primary) / "Cancel" (outlined), in a question-card-coloured card under the header row · why: SPEC copy rules (plain, factual, sentence case) and the existing warning-card style (M6.3) · revert: `src/web/views/session/session-header.ts` + `SessionHeader.tsx`.
- ASSUMED M4.1 · Header chips = the session-start answers in the prototype's New-session words (`nsLaunch`: work, mode, phase, scope, ultracode on in blue) + `stack` for QA + one blue chip per `loops` row (`run` for kind `Workflow`, else `loop`, value = label or kind). Coordination is not shown (nsLaunch omits it). The mock sessions' hand-written chips (`bp 360`, `cap …`, `contract …`, `runbook …`, `expires …`, path-style scope) have no source and are not shown (D13) · revert: `src/core/derive/chips.ts`.
- ASSUMED M4.1 · `Session` (provisional type owned by M4.1) gains additive fields `cwd`, `live` (a pid is recorded), `resumeCommand`, `chips`; the `/hub` contract key test lists them · revert: remove them from `toSession` and `src/core/api.ts` (and `tests/server/hub/hub.test.ts`).
- ASSUMED M4.1 · Pause/Resume label: "Pause" while the session has a live process or its status is `run`/`need`, else "Resume"; the button is disabled while detached (Resume would be refused 409 `detached`) · why: the prototype toggles on a flag the app does not have; D7 Resume applies to a stopped process · revert: `pauseButton` in `session-header.ts`.
- ASSUMED M4.1 · Imported terminal turns are events with the transcript's timestamps (sorted between "Continued in a terminal" and "Attached"), user origin `terminal`, attributed to the main agent; subagents and artifacts are not derived from terminal turns; a sync point missing from the transcript imports nothing and records an error event · revert: `importTerminalTurns` in attach.ts, `transcript-sync.ts`.
- ASSUMED M4.1 · Built early for the oracle ("the command is shown", "the terminal turn appears in the chat"): the handoff card (prototype copy, M4.3's panel) at the top of the right panel, and a minimal ChatTab message list (user / assistant bubbles, M4.2's tab) · revert: M4.2 / M4.3 extend or replace `ChatTab.tsx`, `chat.ts`, `HandoffCard.tsx`, `RightPanel.tsx`.
- ASSUMED M4.1 · The demo seed sets each demo session's `cwd` to the demo workspace root (`D:\acme`, solutions.json `root`) so the header shows the prototype's root line · revert: remove `cwd:` in `src/server/demo/seed.ts`.
- ASSUMED M4.1 · Visual oracle on two sessions: `calendar-func-fix` (every box absolute) and `free-talk-feature` (its hand-written chips wrap in the prototype, so the chip row, header height and tabs are compared relative to the chip row) · revert: `tests/e2e/visual/session-header.spec.ts`.
- ASSUMED M4.2 · `SessionDetail` (provisional, M4.1's) gains an additive `questions: Question[]`: every batch of the session, oldest first, in the contract's `Question` shape · why: the contract's detail has no questions, the Inbox lists only waiting batches, and the chat also needs answered ones for the answers bubble (SPEC) · revert: drop `questions` from `toSessionDetail` / `SessionDetail` (src/server/sessions/wire.ts, src/core/api.ts) and read waiting batches from `GET /api/inbox` filtered by session.
- ASSUMED M4.2 · The chat shows only the main agent's events (and events of no agent); a subagent's own text, tools and prompts stay in its agent card / the timeline, its `Agent · …` call is the main agent's step line · why: the prototype chat is the orchestrator's conversation; `--forward-subagent-text` would otherwise interleave every subagent's work · revert: remove the agent filter in `chatItems` (src/web/views/session/chat.ts).
- ASSUMED M4.2 · Step lines = tool calls (`✓` result, `✕` error result, `●` no result yet), permission requests (`⏸` open, `✓` allowed, `✕` otherwise), automatic denials, failed turns and a permission-mode mismatch (`✕`), each `<mark> <event label>` under the agent text before it; successful results and lifecycle steps are not shown · why: the prototype shows tool lines with these marks under the agent message; the labels are the derivations' (docs/derivations.md) · revert: `stepMark` / `chatItems`.

- ASSUMED M4.2 · `answeredLines` (M3.1's shared helper) now prints the source's part before " · " (prototype `ssAnswered`), e.g. `web: Wrap to 2 rows (as Figma)` · revert: src/web/components/question-card.ts.
- ASSUMED M4.3 · Where an agent works: its first **successful** write (Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit) into a solution sets `solutionPath` = the solution's workspace-relative folder in the prototype's form (`microfrontends/<repo>`, `mobile/`; `solutionFolder` in src/core/derive/artifacts.ts) and `branch` = the session's registered worktree holding the file; later writes elsewhere do not move it, root files place nobody · why: the panel's path + ⎇ branch and the "n solutions · n branches" summary need real data (D13), the stream never says where an agent works, and reading its prompt would be an inference; a write is a fact · revert: remove `#placeAgent` (and `ToolEntry.agentId`) in src/server/supervisor/recorder.ts.
- ASSUMED M4.3 · An agent that has not written into a solution shows the path `workspace root` (every agent runs at the session's cwd) · revert: `WORKSPACE_ROOT` fallback in `agentCards` (src/web/views/session/right-panel.ts).
- ASSUMED M4.3 · The main agent (no description of its own) shows the first line of the session's task as its description · why: the card's desc slot is the agent's job; the task is the main agent's job, not an inference · revert: `agentCards`.
- ASSUMED M4.3 · Status copy = the agent's status text (a subagent's task_progress description) else the prototype's words `needs you` / `running` / `done` / `paused` (+ `failed`, `idle`); while the session is paused, agents the pause cut off (`idle`) read `paused`; the prototype's `resuming` (its mock after Send) is not derived · revert: `STATUS_WORDS` / `agentCards`.
- ASSUMED M4.3 · The summary keeps the prototype's template verbatim, plural words even for 1 (`1 agents · 1 solutions · 1 branches`) · why: exact copy is the D10 gate and the header's wrap depends on the text length (a pluralized first try shortened it, unwrapped the header and moved every box 14 px); the spec is the spec · revert: pluralize in `agentSummary` (and the unit/E2E/visual expectations).
- ASSUMED M4.3 · Terminal tail = the session's recent events as lines: Bash (any agent) `$ <first command line>` + the last 3 non-empty output lines; AskUserQuestion ⏸/✓; a subagent's other tool calls (bare while running, ✓/✕), the main agent's are left to the chat; permission requests ⏸/✓/✕; denials ✕; permission-mode mismatch ⚠; turn results = their text (✕ when failed); lifecycle labels (✕ when failed); `[<agent>] ` prefix for subagents; cursor `▍` while status is `run`; the newest 8 lines; tones = the prototype's `lineColor` · why: the prototype's terminal is free mock text; this is the conservative real equivalent that complements the chat · revert: `terminalLines` / `eventLines` in right-panel.ts.
- ASSUMED M4.3 · Demo seed: each prototype terminal line becomes a finished turn result (`type: 'result'`, kind `ok`) with the line as its text, so the demo tail renders through the real renderer verbatim; `▍` is not stored (derived from status `run`); button-rollout's tail additionally shows M4.2's `⏸ breaker…` request step · revert: the terminal loop + `demoResult` in src/server/demo/seed.ts (and tests/server/demo/seed.test.ts).

## M9 · M9.1 Per-user background service (on main after Wave 2; M8.2's Settings view still in the unmerged lane/w1-tools)
- ASSUMED M9.1 · "Start at login" has its own additive route `GET/PUT /api/service` (`{ manager, startAtLogin, file }`, 409 `{ error, message }` refusals) instead of a key in `PUT /api/settings`; after every change the state is mirrored into the settings key `service.startAtLogin` that M8.2's `GET /api/settings` already reads (read-only there, "M9.1 writes it") · why: the setting's effect is an OS service definition with refusals of its own (no Node ≥ 24, a failed launchctl/systemctl/schtasks) that the settings PUT has no shape for, and M8.2's settings route lives in the unmerged lane · how to revert: at the w1-tools merge, let `PUT /api/settings` accept `service.startAtLogin` and call `LoginService.setStartAtLogin`, then drop `src/server/api/service.ts` + the two client calls
- ASSUMED M9.1 · the toggle sits in the M1.4 placeholder `SettingsView` (only the "Start at login" row on `/settings` and `/settings/claude`) until M8.2 is merged · why: the real Settings view is in lane/w1-tools · how to revert / merge note: take the lane's `SettingsView.tsx`; in its `ClaudeSection` (`src/web/views/settings/sections.tsx`) replace the `start-at-login` row's `<Value>{onOff(settings['service.startAtLogin'])}</Value>` with `<StartAtLoginToggle />` (`src/web/views/settings/StartAtLogin.tsx`); the lane's demo seed stores `service.startAtLogin: true` and the demo `LoginService` also starts on, so its visual spec's `login` value stays "on"; `tests/e2e/start-at-login.spec.ts` + `tests/e2e/visual/start-at-login.spec.ts` find the row by `data-row="start-at-login"` / its label, which the lane's `Row` keeps
- ASSUMED M9.1 · Windows uses a Task Scheduler logon task (the backlog's first option), not a Windows Service; it runs `node.exe` under the user's interactive token, so a console window is visible at logon (closing it stops the service) · why: a Windows Service or a hidden-window launcher needs code outside this one Node project; the task is per-user and needs no admin · how to revert: swap the `<Exec>` for a hidden launcher or register a service instead (`taskSchedulerXml` in `src/core/service-files.ts`)
- ASSUMED M9.1 · no automatic restart on any OS (launchd `KeepAlive` false, systemd `Restart=no`, no Task Scheduler `RestartOnFailure`) · why: every service start resumes the live sessions with "Switchboard restarted. Continue." (M2.4), so a crash loop would keep resuming them · how to revert: `KeepAlive {SuccessfulExit false}` / `Restart=on-failure` + a start limit / `RestartOnFailure` in the generators
- ASSUMED M9.1 · the toggle only registers for the **next** sign-in and only unregisters: it never starts a second instance (the running one holds the port) and never stops the running service (it may be the one answering); starting now is the install script's `--start`, stopping is the uninstall script · how to revert: add the start / stop steps to `LoginService` (`installPlan(target, { start: true })`, `uninstallPlan(…, { stop: true })`)
- ASSUMED M9.1 · names and places: launchd label `local.switchboard` (`~/Library/LaunchAgents/local.switchboard.plist`, output to `<dataDir>/logs/service.log`), systemd unit `switchboard.service` in `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/systemd/user` (journal), task `Switchboard` with its XML + `--env-file` file in `<dataDir>\service\` · why: no domain to build a reverse-DNS label from; one app-data folder per ARCHITECTURE → Security · how to revert: the constants / `servicePaths` in `src/core/service-files.ts`
- ASSUMED M9.1 · the installed service carries the configuration it was installed from: the workspace-root variable (removed by D14) and, when not default, port / data folder / `SWITCHBOARD_CLAUDE_BIN` / `SWITCHBOARD_GH_BIN`, plus a cleaned PATH (absolute, deduplicated, without npm's `node_modules/.bin` + `node-gyp-bin`) for launchd / systemd; never `SWITCHBOARD_CLAUDE_EXTRA_ARGS` / `SWITCHBOARD_DEMO`; Windows gets them through `node --env-file` because Task Scheduler cannot set variables · how to revert: `carriedEnvironment` / `cleanSearchPath` in `src/server/service/target.ts`
- ASSUMED M9.1 · test redirects `SWITCHBOARD_SERVICE_HOME` + `SWITCHBOARD_SERVICE_CTL` (must be set together, else exit 1) and `tools/fake-servicectl` · why: D12 + D13: the toggle's real code path runs end to end in tests without touching this machine's home or service manager · how to revert: drop `loadServiceRedirect` from `main.ts` / the scripts
- ASSUMED M9.1 · copy kept verbatim: "Launch the service when you sign in (Windows / macOS)" although Linux is supported too · why: exact copy (D10) · how to revert: change `START_AT_LOGIN_DESCRIPTION`

## M9 · M9.2 Usage meter (on main after M9.1; M5.3's `GET /api/system` + `SystemProbe` still in the unmerged lane/w2-newsession, M8.2's Settings in lane/w1-tools)
- ASSUMED M9.2 · the meter makes requests (live `get_usage` and the poller) only while a `/hub` client is connected; the backlog restricts only the poller that way · why: nobody sees the meter otherwise, and on connect the next tick (≤ 15 s) reads at once; `rate_limit_event` readings are still stored and warnings still evaluated without a viewer · how to revert: drop the `this.#viewers() <= 0` guard in `UsageMeter.#tick` (src/server/usage/meter.ts) and move it in front of the poller branch
- ASSUMED M9.2 · the poller is also skipped while any reading younger than 5 min exists (a live session or a turn just read it), on top of "at most once per 5 min" · why: each poller run is a CLI process that runs the user's SessionStart hooks (~1.6 s, M0.3); stricter, never more calls · how to revert: remove the `latest.receivedAt` check in `UsageMeter.#tick`
- ASSUMED M9.2 · a failed read (error `control_response`, no answer from a live session in 15 s or it ended, CLI missing, poller exit without an answer or past its 30 s limit → SIGTERM/SIGKILL) is stored as a reading with both windows `null`, so `usagePct` is unknown until the next good reading · why: backlog "unknown when get_usage errors"; never keep showing an older number after a failure · how to revert: skip `record()` for `failed` outcomes in `UsageMeter.#tick`
- ASSUMED M9.2 · value rules: utilization rounded to 2 decimals (float noise such as 0.29 × 100 = 28.999…), capped at 100 (contract range 0–100; past the limit = at the limit), negative / non-finite = unknown; a window whose `resets_at` is null or unparseable is unknown; the recorder's `rate_limit_event` conversion now uses the same `readingFromRateLimit` · why: never invent a number, keep the contract's range · how to revert: `normalizePct` / `windowAt` in src/core/usage.ts; the old inline conversion in `StreamRecorder.#onRateLimit`
- ASSUMED M9.2 · `usageResetsAt` (additive since M1.4) = the reset of the window behind `usagePct`; on a tie the later reset (binds longer) · how to revert: `usageState` in src/core/usage.ts
- ASSUMED M9.2 · the warning reaches the UI as an additive `usageWarnings: [{ window, pct, threshold, resetsAt, firedAt }]` on `/api/system` and the `system` event (present while the warning is in force), not as a new `/hub` event name (the contract's names stay locked); the toast host shows each one once per window + reset per browser (`localStorage` `switchboard.usageWarningsShown`, try/catch), toast only (no chime, no OS notification) · why: a warning fired while no page is open still shows when one opens; hub events are fire-and-forget · how to revert: `systemUsageFields` + `useUsageWarnings`

Nothing is paused automatically.`, only "Later"; M3.4 toast styles · why: built from the prototype's "Usage-limit warnings … Nothing is paused automatically." and Settings' "5-hour window and weekly limit"; no visual oracle possible (D10 n/a) · how to revert: `usageWarningToast` in src/web/toast/usage-warning.ts

## D15 · embedded tools through a local framing proxy (2026-09-28)
- ASSUMED D15-no-csp · an answer without any `Content-Security-Policy` gets one with only `frame-ancestors <Switchboard origins>` · dropping `X-Frame-Options` alone would let any site frame the tool through the proxy (clickjacking); D15's "replace (or add)" read as covering this · revert: the `if (!hasCsp)` line in `proxiedResponseHeaders` (src/server/tools/proxy.ts)
- ASSUMED D15-report-only · `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` is passed verbatim (only enforced CSP headers get the new `frame-ancestors`) · a report-only policy blocks nothing; "keep all other directives verbatim" · revert: treat `content-security-policy-report-only` like the enforced header in `proxiedResponseHeaders`
- ASSUMED D15-host-status · a foreign `Host` gets 403 `forbidden host` (text/plain), for HTTP and upgrades; a non-path request target 400 · mirrors the service's own guard (docs/security.md); the brief allowed 421 or 403 · revert: `refuse(res, 403, …)` / `refuseSocket(socket, 403, …)` → 421
- ASSUMED D15-origin · `Origin` and `Referer` are forwarded unchanged (they name the proxy's origin) · D15 lists the only exceptions; a tool that checks `Origin` (e.g. on WebSockets) would need a rewrite to its own origin · revert/extend: `upstreamRequestHeaders`
- ASSUMED D15-hop-by-hop · connection-level headers (`Connection`, `Keep-Alive`, `Transfer-Encoding`, `TE`, `Trailer`, `Upgrade` outside an upgrade, `Proxy-Connection`, the ones `Connection` names) are the proxy's own, both ways · HTTP requires it; "headers unchanged" read as end-to-end headers · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D15-host-name · `frameUrl` (and a rewritten `Location`) uses the host name the page / request used (`localhost` or `127.0.0.1`) · keeps the frame same-site with the page, so the tool's own cookies in the frame are first-party · revert: `pageHostname` in src/server/api/tools.ts and `proxyHostname` in proxy.ts → always 127.0.0.1
- ASSUMED D15-start · main.ts syncs the proxies before the service listens (a second instance that cannot bind exits right after); `PUT /api/tools` awaits the sync so its answer names live proxies; syncs are serialized · the first `GET /api/tools` already has every `frameUrl` · revert: move the sync after `listenLoopback`
- ASSUMED D15-probe · `POST /api/tools/{id}/probe` adds `framing: "refused"` only when the tool is up, refuses the requesting page's origin (CSP3 `frame-ancestors` matching reduced to http loopback origins, else HTML's `X-Frame-Options` rules) and no proxy runs for it; `ToolProbeProvider.probe` now returns `{ state, framing }` (the demo: `{ down, null }`) · one GET, and existing `{ state }` answers stay exact · revert: the probe route + `ToolProbeReport`
- ASSUMED D15-overlay · the fallback overlay reads "{host} refuses to load in a frame" · "{name} runs but refuses to load in a frame (X-Frame-Options / frame-ancestors), use New tab." · **↗ New tab** (a link to the tool's own URL, `target=_blank rel=noopener`), no iframe, no Retry (↻ Reload in the toolbar probes again) · built from the prototype's sentence; the prototype has no frame for this state (D10 n/a) · revert: the `framingRefused` branch in ToolView
- ASSUMED D15-https · `https:` tool URLs are proxied with normal certificate checks (a self-signed certificate is a 502) · conservative · revert: `rejectUnauthorized: false` for loopback targets in `startToolProxy`
- ASSUMED D15-ports · every proxy (re)start takes a new OS-assigned port, so the tool's origin in the frame (and its `localStorage` there) changes per start; cookies do not (they ignore ports) · D15 says OS-assigned · revert: persist a port per tool (not done)
- ASSUMED D15-e2e-fallback · the E2E fallback check fulfils `GET /api/tools` (`frameUrl: null`) and the probe (`framing: "refused"`) in the browser · the service reports the refusal only without a proxy (demo mode, whose tools are down, or a proxy that failed to start); tests/server/api/tool-frames.test.ts drives that answer for real · revert: n/a

## D17 · footer meters: RAM in use, Session + Week usage bars (2026-09-28)
- ASSUMED D17-model-source · model-scoped windows come from the newest `get_usage` reading (not the newest reading of any source) while it is at most 10 min old (`MODEL_WINDOW_MAX_AGE_MS` = 2 × the poller interval); a failed newer `get_usage` makes them unknown · a `rate_limit_event` carries no model data, so "newest reading only" would hide the model row during every turn, i.e. exactly while it is in use; the age limit keeps an old number from passing as current (e.g. every live session busy in long turns) · revert: `#modelWindows` in src/server/usage/meter.ts, `MODEL_WINDOW_MAX_AGE_MS` in src/core/usage.ts
- ASSUMED D17-model-join · a model window = `rate_limits.model_scoped[]` joined by display name with `rate_limits.limits[]` entries of kind `weekly_scoped` (`model_scoped`'s utilization / resets_at win, `is_active` from `limits`, a model only in `limits` is kept, CLI order); "in use" = above 0 % or `is_active`; parsed from the stored `raw`, no new column or migration · the recorded payload carries both lists and D17 names both conditions · revert: `modelWindowsFromGetUsage` in src/core/usage.ts
- ASSUMED D17-windows-independent · Session and Week are listed independently (a known Week is shown while Session's window has reset without a newer reading, and the other way round); `usagePct` still needs both windows (M9.2 unchanged) · "unknown stays unknown" per window, never hiding a known one · revert: `usageWindows()` in src/core/usage.ts
- ASSUMED D17-rows · the footer always shows Session and Week (`unknown` when the server lists no such window, `—` before `/api/system` answers) and one row per listed model window, labelled with the model's display name (`Fable`); the value is `62% · 1h48` (rounded %, `formatResetsIn`); the usage bars keep the prototype Max bar's bright fill (`--text`) · D17 "two rows", "a third row only while in use", the existing reset format · revert: `usageRows` in src/web/shell/format.ts, Sidebar.tsx
- ASSUMED D17-footer-columns · the usage rows share their own subgrid columns (`.sb-usage`: label ≥ 34 px and value ≥ 76 px, as wide as the widest) because "Session" (46 px in Geist Mono 11 px) does not fit the prototype's 34 px label column; CPU / RAM keep the prototype's columns, so the usage bars start 12 px further right and end at the same x · keeps CPU / RAM identical to the prototype · revert: `.sb-usage` in src/web/shell/shell.css (e.g. one wider label column for every row, which would move the CPU / RAM bars)
- ASSUMED D17-warnings · a model window at the threshold warns once until its reset: `UsageWarning.window: 'model'` + `model` (additive), stored under `usage.warned.model[<name>]`, toast title `Max usage 93%`, sub `Fable weekly limit`, text `Your Max Fable weekly limit reached …`, toast key `model:<name>@<reset>`; the 5-hour / weekly keys and copy are unchanged · additive, old stored state still reads · revert: `dueWarnings` / `readWarnedState` / `usageWindowLabel` in src/core/usage.ts, src/web/toast/usage-warning.ts
- ASSUMED D17-demo · demo mode: the prototype's Max figure (`62% · 1h48`) stays `usagePct` / `usageResetsAt` and becomes the Session window; the Week window only from an optional `footer.week` in system.json, which the prototype data does not have, so the demo's Week row reads "unknown"; no model row · "no invented numbers"; D17's "week from the demo data if present, else omit it" · revert: src/server/demo/providers.ts, `DemoSystem` in src/server/demo/data.ts
- ASSUMED D17-ram-cadence · the memory read starts when the probe is created and then at most every 2 s from a `system()` call (`MEMORY_READ_INTERVAL_MS`; the answer is the last finished read, i.e. the one after the previous 5 s tick), `/usr/bin/vm_stat` by absolute path with a 5 s timeout; a failed read falls back to `total − free` until a later read succeeds, nothing is logged; the value is capped at `ramTotal` · "read asynchronously and cached between ticks", never blocking a request · revert: `SystemProbe.refreshMemory` in src/server/system/probe.ts, src/server/system/memory.ts

- ASSUMED D17-visual · shell.spec / full-pass.spec: the footer keeps its bottom edge and grows upward by the Week row, so the rows above the usage rows (and Settings in the full pass) are compared with y relative to the footer's top, the footer by x / width / bottom; the Session / Week rows are listed next to the prototype's Max row and gated only on footer rules (labels, text styles = RAM row, the Max bar's height / radius / colors, 7 px rhythm, right edges = RAM row, footer growth = the added rows; tests/e2e/visual/usage-rows.ts) · the prototype has one Max row · revert: the two specs + usage-rows.ts

## D23 · Week bar pace (2026-09-28)
- ASSUMED D23-precision · the allowance is `day × 100 / 7` rounded to 2 decimals (14.29 … 57.14, 71.43 … 100), the precision of the utilization (`normalizePct`), and on pace = `pct < allowance` against that rounded value, so a Week at exactly the allowance the tooltip shows (57.14 %) is yellow · the tooltip never reads "On pace: 57.14% of 57.14%"; the difference from the exact fraction is < 0.005 points · revert: compare against the unrounded fraction in `weeklyPace` (src/core/usage.ts)
- ASSUMED D23-range · a Week reset more than 7 days ahead of now gives no pace (unknown), like a past reset · now would be outside the window the reset closes, so any day number would be a guess · revert: clamp to day 1 in `weeklyPace`
- ASSUMED D23-clock · computed in the browser from `usageWindows` (`key: 'week'`) and the page's clock, no API field; the step shows within the sidebar's 30 s re-render (or the next `system` event) · D23 allows either; no contract change · revert: n/a (a server field would be additive)
- ASSUMED D23-marker · the marker is 2 px wide in `--muted-3`, centered on the allowance, inside the 4 px track (no change to the row's height or the D17 rhythm); on day 7 (100 %) half of it shows at the bar's end · `--muted-3` stays visible both on the dark track (green case: the marker is ahead of the fill) and on the yellow fill (ahead case) · revert: `.sb-meter-marker` in src/web/shell/shell.css, `MeterRow` in src/web/shell/Sidebar.tsx
- ASSUMED D23-tooltip · the row's `title` shows the utilization and the allowance with up to 2 decimals, trailing zeros dropped (`33%`, `18.4%`, `57.14%`, `100%`), while the row's value keeps the rounded `18%`; "until" = the next step (the end of the current day, on day 7 the reset) as the local English short weekday + 24 h `HH:MM` (seconds dropped) · the D23 examples read `33%` / `57.14%`, and whole-number rounding could read "Ahead of pace: 57% of 57.14%" · revert: `weekPaceView` in src/web/shell/format.ts
- ASSUMED D23-look · colors through `data-pace="on|ahead"` on the row + shell.css (status done / status need on the fill); without a pace the Week row keeps the D17 look (`--text` fill, no marker, no title), so the demo views and the visual gates are untouched · the footer already colors by data attribute (`.sb-footer-dot[data-state]`) · revert: the D23 block in src/web/shell/shell.css
- ASSUMED D23-e2e · tests/e2e/week-pace.spec.ts seeds one get_usage reading (Week 62 %, reset about 74 h ahead) and moves only the page's clock (`page.clock.setFixedTime`, timers running) with `timezoneId: 'UTC'`; the server keeps its real clock, so it lists the window throughout · the pace is a browser computation, and the server's validity check needs a real future reset · revert: the spec

- ASSUMED D19-turn-start · a turn starts at `system/init` or at the replay of the stdin message, whichever comes first (a stdin write alone does not start it); ticks and lines while no turn runs are ignored · M0: `init` opens every turn, the replay is the delivery ack · revert: the `startTurn` calls in src/server/supervisor/recorder.ts
- ASSUMED D19-tokens · `thinkingTokens` = the sum of `estimated_tokens_delta` over the turn (`estimated_tokens` restarts with each model message), main agent only (the ticks carry no `parent_tool_use_id` on CLI 2.1.283); shown as `↓ 1.2k tokens`, rounded down · "estimated tokens so far this turn" · revert: `ActivityTracker.thinkingTokens` in src/core/derive/activity.ts, `formatTokens` in src/web/activity/activity.ts
- ASSUMED D19-states · after a `tool_result` the agent is `thinking` again until its next block (the model continues with the result); a thinking block is `thinking`; with several tools open the newest shows, then the older one again with its own start · like Claude Code's spinner between steps · revert: `toolEnded` / `#agentActivity` in src/core/derive/activity.ts
- ASSUMED D19-background · the turn's `result` ends the activity even while a background subagent still runs; its card then shows its status text until the CLI starts a turn of its own, and its lines outside a turn are ignored · the brief: "back to idle (null) on the turn's result" · revert: `endTurn` in `#onResult` and the `running` checks in ActivityTracker
- ASSUMED D19-clocks · every view times `thinking` / `writing` from the agent's start in the turn (the turn's start for the main agent: `12s`, `1m 23s`) and `tool` / `waiting` from their own start (`0:42`); the sidebar and the agent cards say `Thinking…` / `Writing…`, the rotating verb is the chat line's only · one rule everywhere; several rotating verbs at once would be noise · revert: `activityLabel` / `sessionActivityLabel` in src/web/activity/activity.ts
- ASSUMED D19-glyphs · the chat line's glyph: the stepping spinner `·✢✳✶✻✽` (running blue) while thinking / writing, a blinking `●` for a running tool (the line reads literally `● Bash: npm test  0:42`), a still amber `⏸` while waiting; tokens only while thinking · D19's literal tool line; `⏸` is the chat's own waiting mark · revert: `chatActivityLine`, src/web/activity/activity.css
- ASSUMED D19-sidebar · the row keeps its D14 folder tag and replaces only the mode text; the dot pulses while the session has an activity other than `waiting` (so the demo's `run` rows, which have none, stay still) · the folder stays visible; "idle rows are unchanged" · revert: `SessionActivityOr` / `data-activity` in src/web/shell/Sidebar.tsx
- ASSUMED D19-summaries · only the tools D19 names get a summary (MultiEdit, NotebookEdit, … show the tool name); a summary equal to the tool name shows the name alone; at most 80 characters, and the agent card's slot cuts the action at 160 px (the time always shows) · D19's literal list; the 380 px panel · revert: `toolSummary` in src/core/derive/activity.ts, `toolText`, activity.css
- ASSUMED D19-verbs · Switchboard's own 20 verbs: D19's three examples plus operator-themed ones ("Patching through…", "Plugging in…", "Routing…", …), 4 s each, the first picked from the turn's start time (the same verb in every tab) · deterministic for tests · revert: `THINKING_VERBS` / `thinkingVerb` in src/web/activity/activity.ts

- ASSUMED D24-reattach-failure-id · a failed reattach after a new process clears only `remote_enabled` (and records the CLI text in the chat, publishes); the stored id stays, so a later manual "on" tries it once more (then D24-reattach-manual) · the brief's words ("clear remoteEnabled") · revert: `#reattachFailed` in remote.ts

## D27 · Readable reported table (2026-09-28)
- SUPERSEDED D21-look-printed (by D27, docs/decisions.md, and the developer's D27 addition relayed by the coordinator: "we shouldn't scroll horizontally when it comes to the right panel") · was: the reported table shown as printed in the panel, a box table as the chat's code block scrolling sideways inside itself, a pipe table through `ChatMarkdown` · now: drawn as a table like the derived one; the original only behind the "as printed" popover over the main area (a table that cannot be parsed shows in the panel wrapped, plus the popover)
- ASSUMED D27-parse-place · the parser (`parseStatusTable`, `reportedStatus`, `plainCellText`) lives in src/core/derive/status-table.ts next to the detection and runs in the UI (`reportedTableView`); the service still sends `SessionDetail.reportedTable` as printed, so the contract is unchanged · the brief's "pure parser in core" + "draw the parsed table" · revert: parse on the server and add rows to `ReportedTable` (additive)
- ASSUMED D27-box-shape · a box table's `│` lines are split on their `│` characters (never on positions: emoji and CJK are wide), cells trimmed; the `┌` / `├` / `┼` / `└` lines split blocks; the first block is the header (a two-line header is joined); with separator rows each further block is one row (each column's non-blank lines joined with one space); when the body is one block every line is a row, except a line whose **first cell or Status cell is blank**, which continues the row before it (a wrapped cell in a table without separators; a row printed over lines whose Agent and Status both wrap would split — rare); a table with no rule under its header takes its first line as the header; box cells stay literal (no Markdown reduction: they are code) · the brief's rules plus the one-block case it leaves open · revert: `parseBoxTable` / `rowsByLine` in src/core/derive/status-table.ts
- ASSUMED D27-null · `null` (shown as printed) whenever any line's cell count differs from the header's, for GFM too (a GFM renderer would pad or drop cells), and when a box line does not start and end with `│` (a table cut short by the 4000-character payload limit falls back whole rather than losing its last row silently) · the brief: "null when the rows don't have a consistent column count" · revert: `parseBoxTable` / `parsePipeTable` (e.g. drop an incomplete last line)
- ASSUMED D27-gfm-inline · besides the brief's `**x**`, `*x*`, `` `x` `` and `[text](url)`: `__x__`, `_x_` (only at word boundaries, so `snake_case` stays), `~~x~~`, `![alt](url)` → alt, `<url>` → url, backslash-escaped punctuation → the character; a code span's content verbatim (a single surrounding space pair dropped); raw HTML stays text (D20) · plain text of what D20 renders · revert: `plainMarkdownText` / `plainCellText`
- ASSUMED D27-status-order · glyphs before words, as D27 says; among glyphs, the **first one in the cell** (anywhere, by position) decides; among words, the **first whole word in the cell** decides (case-insensitive; `in progress` also with a hyphen or several spaces), so `done · merge blocked` is done and `blocked, then done` is need; the glyph list is exactly D27's (`✔`, `✖`, `✗`, `🟠`, `🔵` are not status glyphs, their words decide); `needs` matches, `need` alone does not; `failed` matches, `failing` does not · the brief asks for the order to be recorded · revert: `STATUS_GLYPHS` / `STATUS_WORDS` / `reportedStatus` · **developer ruling 2026-09-28:** also `✔` done, `✖` `✗` failed, `🔵` running, `🟠` waiting
- ASSUMED D27-strip · only the cell's **leading** status glyphs (and an emoji presentation selector after them) are removed from the text; a trailing one (`merged ✅`) and any other emoji (`🔵 planning`) stay, since removing an emoji Switchboard does not map would drop information · D27 "any leading status emoji or glyph" · revert: `LEADING_GLYPHS`
- ASSUMED D27-glyph-words · a cell that is only a status glyph reads the derived table's word for its key: `running` (🟢), `done` (✅ ✓), `waiting` (🟡 ⏳ ⏸), `failed` (❌ ✕ 🔴) · the brief's example `🟢` → `running`; the derived table's `● running` / `✓ done` / `⏸ waiting` / `✕ failed` · revert: `STATUS_GLYPH_WORDS`
- ASSUMED D27-look · the table reuses the derived table's classes (`sb-overview-table`, `sb-overview-agent` for the column named `Agent`, `sb-overview-desc` (muted) for every other column, `sb-overview-status` for `Status`); the Status dot is the agent card's 7 px circle in `currentColor` with a 6 px gap; every cell's tooltip is its shown text (a Status cell's without its glyph); header cells have their text as tooltip too · "reuse its CSS classes and tokens" · revert: src/web/views/session/AgentOverview.tsx, agent-overview.css
- ASSUMED D27-widths · fixed shares by header: Description 2, Status 1.5 (its dot and a word such as `running` or `planning` stay readable at the panel's width; at 20 % `running` was cut), any other column 1 → 18.18 / 36.36 / 18.18 / 27.27 % for the usual four columns; no content-based widths · "share the width sensibly, Description the most (record it)" · revert: `REPORTED_COLUMN_SHARES`
- ASSUMED D27-toggle · "as printed" is a text button on the heading line's right in the heading's mono 11px `--muted-2` (`--text-2` on hover and while open, `aria-expanded`); its label stays "as printed" open or closed; it is shown for parsed and unparsed tables alike; its state is kept per session view (reset when another session opens) and stays open when a newer table arrives (the popover follows it) · "a small text button in the section's muted style" · revert: `ReportedTableView`
- ASSUMED D27-popover · developer addition (relayed by the coordinator): nothing in the right panel scrolls or overflows sideways. The original therefore opens in a **popover** (the coordinator's preference, since wrapping ruins box tables), not inline: `role="dialog"`, rendered into `document.body` (so it can never widen the panel), `position: fixed`, 8 px left of the panel, as wide as the printed table up to 16 px from the window's left edge (it may cover the sidebar: at 1440 px a 128-column orchestrator table then fits whole, while the main area alone is ~800 px), top at the reported section's top moved up to stay inside the window, at most the window's height less 16 px each side, a code block wider than that scrolling sideways inside the popover; the Remote popover's look (bg-card, border-card, 10 px radius, shadow, z-index 20, uppercase label `As printed by the agent`, ✕); closed by the button, ✕, Esc or a mousedown outside it · revert: `PrintedPopover` / `printedPopoverPlace` (e.g. bound it to the main area) · **developer confirmed 2026-09-28** (may cover the sidebar)
- ASSUMED D27-fallback · a table that cannot be parsed shows as printed **in the panel, wrapped** to its width (box: `white-space: pre-wrap`, `overflow-wrap: anywhere`; pipe: the Markdown table at 100 % width, fixed layout, cells wrapping), plus the same "as printed" popover for the unwrapped original; a wrapped box table is complete but hard to read · D27 "falls back to the printed text" under the no-sideways-scroll rule (the other options: clip it, which hides the Status column that is usually last, or show only the button) · revert: `.sb-overview-printed-wrapped` in agent-overview.css / `ReportedTableView` · **SUPERSEDED by the developer ruling 2026-09-28:** the panel shows a one-line note (`overview-unreadable`); the original is only in the "as printed" popover

## D28 · Frame helper (2026-09-28)
- BLOCKED D28-safari-headers · Safari's extensions cannot remove response headers today: MDN's compatibility data lists `declarativeNetRequest` `RuleAction.responseHeaders` as unsupported in Safari, and WebKit main (checked 2026-09-28) applies `modify-headers` rules to requests only (`applyResultsToRequest` → `ModifyHeadersAction::applyToRequest`; the parsed `response-headers` are never used). So Jira cannot open in Safari's tool frame through this extension; Switchboard detects it at run time and shows "{host} can't open in a frame in this browser" + Open in new tab (ASSUMED D28-capability-check). Chrome works (E2E). Unverified in Safari itself (no automated Safari run; the build could not run here, next line) · a platform limit, no workaround inside a WebExtension · revert: n/a; options for the developer: Chrome for Jira in the frame, Safari with New tab, or wait for WebKit (the Safari build lights up without changes)
- BLOCKED D28-safari-build · `npm run frame-helper:safari` could not run the converter here: `xcodebuild -checkFirstLaunchStatus` exits 69 and `xcrun safari-web-extension-converter` prints "A required plugin failed to load. Please ensure system content is up-to-date — try running 'xcodebuild -runFirstLaunch'." and exits 139 (also on a minimal manifest). Installing the first-launch components needs an admin password, which the brief rules out; the script checks this first and prints the fix. xcodebuild was therefore never reached · developer: open Xcode once (or `sudo xcodebuild -runFirstLaunch`), then `npm run frame-helper:safari` · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D28-site-rule · a "site" is a non-loopback `https:` URL; loopback = `localhost` + subdomains, `127.0.0.0/8`, `0.0.0.0`, `::1`, IPv4-mapped loopback; `http:` on another host stays a local tool (D15 proxy) · the brief's wording · revert: `isSiteToolUrl` in src/core/site-tools.ts
- ASSUMED D28-no-proxy · the service starts no D15 proxy for a site (`frameUrl: null`; a proxied tool whose URL becomes a site loses its proxy); the probe is unchanged and its `framing: "refused"` is ignored for sites in the UI · the proxy's loopback origin can never carry the site's cookies · revert: the `isSiteToolUrl` filter in `ToolProxies.#apply` (src/server/tools/proxies.ts)
- ASSUMED D28-capability-check · the marker alone is not trusted: with it, the Tool view frames `GET /api/frame-helper/check` (additive route; `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`) once per page, hidden, and frames the site only when that page shows (`ready`), else shows `blocked` ("{host} can't open in a frame in this browser", the reason, docs, Open in new tab) · with Safari's limit (BLOCKED D28-safari-headers) a marker-only rule would give Safari a blank frame; it also catches a Chrome helper without site access · revert: make `watchFrameHelper` report `ready` on the marker, drop the route (src/server/api/tools.ts) and the `blocked` overlay
- ASSUMED D28-marker · the marker is an attribute on `<html>` (`data-sb-frame-helper="<manifest version>"`) set at `document_start` (a MutationObserver sets it once `<html>` exists if the engine runs earlier); the page waits 1.5 s for it (`MARKER_WAIT_MS`) and keeps watching afterwards; the check frame may take 4 s (`CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`) · the brief allows attribute or message; an attribute survives React's rendering and needs no listener timing · revert: src/web/tools/frame-helper.ts
- ASSUMED D28-checking-blank · while the helper is being checked the frame area stays empty (no overlay, no frame), i.e. up to ~1.5 s before "needs the frame helper" · no flash of the wrong state · revert: `siteOverlay` in ToolView.tsx
- ASSUMED D28-overlay-copy · the button reads "Open in new tab" (no ↗), the docs path is plain text (Switchboard serves no docs), `{host}` = the URL's host with its port (not `urlHost`, which keeps the path); the `blocked` copy is mine; `down` still comes first · the brief's copy · revert: `siteOverlay` in ToolView.tsx
- ASSUMED D28-sandbox · a site's frame gets `allow-storage-access-by-user-activation` on top of the audit's sandbox; local tools keep theirs unchanged · the Storage Access API only works in a sandboxed frame with that token · revert: `SITE_FRAME_SANDBOX` in ToolView.tsx
- ASSUMED D28-host-permissions · `https://*/*`, `http://127.0.0.1/*`, `http://localhost/*` instead of `<all_urls>`; only `declarativeNetRequestWithHostAccess` (no `declarativeNetRequest`: Chrome loads and applies the static rule with it alone, E2E) · the narrowest set: Chrome needs host access to both the framed URL (any https site) and the initiator for `modifyHeaders` · revert: manifest.json · 2026-09-28 (feature/frame-scope): kept for the tab-scoped rules, see ASSUMED D28-scope-permissions
- ASSUMED D28-min-chrome · `minimum_chrome_version: "101"` · an older Chrome would not know `initiatorDomains` and could apply the rule to every frame · revert: manifest.json · 2026-09-28 (feature/frame-scope): still 101, now for `requestDomains`
- ASSUMED D28-safari-domains · the Safari build ships the rule with `domains` (WebKit `if-domain`: the top page's host, any port) instead of `initiatorDomains` (WebKit turns it into a frame-URL pattern wanting `/` right after the host, so `127.0.0.1:4870` never matches), and drops `minimum_chrome_version`; Chrome keeps `initiatorDomains` · Chrome rejects a rule with both keys, and `domains` alone in Chrome is a deprecated alias whose silent removal would widen the rule · revert: `safariRules` / `safariManifest` in tools/frame-helper/build-safari.ts · superseded 2026-09-28 (feature/frame-scope): no static rule any more, `safariRules` removed (ASSUMED D28-scope-safari)
- ASSUMED D28-safari-build · output `.frame-helper-safari/` (gitignored, rebuilt from scratch): `extension/` (staged), `project/` (converter), `DerivedData/…/Debug/Switchboard Frame Helper.app`; bundle id `local.switchboard.framehelper`; Debug with `CODE_SIGN_STYLE=Manual CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY=- DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=` · "Sign to Run Locally", no Apple account · revert: build-safari.ts
- ASSUMED D28-banner · `storage-access.js` runs at `document_idle` in every `https:` frame and acts only when its direct parent is the top page and that is `http://127.0.0.1` / `http://localhost` (any port); closed shadow root, inline styles, a × to dismiss; the refusal text is the brief's; the page is not paused, so a site that sends itself to its login page by script before the click cannot be helped (moot while Safari cannot remove the headers) · the brief · revert: tools/frame-helper/storage-access.js
- ASSUMED D28-e2e-site · the E2E "site" is an https stub named `site.test` (and `outside.test` for the scope check) on 127.0.0.1: `--host-resolver-rules` maps the names, every other name is `~NOTFOUND`, certificate errors are ignored in that browser only, and the self-signed certificate is made with `node:crypto` (tests/helpers/self-signed.ts: no openssl, no key in the repo); the page's probes are answered in the browser, so the service never looks up `site.test` · no test-only override in the app, and nothing leaves the machine · revert: tests/e2e/frame-helper.spec.ts, tests/helpers/site-stub.ts
- QUESTION D28-scope-sites · the rule works for frames started by **any** loopback page (any port; rules cannot name a port), and the framed document loses its whole CSP, not just `frame-ancestors` (`docs/frame-helper.md` → *Security scope*). Keep it, or narrow it to the sites you use (`requestDomains`, e.g. `*.atlassian.net`, fed by Switchboard as dynamic rules)? · the brief's scope, implemented as written · revert: n/a · ruled 2026-09-28 (D28 developer rulings): narrowed to the saved site tools' hosts in Switchboard's tab; built on feature/frame-scope (ASSUMED D28-scope-* below)
- QUESTION D28-in-frame-navigation · a full-page navigation that the site itself starts inside the frame (e.g. Jira → another Atlassian product, or Jira's own redirect to its login) has the site as initiator, so the rule does not apply and the next page may refuse the frame (↻ Reload returns to the tool URL). Covering it would need tab-scoped session rules (Chrome only: Safari has no `tabIds`) · recorded, not built · revert: n/a · 2026-09-28 (feature/frame-scope): the tab-scoped rule has no initiator condition, so such navigations stay framed while they stay on saved hosts; to any other host (e.g. `id.atlassian.com`) they are still refused
- ASSUMED D28-check-host · the capability check keeps working because the page puts its **own host** (`location.hostname`: `127.0.0.1` or `localhost`) first in the host list, not through a separate check-only rule · the simplest correct option: one message, one rule per tab, and the check page is on that host; side effect: in Switchboard's tab every frame from that host (the local tools' D15 proxies included) loses its headers too, as with the first build, and they load either way · revert: drop the page host in `frameHelperHosts` (src/core/site-tools.ts) and add a check-only rule (a `urlFilter` on the check path of the sender's origin) in tools/frame-helper/background.js
- ASSUMED D28-scope-protocol · page → its own window (targetOrigin = its origin): `{ source: 'switchboard', type: 'frame-helper:sites', id, hosts }`; content script → worker `{ type: 'frame-helper:sites', hosts }`; answer back to the page `{ source: 'switchboard-frame-helper', type: 'frame-helper:sites-applied', id, ok, hosts, error }`; only the answer to the latest `id` counts; the page waits 2 s for it (`SITES_TIMEOUT_MS`); the capability check waits for it; a site frames only once its host is confirmed (`checking` while the list waits, `blocked` when the helper answered without it) · the brief's message plus an answer, so no frame loads before its rule exists · revert: src/web/tools/frame-helper.ts, useFrameHelper.ts, tools/frame-helper/marker.js
- ASSUMED D28-scope-hosts · a plain host name = lower-case LDH labels, two or more (punycode for IDN), a dotted IPv4 address, or `localhost`; no `*`, ports, paths, schemes, IPv6 brackets, trailing dot or single label (a bare `net` would match every `.net` site through `requestDomains`' subdomain matching); the worker refuses the whole list on any bad host or more than 50 and then leaves the tab with no rule; the page leaves such hosts out first and stops at 50 (its own host + 49 sites), so such a site tool shows `blocked` · the brief ("plain hostnames, at most 50") · revert: `HOST` / `MAX_HOSTS` in background.js and `isFrameHelperHost` / `MAX_FRAME_HELPER_HOSTS` in src/core/site-tools.ts (a unit test keeps the two in step)
- ASSUMED D28-scope-rule · one session rule per tab, `id` = the tab's id, `priority: 1`, condition exactly `{ tabIds: [tab], resourceTypes: ['sub_frame'], requestDomains: hosts }` (no initiator condition, the brief's shape); each list replaces it, an empty one removes it, a failed `updateSessionRules` leaves no rule and answers `ok: false` with the browser's text; updates run one at a time in order · stateless across service-worker restarts (nothing kept in memory) · revert: tools/frame-helper/background.js
- ASSUMED D28-scope-clear · a tab's rule goes when the tab closes (`tabs.onRemoved`), when `tabs.onUpdated` reports a navigation (a `url` change or `status: loading`) to anything but an `http:` page on `127.0.0.1` / `localhost` (a URL the extension may not see counts as not loopback), and when a new loopback document starts in it (`frame-helper:reset` from marker.js at document_start; Switchboard asks again after each load) · "only in the tab that runs Switchboard": another local page loaded into Switchboard's tab must not inherit its hosts; SPA navigation inside Switchboard keeps the rule · revert: the reset message in marker.js / background.js
- ASSUMED D28-scope-senders · the worker takes a list only from its own content script (`sender.id`) in frame 0 (`sender.frameId === 0`) of an `http://127.0.0.1` / `http://localhost` page (`sender.url`, any port); the content script relays only messages from the page's own window and origin, and talks to the worker only when `window.top === window` on such a page; any other loopback page can still name hosts for **its own tab** (nothing proves a loopback page is Switchboard; declarative rules cannot name a port) · documented in docs/frame-helper.md → *Security scope* · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D28-scope-permissions · unchanged: `declarativeNetRequestWithHostAccess` and `https://*/*`, `http://127.0.0.1/*`, `http://localhost/*`, plus `background.service_worker`; no `tabs` (tab events work without it and loopback URLs are visible through the host permission), `storage`, `webNavigation` or `scripting` · `https://*/*` stays: site hosts are only known at run time, and `modifyHeaders` needs host access to the framed URL and its initiator · revert: manifest.json
- ASSUMED D28-scope-version · extension version 2.0.0 (the marker's value); a helper that never answers a host list (1.0.0, not reloaded yet) is trusted on its capability check alone, so it keeps working the old way until the developer reloads it · revert: manifest.json, `siteFrameStatus` in src/web/tools/frame-helper.ts
- ASSUMED D28-scope-senders-of-list · both the sidebar (its tools list, reloaded on `useToolsChanged`) and the Tool view (its own `GET /api/tools`, fresher when another tab added a tool) send the list; an unchanged list is not sent again; sent in a layout effect (no paint of a stale state); sent again after a back/forward-cache restore and when the marker shows up after an unanswered list · the brief's "tools hook / useToolsChanged" plus the Tool view so a tool added elsewhere is not stuck · revert: the `useFrameHelperSites` calls in Sidebar.tsx / ToolView.tsx
- ASSUMED D28-scope-safari · the Safari build copies background.js and keeps `background.service_worker`; `safariRules` (the `domains` form) is gone with rules.json; whether Safari takes `tabIds` / `requestDomains` session rules is unverified (no Safari run; the converter still cannot run on this Mac, BLOCKED D28-safari-build); its "can't open in a frame" state is unchanged (a failed worker update answers `ok: false`, and the check decides) · Safari cannot apply response-header rules anyway (D28 ruling) · revert: tools/frame-helper/build-safari.ts

- ASSUMED D31-default-value · the CLI's `default` entry is stored as NULL (no `--model`); going back to it sends `set_model "default"` (probed: accepted); an effort of `null` is stored as NULL (no `--effort`) and sends `apply_flag_settings {effortLevel: null}` · "Sessions without a choice use the CLI's defaults, exactly as today" · revert: `normalizeModel` (src/core/model-choice.ts), `#setModelNow`
- ASSUMED D31-check-unknown · with no reported list the route takes any model name (letters / digits first, then `. _ : - [ ] / @`, ≤ 100 characters: never a leading `-` that argv would read as a flag) and the CLI decides (a live process refuses it verbatim; otherwise the next spawn may fail with the CLI's text); an effort must then be one of `claude --help`'s `low, medium, high, xhigh, max` · the CLI does not check an effort itself (probed: `bogus` is a silent success) · revert: `checkModelChoice`
- ASSUMED D31-effort-with-model · the resulting pair is checked: a body that changes only the model is 422 on `effort` when the stored effort is not one of the new model's levels; the UI's model pick always sends both (the effort kept when the new model has it, else `null`) · explicit, no silent server-side reset · revert: in `#setModelNow`, clear an unsupported stored effort when `effort` is absent
- ASSUMED D31-partial-failure · model and effort are two requests, `set_model` first: when it is refused nothing more is sent and nothing is stored; when the effort is refused after the model was taken, the model **is** stored (the process runs on it, with its own step line) and the effort is not; 502 either way · D31's "the stored choice is left unchanged" cannot hold for a model the process already switched to · revert: send `set_model` with the old value to roll back instead of storing
- ASSUMED D31-step-not-live · a change to a session without a live process (paused, ended, detached) records the chat step line too (payload `live: false`) and is stored for the next spawn; the brief says "when not live, it only stores" · D31: a change "shows as a chat step line", and the chat would otherwise not show when the model changed · revert: skip `#recordModel` when there is no live process
- ASSUMED D31-failure-lines · a refusal is also an `error` step line (`Could not change the model: <CLI text>` / `Could not change the effort: …`) besides the 502 text the popover shows · like D24-events · revert: the failure `#recordModel` calls in supervisor.ts
- ASSUMED D31-timeouts · each reply may take 30 s (the CLI may check the model with its server first; the probe saw them at once); no reply = 502 "claude did not answer the set_model request within 30 s"; a process that ends or starts stopping while a request waits makes the change a stored-only one (success, applied at the next spawn) · revert: `MODEL_CONTROL_TIMEOUT_MS`, `#modelRequest`

- ASSUMED D31-live · "live" = a live process that is not being stopped and whose stdin is open; a detached session only stores (the terminal owns its process; the next attach passes the flags) · revert: `#applying`
- ASSUMED D31-ui-one-trigger · one header action `Opus 5.5 · high ▾` (the brief's example) opening a popover that holds both pickers (the model rows, then the effort pills), rather than two triggers; it is the first header action; the popover stays open after a pick so the effort can be picked next; a refusal's text shows in the popover · revert: ModelPicker.tsx, session.css (*D31*)
