## D33 · Close sessions (2026-09-28)
- ASSUMED D33-order · the close stops the process first (Pause's sequence) and stores `closed_at` only once it has exited, as the brief orders; a service crash during that stop leaves the session paused and open (recovery finishes the pause) and the developer closes it again · storing `closed_at` first would hide a session whose process may still run · revert: `#closeNow` in src/server/supervisor/supervisor.ts (store `closedAt` before `#stop`)
- ASSUMED D33-questions-label · batches that still wait (open, or stale and unanswered) close through a new nullable `question_batches.closed_reason` = `session closed` (0010; state `stale`, the stale path), not D24's `answered_on` (that would mark them `answered` and read "Answered on session closed"); they leave the Inbox and `openQuestionCount`, stay closed after Reopen, answering them is 409 `not-open`, and the chat shows "Closed · session closed"; open permission requests only go stale (their existing Inbox exit; no label column) · the brief: reuse the stale/withdrawn path and label it · revert: 0010's second ALTER, `closeUnanswered`, `isBatchWaiting` / `openQuestionCount`, `Question.closedReason`, the chat bubble
- ASSUMED D33-refuse-while-closed · a closed session (or one being closed) refuses messages, Resume and Attach with 409 `closed` ("the session <title> is closed: reopen it from History first") instead of reopening itself; rename, Pause and Continue in terminal still answer; `GET /api/sessions/{id}` still answers · Reopen is the ruling's way back, and a message racing the close's stop must not spawn a new process · revert: the `#assertNotClosed` calls in supervisor.ts
- ASSUMED D33-live-idle-ui · the UI asks for confirmation only for a running or waiting session (status `run` / `need`, or a turn runs: `activity` set), as D33's UI wording says; a live process idle between turns is closed at once, posted with `confirm: true` (the API needs confirm for any live process, per the brief); a `close-needs-confirm` answer (it started running meanwhile) opens the confirmation · stopping an idle process loses nothing · revert: `closeAsks` in src/core/session-close.ts (add `live`)
- ASSUMED D33-after-close · after closing from the session view, and after closing the session on screen from the sidebar, the Inbox opens (not the next session); closing another session from the sidebar stays where it is · the brief's "Inbox (or the next session)": the Inbox is predictable · revert: the `onClosed` callbacks in SessionHeader.tsx / Sidebar.tsx
- ASSUMED D33-hover-x · the row's × (9 px drawn glyph, 17 px target, `--muted-2`, `--text` + `--bg-selected-card` under the pointer) sits at the row's top right over the age, which is hidden (`visibility`) while the × shows; it shows on hover and on keyboard focus (`:focus-visible` on the row or the ×), not after a mouse click that focuses the row; it is the row link's last child, absolutely placed · "at the row's right" without shifting the layout or the prototype's child paths; the two would overlap otherwise · revert: the D33 block in src/web/shell/shell.css, `SessionCloseButton`

- ASSUMED D33-list-query · `GET /api/sessions?closed=exclude|include` (default `exclude`; anything else 422 on `closed`); `include` is used by the New-session name check (a closed session keeps its short name) and the Inbox's folder-tag lookup; the folder switcher, loop cards, palette and notifications keep the default · the brief's "`?closed=include` (or similar)" · revert: `parseClosedFilter` + the two `{ closed: 'include' }` calls

## D34 · Installable app (2026-09-28)
- ASSUMED D34-theme-color · `theme_color` = `--bg-sidebar` #111214 (the shell's own background, so an app window's title bar joins the sidebar); `background_color` = `--bg-app` #0b0c0d (the page body, the icons' ground); the page's and the offline page's `theme-color` meta follow the manifest · "background and theme colors from the SPEC tokens" · revert: manifest.webmanifest, the two metas
- ASSUMED D34-icon-geometry · the `any` icon is a full-bleed `--bg-app` square with the brand mark at 320/512, in the sidebar's proportions (radius 6/22, the S at 12/22 on the 15/22 baseline, as Chromium lays out the 22 px mark: 0.41% pixel diff at 16×); the maskable one has the mark at 256/512 (farthest point ≈ 152 px from the center, inside the 204.8 px safe radius); the S is Geist SemiBold's own glyph outline, read once from @fontsource/geist's WOFF, so the SVGs need no font · revert: the SVGs + `npm run icons`
- ASSUMED D34-extra-icons · besides the brief's 192 / 512 / maskable-512 / 180 PNGs: `favicon.svg` (the bare 22 px mark: a dark square would vanish in a dark tab strip) and a 32 px favicon PNG; the manifest also has `id: "/"` and `lang: "en"`, and the head `mobile-web-app-capable` next to the Apple meta (Chrome warns about the Apple-only one) · revert: drop them
- ASSUMED D34-sw-plain-js · `sw.js` is plain JavaScript in Vite's public folder, type-checked through JSDoc by a fourth config `tsconfig.sw.json` (WebWorker lib, strict, in `npm run typecheck`), not a TypeScript entry bundled by Vite (a fixed `/sw.js` output would need a second build input) · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D34-navigation-preload · the worker answers navigations with the navigation-preload response (falling back to `fetch(event.request)`): probed in the test Chromium, a worker's own fetch of a typed URL reaches the service as `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Dest: empty`, the preload keeps the browser's `none` / `document`; both keep a cross-site link `cross-site`, so gap #20's cookie rule holds either way (E2E-checked) · revert: `navigate()` in sw.js
- ASSUMED D34-static-routing · where the browser has Chromium's Static Routing API, install adds `{ condition: { not: { requestMode: 'navigate' } }, source: 'network' }`, so API, `/hub` and assets never reach the worker; elsewhere the fetch listener returns without answering; a refused rule never fails the install · "every other request is not intercepted at all" · revert: `routeAroundWorker` in sw.js
- ASSUMED D34-skip-waiting · a new worker takes over at once (`skipWaiting` + `clients.claim`), safe because it serves nothing but the offline page; `updateViaCache: 'none'` on register · revert: sw.js install, service-worker.ts
- ASSUMED D34-offline-trigger · only a failed connection shows the offline page; any HTTP answer from the service (403, 404, 500) passes through untouched; without a cached page the browser's own error shows (`Response.error()`) · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D34-cache-pin · `tests/web/service-worker.test.ts` pins offline.html's SHA-256 to `CACHE_VERSION`, so a change to the page fails the test until the version is bumped (the brief's "bump a cache version constant") · revert: the pin
- ASSUMED D34-routes · "the guard's public list" is the route flag `config.public`, so the list is `APP_FILES` (`/manifest.webmanifest`, `/sw.js`, `/offline.html`: read and sent with fixed types and `no-cache`) + `APP_ICONS_PREFIX` (`/icons/*`, `sendFile`: `public, max-age=0`) in web.ts, registered ahead of the static wildcard (which already served any file publicly); an unbuilt UI answers 404 for them · revert: web.ts
- ASSUMED D34-row-place · Settings has no General section, so the row sits in the first one, Claude Code, right after Start at login (both are about how Switchboard runs): label "Install as app", description "Open Switchboard in its own window, from the Dock or the taskbar", an outlined **Install** Action (the Send test style); `docs/visual/install-row.md` checks it on its own against the prototype's row template · revert: sections.tsx, InstallApp.tsx
- ASSUMED D34-safari-hint-form · in Safari the hint is a one-line settings row (the same frame and divider) holding only "Install: File → Add to Dock…" in the description style: no label, no control · "a one-line hint instead" · revert: InstallApp.tsx
- ASSUMED D34-one-dialog · Install uses the offer up whatever the answer (Chrome allows one `prompt()` per event), so the row hides until Chrome offers again (a later load); `appinstalled` hides it too · revert: `createInstallPromptStore().install`
- ASSUMED D34-safari-ua · Safari = `Safari/` without `Chrome` (HeadlessChrome included), `Chromium`, `CriOS`, `Edg`, `OPR`, `FxiOS`, `Firefox`; so the test Chromium never shows the hint · revert: `isSafari`
- ASSUMED D34-standalone-emulation · the test Chromium does not emulate `display-mode` through CDP `Emulation.setEmulatedMedia` (probed) and `page.emulateMedia` has no such option, so the E2E stands in with an init script that makes `matchMedia('(display-mode: standalone)')` match; the unit test covers the rule · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D34-e2e-other-site · "a link from another site" in the E2E is a blank page in the same context (an opaque origin, so the link is cross-site) instead of a second stub server · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D34-frame-helper-spec · `helperWorker()` in tests/e2e/frame-helper.spec.ts now waits only for the extension's worker (a `predicate`): Switchboard's own `/sw.js` is a second service worker in that context and could win the bare `waitForEvent('serviceworker')` · test-only · revert: the predicate
- ASSUMED D34-restart-helper · `startServerOn(port, env)` in tests/helpers/server-process.ts (the wait shared with `startServer`) brings a stopped server back on the same port for the offline E2E · test-only · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D34-dev-mode · `npm run dev` (development build) registers no worker; one that a production build registered earlier on the same address stays (it passes every page load through) · "only in production builds" · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D34-no-readme · the repo has no README, so docs/install-app.md is linked from docs/settings.md, docs/security.md and docs/configuration.md · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D34-composer-fonts · the D26 composer measured its one-line height once, at the first render; under the service worker Chromium no longer reuses the previous page's fonts from its memory cache (a controlled page loads them from the disk cache, after the first render), so the height was taken with the fallback font (40 px instead of 41) and the D26 E2E failed every time. `Composer` now measures it whenever the field is empty and again when `document.fonts` finishes loading (also true on a cold cache without the worker) · the smallest fix that keeps D26's behavior · revert: the `fontLoads` state and the `field.value === ''` condition in ChatTab.tsx
- OPEN D34-origin (for the developer) · `127.0.0.1:4870` and `localhost:4870` are two origins, so two different installed apps (and two cookies); the docs say to install from the address normally used. Switchboard could redirect `localhost` page loads to `127.0.0.1` (the service address it reports) to make that one app · not done: a redirect changes the Host guard's behavior, which D34 keeps unchanged · **developer ruling 2026-09-28:** redirect page loads on localhost to 127.0.0.1 (built)

## D35 · Guided frame-helper setup (2026-09-28)
- VERIFIED D35-no-inject · Chrome does not add a manifest content script to a page that is already open when an unpacked extension is loaded: on Playwright's Chromium with CDP `Extensions.loadUnpacked` the open page never gets `data-sb-frame-helper`, a new tab and a reload do (`tests/e2e/frame-helper-setup.spec.ts`, last test) · so the panel cannot turn green "the moment Chrome loads it" (D35) without a reload of the Switchboard tab · see ASSUMED D35-reload-step, QUESTION D35-auto-green · **developer ruling 2026-09-28:** keep the Reload step (no new permission)
- ASSUMED D35-reload-step · the panel has a 4th step, "Reload this tab" (**Reload tab** → `location.reload()`, "Chrome adds the helper to pages opened after it loads."), after D35's three; the status turns green after it, and a site tool's view then frames the site by itself (the view already re-checks a late marker: `watchFrameHelper` keeps observing, unit-tested) · the D35 flow cannot work otherwise with helper 2.0.0 (VERIFIED D35-no-inject); `docs/frame-helper.md` → Install already had "Reload the Switchboard tab" as step 4 · revert: the `reload` step in src/web/tools/FrameHelperSetup.tsx
- QUESTION D35-auto-green (for the developer) · to turn green without the reload the helper itself would have to act on install: in `runtime.onInstalled`, inject marker.js into open loopback tabs (`chrome.scripting.executeScript`: a new `scripting` permission) or reload them (`chrome.tabs.reload`: no new permission, but it reloads every loopback tab, other dev servers included). Either means helper 2.1.0 and a wider extension; not built · no change made
- ASSUMED D35-status-source · the status comes from the marker alone (`useFrameHelperMarker`: read on mount, on every attribute change, every 2 s while the panel is open), not from `useFrameHelper` (which also runs the capability check), so "Frame helper 2.0.0 is on ✓" means the helper is installed and current, not that it can frame here (a Chrome helper without site access reads "on ✓" in Settings and "can't open in a frame" in the Tool view) · the brief: "live from the marker" · revert: `useFrameHelperSetup` in FrameHelperSetup.tsx
- ASSUMED D35-versions · versions compare as dotted numbers part by part (a missing part is 0, Chrome's form); a newer marker than the manifest is "on"; a marker that is not a version (`unknown`, marker.js without `getManifest`) reads "older"; until `GET /api/frame-helper` answered, any marker reads "on" · "at least the manifest's" · revert: `frameHelperSetupStatus` in src/web/tools/frame-helper-setup.ts
- ASSUMED D35-older-copy · the older state reads the brief's "An older frame helper (<v>) is on: press reload on it in chrome://extensions" (amber), not D35's shorter "Reload the frame helper in chrome://extensions"; on ✓ is green (`--status-done`), the rest muted · the brief is the later wording · revert: `setupStatusText`
- ASSUMED D35-safari · Safari is detected from the user agent (`Safari/` without a Chromium or Firefox token, `HeadlessChrome` included); in Safari the Settings row shows only the status line (no Set up: nothing to set up), and a site's page keeps **Set up frame helper**, whose panel shows the Safari line with no steps · "in Safari: …, with no steps" · revert: `isSafariUserAgent`, the `safari` branch in FrameHelperRow.tsx
- ASSUMED D35-labels · the reveal button reads "Reveal in Finder" (macOS), "Reveal in File Explorer" (Windows), "Open the folder" (elsewhere), by `navigator.platform` (the service is loopback only, so the browser is on the same machine); the paste hint is the brief's "In the file dialog press ⌘⇧G and paste" on macOS, "paste it into the Folder field" on Windows, "press Ctrl+L and paste" elsewhere; step 1's button reads "Open extensions" · "label by platform" · revert: `revealLabel` / `pasteHint` in frame-helper-setup.ts
- ASSUMED D35-explorer · Windows reveal is `explorer /select, <path>\manifest.json` as **two** arguments (Node quotes a path with spaces by itself; the single argument `"/select,C:\a b\…"` is known to open the wrong folder), and explorer's exit code is ignored (it exits 1 even when it opened the window); open-extensions falls back to `cmd /c start "" chrome chrome://extensions` (the empty title keeps `start` from taking `chrome` as the window title) · unverified on Windows (no Windows run here) · revert: `revealCommand` / `extensionsCommands` in src/server/tools/frame-helper.ts
- ASSUMED D35-launch · each opener is spawned detached (`shell: false`, stdin/stdout ignored, stderr read); it counts as started when it exits 0 or still runs after 2 s (a browser binary that was not running yet, Linux / Windows, stays as the browser and is left to run); on any failure the next command is tried; the 502 message joins every failure with "; " · none of `open` / `explorer` / `xdg-open` needs more, and a waiting browser must not be killed by a timeout · revert: `launch` / `createFrameHelperOpener`
- ASSUMED D35-open-command · `SWITCHBOARD_OPEN_COMMAND` is an argv prefix put **before** the opener's whole argv (its executable included), parsed like `SWITCHBOARD_GH_BIN`, so the fake records exactly what the real opener would get (`open -R …/manifest.json`); it is not carried into the installed service's environment; `tools/fake-opener` (`FAKE_OPENER_LOG`, `FAKE_OPENER_FAIL`, like fake-servicectl) is a default of every test server · "configurable through SWITCHBOARD_OPEN_COMMAND … a fake that records its argv" · revert: `openCommand` in src/server/config.ts, `testServerDefaults()`
- ASSUMED D35-provider · the openers are a provider (`providers.frameHelperOpener`), wired by main.ts in normal and demo runs; a test app built without one answers 501 `not-implemented` (`item: "D35"`) on both POSTs, as `/api/service` does for M9.1, so nothing opens a real app by accident; `GET /api/frame-helper` always answers (read-only) · revert: the provider in src/server/providers.ts / main.ts
- ASSUMED D35-statuses · the POSTs answer 204 once the opener started, 502 `{ error: "open-failed", message }` otherwise; they read no body or query (ignored if sent); the GET reads `manifest.json` on every request (async), so a pulled helper update shows at once · revert: src/server/api/frame-helper.ts
- ASSUMED D35-placement · the Frame helper row is the Embedded tools section's last part (after the tool cards and "Add a tool"), a settings row (label, description, status in the value style, **Set up** / **Close** in the small action style), and its panel opens under it in the tool cards' style, so the prototype's parts keep their paths; on a site's page **Set up frame helper** is the card's second action, outlined (SPEC: the first is primary), with its own class `sb-tool-overlay-outlined` (the D28 spec's `.sb-tool-overlay-button` still finds only Open in new tab), and the panel opens inside the card under a row line · "a Frame helper row … opens the panel inline"; "next to Open in new tab" · revert: FrameHelperRow.tsx, `OverlayCard` in ToolView.tsx

## D39 · Own answers (2026-09-29)
- VERIFIED D39-cli-other · Claude Code's own "Other" sends the typed text as the answer string: the 2.1.284 binary's bundled AskUserQuestion form (read-only `strings`, never run for D39) adds `{type:"input", value:"__other__", label:"Other", placeholder:"Type something."}` to every question; single-select → `answers[q] = <typed text>` (plus " (Image attached)" when an image was pasted); multi-select → the picked labels and the typed text joined by the CLI's multi-value helper (`", "`, an item containing `", "` or `"` written as a JSON string). A strictly checked host answer (`admitCardAnswer`) may only add `answers` (+ `annotations`, `response`, `followUp`) to the unchanged input, each value a string ≤ 8192 characters or, for multi-select only, an array of ≤ options + 1 strings · recorded in docs/questions.md → *Own answers (D39)* · Switchboard keeps sending one string per question
- ASSUMED D39-multiselect · a multi-select question takes one option **or** one own answer, never both: the contract (and the card) carry one pick per question since M3.1, and the brief's "allow `text` next to the picked indexes if the shape allows several" does not apply (it allows one), so both-given is 422 for every question; D39's "the typed text is one more picked item" would need several picks per question (see OPEN D39-multi-pick) · the most conservative reading of the locked brief · revert: allow `answerIndex` + `text` on multiSelect questions in `validateAnswers` and join them in `answersByText`
- ASSUMED D39-validation-order · the contract's 400s come first (no/garbled body, an unknown or repeated question, an `answerIndex` that is not an integer option index, a question without an entry), then 422 for every refused entry at once (both or neither of `answerIndex` / `text`, a `text` not 1–2000 characters once trimmed or not a string); `null` counts as not given, so an entry with neither (formerly 400 "answerIndex undefined is not an option") is now 422 · the brief: "both or neither given → 422", "else 422 on that question" · revert: `validateAnswers` in pipeline.ts
- ASSUMED D39-422-shape · the refusal is the API's usual 422 shape plus the question: `{ error: "invalid", message, errors: [{ questionId, field: "text" | "answer", message }] }`, `message` = the entries' messages joined with "; " (the card shows it as `Not sent: …`) · revert: `sendError` in src/server/api/inbox.ts
- ASSUMED D39-trim · the text is trimmed (inner line breaks kept) before the length check, and the trimmed text is what goes to the CLI, what is stored and what the bubble shows ("verbatim" = as typed minus surrounding whitespace); the length counts UTF-16 code units like D22's title rule; the field's `maxlength` is 2000 · revert: `checkOwnAnswer` in src/core/own-answer.ts
- ASSUMED D39-storage · no migration: an own answer is `answer_index` NULL + the text in `answer_label` (that column already holds what went into `answers`), `answered_at` set; the batch completes when every question has `answered_at` (was: every `answer_index` set); `ownAnswerOf` reads it back and `Question.answerText` exposes it · the brief: "a migration only if the answer is stored in a column that can't hold it" · revert: add `0011_answer_text.sql` with an `answer_text` column and read it instead
- ASSUMED D39-answertext-optional · `Question.answerText` is optional (`?: string | null`) in src/core/api.ts, like D24's `answeredOn` and D33's `closedReason`, so older fixtures type-check; the server always sends it · revert: make it required and update the fixtures
- ASSUMED D39-duplicate-join · two questions with the same text keep the existing `", "` join, own answers included, without the CLI's JSON quoting of items that contain `", "` or `"` · "keep that format" · revert: quote such items in `answersByText` like the CLI's helper
- ASSUMED D39-card-flow · Other… is one more option pill (the options row's last child, same classes and look); picking it opens a two-line textarea under the options, focused; Enter confirms only when the text counts and closes the field onto the answer (shown verbatim in the selected pill's colors; a click or Other… again reopens it with the text); Enter on an empty or blank field does nothing; Esc drops the pick and the text; both return focus to the Other… pill; an IME composition keeps its own keys; picking an option drops the typed text; a non-empty text counts at once, confirmed or not · the brief's "Enter confirms, Shift+Enter makes a new line; Esc cancels back to no pick" · revert: QuestionCard.tsx + question-card.ts
- ASSUMED D39-copy · the pill "Other…" (tooltip "Answer in your own words"), the field's placeholder "Your answer · Enter confirms · Shift+Enter adds a line · Esc cancels", its label "Your answer to “<question>”", the confirmed answer's tooltip "Edit your answer"; the field in the pill's type (12.5px, 1.5) and radius (7px) and paddings (6px 12px Inbox, 5px 11px chat), `--option-selected-border` on `--bg-sidebar`, `--question-label` border while focused, placeholder `--muted-3`; the confirmed answer on `--option-selected-bg` in `--text-strong` · no prototype frame, SPEC tokens only · revert: question-card.ts / question-card.css
- ASSUMED D39-bubble · every answers-bubble line gets `white-space: pre-wrap` and `overflow-wrap: anywhere` so an own answer's line breaks show and a long word breaks inside the bubble; option labels and the D24 / D33 lines render as before (the chat visual spec's bubble parts are unchanged) · revert: `.sb-chat-answers-bubble > div` in session.css
- ASSUMED D39-visual · the Inbox and Chat visual specs forgive exactly one difference, a question's (or its options row's) text ending in "Other…" (`sameCopyBesidesOther`), and gate the pill on its own (`tests/e2e/visual/own-answer.ts`: last child after the prototype's options, copy, on the options' line, one gap after the last option, the options' height, inside the row, an unpicked option's computed styles); `docs/visual/inbox.md`, `chat.md` and their side-by-side PNGs regenerated, `full-pass.md` not (its Inbox and Session · Chat rows compare view boxes and landmark copy, which the pill does not change; its two PNGs predate the pill) · revert: n/a
- OPEN D39-multi-pick (for the developer) · D39 says a multi-select question's typed text is "one more picked item", but Switchboard's card and contract take one pick per question (M3.1), so today it is an option or own words. Letting a multi-select question take several options plus own words needs an additive contract change (e.g. `answerIndexes: number[]` next to `text`), the card to toggle pills, and the answer string joined like the CLI (`", "`, quoting items with `", "` or `"`) · no change made

## D41 · Collapsible panes (2026-09-29)
- ASSUMED D41-api · the two states are editable keys of the existing `GET/PUT /api/settings`: `ui.sidebarHidden` and `ui.rightPanelHidden` (booleans, default `false`), validated like the other boolean keys; no new route (local-api.md → D41) · "use the existing settings route if it can carry them (additive)" · revert: `EDITABLE_SETTINGS` / `SETTING_DEFAULTS` / `KnownSettings` in src/core/settings.ts
- ASSUMED D41-first-paint · `main.tsx` reads `GET /api/settings` before it renders the app at all (`loadPaneState`), so a hidden pane is hidden from the first frame; it waits at most 1.5 s, then shows both panes (also on an error), and ignores a late answer · "loads them before the first paint of the shell, so a hidden pane never flashes open" · revert: render at once with `PANES_SHOWN` in main.tsx
- ASSUMED D41-no-hub · no `/hub` event: other open pages (a second tab, the installed app next to a browser tab) keep their pane state until their next load. Each change stores only its own pane's absolute value, so a stale page never flips the other pane. Skipped because it would add an event to the locked hub contract and an echo race (a page's own quick toggles coming back out of order) for a nice-to-have · "nice-to-have, record as ASSUMED" · extend: publish a `settingsChanged` event on `PUT /api/settings` and follow it in `PanesProvider`
- ASSUMED D41-rail · a hidden pane leaves a 6 px rail (its grid column narrows to `--pane-rail`, not 0) that holds its reveal handle, so at 1440 px the main area starts at x = 6 and, with the panel hidden, the chat ends at x = 1434. An overlay handle at x = 0 / 1440 would cover the chat's 8 px scrollbar at the right edge. At rest the rail looks like the hidden pane's edge (left: the sidebar's background and divider; right: the main background and the panel's divider) · "a slim reveal handle … a few px wide"; the own-check "the main area starts at x=0 plus the handle" · revert: `--pane-rail` in shell.css (0 would need the handle positioned as an overlay)
- ASSUMED D41-handle · the whole rail is one `<button>` ("Show sidebar (⌘B)" / "Show panel (⌥⌘B)"), so a click anywhere on it reveals the pane; its small 20 px button (the chip: bg-card, border-control line, the panel glyph) shows on hover or keyboard focus, 4 px inside the main area, **halfway down** the window: at the top it touched the session dot (left) and "Continue in terminal" (right) · "visible on hover, with a small button" · revert: `.sb-pane-handle-chip` in shell.css
- ASSUMED D41-sidebar-button · the sidebar's hide button is the brand row's last DOM child (after the ⌘K key, so the visual oracle's child paths hold) and is drawn before the key with CSS `order`; its auto margin takes the key's, so the key stays flush right and the mark, name and key keep their boxes (checked). 20×20, the panel glyph (a window with its sidebar marked), `--muted-2`, the text color on a `--bg-selected-card` hover, like the sidebar rows' × · "in the brand row's free space at the right, so no prototype part moves" · revert: `.sb-brand > .sb-brand-kbd` / `.sb-brand > .sb-brand-hide` in shell.css
- ASSUMED D41-panel-button · the panel's hide button is at the right of the panel's **first** row, the D21 overview's label row ("Agents overview"), absolutely placed so the row keeps its height; the prototype's "Agents & solutions" head is the panel's second row since D21, and a button there would sit mid-panel · "a hide button in the panel's head row" · revert: `labelAction` in RightPanel.tsx / AgentOverview.tsx
- ASSUMED D41-shortcuts · ⌘ or Ctrl on every platform (as ⌘K is), no ⇧; ⌥⌘B / Ctrl+Alt+B only while the session view is on screen (elsewhere the key is left to the browser and nothing is stored); ignored while focus is in a text field (a textarea, a text-like input, contenteditable; a checkbox, select or button does not count), while a modal is open (`aria-modal="true"`), on a key repeat, during IME composition, or when another handler took the key. With ⌥ held, the physical B key counts (⌥B types `∫` on a Mac), unless it types another letter (another layout). No existing shortcut uses B · "not while typing in a text field; check that no existing shortcut uses it" · revert: `paneForShortcut` in src/web/shell/panes.ts
- ASSUMED D41-focus · focus follows the control: a pane slid out while focus was inside it gives focus to its reveal handle, and a pane brought back from its handle gives it to its hide button (the panel's once its content has loaded); otherwise focus stays · the brief's "not focusable" + keyboard-reachable handles · revert: the layout effect in `PanesProvider`
- ASSUMED D41-save · every change is saved at once with only its pane's key, one save after the other (the service keeps the last); a failed save is silent (the page keeps its state, the next change saves again) · "changes are saved at once" · revert: `setHidden` in src/web/shell/Panes.tsx
- ASSUMED D41-mount · a hidden pane stays mounted (`inert`, `aria-hidden="true"`, not painted after the slide), so its lists keep loading and its state (e.g. D37's expanded finished subagents) is kept · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D41-popovers · D27's "as printed" popover closes when the panel slides out (its toggle goes with the panel) and stays closed when the panel comes back; the toast, the modals and the D24 Remote / D31 model popovers needed no change (the toast's box is the same with the sidebar hidden; the Remote popover stays under its toggle, checked in E2E) · revert: `panelHidden` in `ReportedTableView`
- ASSUMED D41-slide · 180 ms `ease` on the grid columns and the pane's transform together (the pane keeps its width, so its content never reflows); `visibility: hidden` after the slide; the session view clips sideways (`overflow-x: clip`) so the panel past the edge never scrolls the page (without it the page was 1820 px wide, caught by the E2E); `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` turns every part off · revert: the D41 blocks in shell.css / session.css
- ASSUMED D41-settings-view · Settings shows no row for the two keys; the panes' own controls change them · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D41-visual · the default layout is gated by the existing specs unchanged (shell, session-panel, session-header, full pass: green); the controls and hidden states are checked on their own in `tests/e2e/visual/panes.spec.ts` (like D18's Name row); only `docs/visual/panes.md` and its PNGs were written (`SWITCHBOARD_VISUAL_REPORT=1` for that spec only) · revert: n/a
- OPEN D41-browser-keys (for the developer) · Chrome on a Mac also binds ⌥⌘B (bookmark manager) and Firefox binds ⌘B (bookmarks sidebar). Switchboard takes the keys where it acts (`preventDefault`; the E2E proves the page receives them in the test Chromium), and leaves ⌥⌘B to the browser outside the session view. - ASSUMED D40-no-branching-body · a `POST /api/sessions` body with Worktrees on and a ticket branch but no `branching` is a task without an epic (D40's ruling: fetch, cut from the origin default branch), so every new session's worktrees follow D40, not only the form's · the ruling covers every task without an epic · revert: in `startNewSession` use `createForSession` when the body has no `branching`
- ASSUMED D40-no-origin · a repo without an `origin` remote is cut from its current HEAD as before D32 (an existing task branch still reused), the preflight row says `no origin remote: the worktree starts from the repo's current HEAD`, and a task without an epic whose repos all lack origin gets no Branching line · refusing would block every local-only repo · revert: `#planTask` in src/server/worktrees/manager.ts, `handoffBranching`
- ASSUMED D40-fetch-failed · a failed `git fetch origin` at Start refuses the session (409 `fetch-failed`, nothing created) rather than cutting from possibly stale refs; the preflight shows the failure as the row's error · the ruling's "always git fetch origin first" · revert: ignore the fetch result in `#planTask`
- ASSUMED D40-base-missing · a repo whose cut point is missing on origin and that is not dropped refuses the start (409 `base-missing`, nothing created); the form keeps Start disabled while a preflight row shows it without a choice, but a preflight still running or failed does not block Start (the server decides). With the epic on origin a missing base does not block (nothing is cut from it) · revert: `branchingWarnings` / `#planTask`
- ASSUMED D40-reuse · reuse order: an existing **local** task branch is used as it is (never reset to origin, its upstream untouched), else a new local branch tracking `origin/<task>`; a local branch checked out in another worktree is 409 `branch-checked-out`; a reused local branch is never deleted when a failed start is undone · the brief lists origin first, but a local branch cannot be created again and may hold unpushed work · revert: `#planTask` / `#createTask`
- ASSUMED D40-no-track · a new task branch is created with `--no-track` (otherwise git would make it track `origin/dev` / `origin/<epic>`, and a bare `git pull` / `git push` would go there); the agent's `git push -u origin <same name>` sets its upstream · revert: drop `--no-track` in `#createTask`
- ASSUMED D40-base-ref · a D40 worktree's `base_ref` is `origin/<cut point>` (Diff merge-base, unpushed count, the repo note's `from`); adopted worktrees of a branching session get the same by the stored rule (read in the worktree, no fetch) · revert: store the main checkout's branch as before
- RULING D40-epic-name (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D40-shell-chars) · the derived epic branch keeps only letters, digits, `-`, `_` and `.` after `feature/`; anything else (spaces, `/`, `[ ] ( ) & ! ## D43 · Every background task counts (2026-09-29)
- VERIFIED D43-cli · CLI 2.1.284 (`~/.local/bin/claude` → `versions/2.1.284`, its strings read read-only; no real-CLI run): task types `local_bash` `local_agent` `remote_agent` `in_process_teammate` `local_workflow` `monitor_mcp` `monitor_ws` `mcp_task` `dream` `auto_mode_scan` (id prefixes b a r t w m s k d e; the evidence's `wbetnz0pi` is a `local_workflow`); `system/task_started` is emitted whenever a task registers, with `is_backgrounded` (false for foreground work, absent for a workflow), `workflow_name`, `owned_by_subagent`, `ambient`; each task's first terminal status (`completed` / `failed` / `killed`) emits `task_updated` + one `task_notification`; the Workflow tool registers its task during the call (so `task_started` comes before its result) and answers `Workflow launched in background. Task ID: <id>\nSummary: <meta.description>…` with `tool_use_result {status: "async_launched", taskId, taskType: "local_workflow", workflowName, runId, summary, …}` (remote: `Workflow launched in a remote CCR session. Task ID: …`, `remote_launched`) · `docs/derivations.md` → *Every background task counts (D43)*
- ASSUMED D43-foreground · a `task_started` with `is_backgrounded: false` (a foreground subagent; a foreground Bash that ran long enough to be registered) is no background work: it counts only once a `task_updated` with `patch.is_backgrounded: true` moves it there; foreground entries are forgotten at the next turn's start · the CLI's own background list (`Hm`) excludes them, and a long foreground Bash that ends in the foreground might never report an end · revert: `BackgroundTracker.started` / `updated` in src/core/derive/background.ts
- ASSUMED D43-ambient · a `task_started` with `ambient: true` (transcript-less tasks, ambient `monitor_ws` watchers) never counts · the CLI keeps them out of its own background list; an ambient watcher can run for the whole session · revert: the `ambient` check in `BackgroundTracker.started`
- ASSUMED D43-subagent-tasks · tasks the CLI reports for a subagent's calls (`owned_by_subagent`, a subagent's background Bash or Workflow) count too; D30-main-only still holds for the tool-call path (only the main agent's calls are matched to D30's wordings) · "every system/task_started … whatever its type" · revert: skip lines whose `tool_use_id` is not a main-agent call, or with `owned_by_subagent: true` (parse the field)
- ASSUMED D43-held · a `task_started` whose `tool_use_id` is a main-agent call still waiting for its result is held for that result, which adds the call's task (D30's fields, the call's time, the CLI's id), so there is one entry; if the result's wording is not one Switchboard knows, the held start still counts with the call's kind and summary; an error result or a Bash that ends with the turn adds nothing · "unless the tool call already registered it (matched by task id or tool_use_id)", and the CLI streams `task_started` before the result · revert: `#held` in `BackgroundTracker`
- ASSUMED D43-kinds · type → kind: `local_bash` → bash, `local_agent` → agent, a type containing `workflow` (`local_workflow`) → workflow, anything else → task (`remote_agent` incl. a remote workflow not seen as a main-agent Workflow call, `monitor_mcp`, `monitor_ws`, `mcp_task`, `dream`, `auto_mode_scan`, `in_process_teammate`) · the brief's mapping, "workflow types" read as a type containing `workflow` · revert: `taskKind`
- ASSUMED D43-workflow-remote · a remote launch (`Workflow launched in a remote CCR session. Task ID: …`, `remote_launched`) counts as a `workflow` too · its text says "You will be notified when it completes" · revert: `WORKFLOW_LAUNCHED` / `WORKFLOW_LAUNCH_STATUSES`
- ASSUMED D43-workflow-summary · a workflow's summary: the result text's `Summary:` line, else `tool_use_result.summary`, else the `task_started` description, else the input's `description`, else `name`, else `Workflow` (the text's `Task ID:` likewise before `tool_use_result.taskId`); a script that failed to compile (`tool_use_result.error`) is no launch · the brief's order, with the structured fields the CLI also gives · revert: `workflowSummary` / `startedId`
- ASSUMED D43-generic-fields · a task known only from its `task_started`: `startedAt` = that line's arrival; `toolUseId` = its task id when the line has no `tool_use_id` (the field stays a string: additive); `summary` = its description (a workflow without one: `workflow_name`; else the type), a shell's `gh …` command when it waits for GitHub Actions (`github` on the description, D30's flag); the `background` activity's `tool` is `null` for a `task` (`Workflow` for a workflow) · the brief ("the summary is the line's description") · revert: `taskFromStart` / `BACKGROUND_TOOLS`
- ASSUMED D43-task-updated · a `task_updated` with a terminal status (`isTaskFinished`: completed, failed / error, killed / stopped) ends a task by its task id, one line before its notification; `paused` (a workflow) is not terminal, so a paused workflow keeps the session working until its notification or the process's end · D43 "a terminal system/task_updated ends it"; the CLI's own terminal set is completed / failed / killed · revert: `BackgroundTracker.updated`
- ASSUMED D43-fake · `[fake:workflow <s>]` and `[fake:bg-task <s> [<type>]]` reshape the D30 `bg-bash` probe instead of a new recording (no real-CLI run, the brief's "prefer none"): the Workflow call, its `local_workflow` `task_started` and its launch result are written as the 2.1.284 code builds them; the workflow's notification summary is its description (the real `terminal.summary` of a workflow was not read); the fake's script / transcript paths (`/tmp/fake-claude/workflows/<run id>/`) are text only · revert: tools/fake-claude/scenarios.ts / session.ts
- OPEN D43-long-lived (for the developer) · every task the CLI reports counts until it ends, so long-lived ones keep a session showing as working until they end or the process exits: agent-team teammates (`in_process_teammate`, which go idle rather than end), long-running `remote_agent` sessions, auto-memory `dream` runs, a paused workflow · D43 "whatever its type" · no change made (an exclusion list by type would be a new rule)

## D46 · Session bar pace (2026-09-29)
- SUPERSEDED D46-minutes (by RULING D46-first-minute below) · the allowance after *m* whole minutes is `m × 100 / 300` (floor), the minutes counted from the window's start (its reset minus 5 h), not from the clock's minutes: so the window's first minute allows 0 % (any usage then reads "Ahead of pace: 3% of 0%"), the last one 99.67 % (never 100 %), and a reset at 16:00:20 steps at :20 s · D46's "minutes elapsed ÷ 300 × 100 %" and the brief's "floor to the minute" read literally; D23 also counts from the reset instant · revert: `(at.step + 1)` in `sessionPace` (src/core/usage.ts) makes each minute's share available from its start, like D23's days (then 0.33 % in the first minute, 100 % in the last)
- ASSUMED D46-precision · rounded to 2 decimals and compared against the rounded value, shared with D23 (`paceStep` / `paceAgainst` in src/core/usage.ts; `weeklyPace`'s results unchanged, its D23 tests untouched): a Session at exactly the allowance the tooltip shows is yellow ("Ahead of pace: 62% of 62%") · as D23-precision · revert: compare against the unrounded fraction in `paceAgainst` (both windows)
- ASSUMED D46-range · a reset exactly 5 h ahead is the window's first minute (0.33 % since the ruling below); more than 5 h ahead, not ahead of now, unparseable, or no usable pct → `null` (the D17 look) · as D23-range · revert: `paceStep`
- ASSUMED D46-tooltip · the Session's `title` is exactly as D46 words it, `On pace: 38% of 50% until 14:05` / `Ahead of pace: …`: no "allowed" (D23's Week reads "… of 57.14% allowed until Mon 15:00") and no weekday (the step is at most a minute away), the numbers as D23-tooltip (up to 2 decimals, trailing zeros dropped), the next minute step as local 24 h `HH:MM` · the brief quotes the same text · revert: the `until` line of `paceView` in src/web/shell/format.ts
- ASSUMED D46-clock · computed in the browser from `usageWindows` (`key: 'session'`) and the page's clock, no API field; the sidebar keeps its 30 s re-render (and every `system` event), so each minute step shows within 30 s, not on the second · the brief: "today every 30 s, which is fine" · revert: a timeout at `nextStepAt` in Sidebar.tsx would show it on the second
- ASSUMED D46-look · the Session row reuses D23's `data-pace`, colors and marker unchanged (shell.css's D23 block already applies to any usage row; only its comment changed); `WeekPaceView` became `PaceView` with one `paceView` for both rows · "generalise it rather than copy it" · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D46-demo · the brief expected the demo's Session window to have no computable pace, but it has one: the demo lists the prototype's `62% · 1h48` as a Session window resetting 1 h 48 min after each answer (D17), so about 192 of its 300 minutes have passed (≈ 64 % allowed) and 62 % is on pace: the demo's Session bar is now green with a marker near 64 %. Keeping the demo unchanged would need either an API signal (D46: "no API change") or a demo-only rule in the browser (which cannot tell the demo from a real run), or dropping / moving the demo's reset (which changes its `62% · 1h48`). Chosen: D46 applies to every Session window, the demo's too, and the D17 visual gate (`tests/e2e/visual/usage-rows.ts`, used by the shell and full-pass specs) gates a row with a pace on its pace color (status done / need) and its marker (2 px, bar height, inside the bar, `--muted-3`) instead of the prototype Max bar's fill; everything else it gated is unchanged · the most conservative option that keeps D46's rule and its no-API-change clause · revert: the pace branch in `usageRowChecks`, if the developer prefers a demo without the pace (then pick one of the options above)
- ASSUMED D46-visual-reports · `docs/visual/` not regenerated: its reports are records written only with `SWITCHBOARD_VISUAL_REPORT=1`, and every side-by-side PNG shows the sidebar, so a regeneration would rewrite most of them next to five other lanes; `shell.md` / `full-pass.md` still record the Session fill as the Max bar's · revert: `SWITCHBOARD_VISUAL_REPORT=1 npm run e2e` after the merge
- ASSUMED D46-e2e · tests/e2e/session-pace.spec.ts seeds one get_usage reading (Session 62 %, reset about 2 h ahead on a whole minute; Week 18 %) and moves only the page's clock (`page.clock.install` → `pauseAt` → `runFor(60 s)` → `pauseAt` past the reset → `setSystemTime` before the window) with `timezoneId: 'UTC'`; the server keeps its real clock, so it lists the window throughout · as D23-e2e, with a running page clock so the 30 s re-render is what shows the minute step · revert: the spec
- ASSUMED D46-d23-e2e · tests/e2e/week-pace.spec.ts's Session reset moved from 2 h to 1 h after the seed, so it has passed on that spec's page clock and the Session keeps the D17 look there ("the Session row never takes the Week's pace"); tests/e2e/usage-footer.spec.ts now expects two paced rows (Session on pace, 64–64.67 % allowed since the first-minute ruling; Week) and none on the model row · D46 supersedes D23's "the Session bar is unchanged" · revert: the two specs
- RULING D46-demo (developer, 2026-09-29) · keep it as delivered: the demo's Session bar shows its pace (green, marker near 64 %), and the visual gate checks a paced row on its pace color and marker (ASSUMED D46-demo stands)
- RULING D46-first-minute (developer, 2026-09-29) · count each minute from its start, like D23's days: during minute *n* (1–300) the allowance is `n × 100 / 300` % (`(at.step + 1)` in `sessionPace`), so the first minute already allows 0.33 % and the last 100 %; still stepping at the end of each minute counted from the window's start, rounded and compared as before · replaces D46-minutes; unit tests, session-pace.spec and usage-footer.spec follow, docs/usage.md → Session pace updated

## Fix: stuck running status (2026-09-29)
- VERIFIED stuck-run-cause · session `proj-3013-configure-hubspot-opt-in-cookie-banner-across-both-dom` stayed `run`: its DB events (read-only) show user messages at 06:58:05 and 06:58:42 and the next `result` at 07:03:04 with `taskNotification: true`; its transcript shows both as `queued_command` attachments inside the CLI's own turn for workflow `wrn3br7gm` (opened 06:41:28); CLI 2.1.284's code yields a replay line (`isReplay: true`, the message's uuid) for each such attachment. The recorder's rule left `#pendingTurns` at 2 for good (10 messages, 8 results without the task-notification origin). Same rule in `440fea1` and `master` `5b26747`
- VERIFIED stuck-run-not-background · the Workflow `wbetnz0pi` (and the three later ones) did not keep the status: D30 / D43 background tasks change only `Session.activity`, and the only subagent row (`adf795bee5f4c3f53`) ended `done` at 07:26:41
- VERIFIED stuck-run-d44 · the D44 lead (commit 39446d3 on feature/queued-messages, not merged: an absorbed message's replay ends its pending turn while another turn is counted) matches this root cause, and D44's code passes the proj-3013 sequence of tests/server/supervisor/stuck-running.test.ts (run on a `git archive` copy of 39446d3). It does not pass the race in that file (a message written just before a task-notification turn's `init`, then folded into it: D44 counts the CLI turn as that message's, so the replay is not "absorbed" and the task-notification result keeps it) → stays `run`. This fix replaces the counting rule itself (replay = taken up, result = the open turn ends), which also passes D44's two recorder cases (a CLI turn absorbing a message; an early replay keeps the turn). The two touch the same lines of recorder.ts (`#onInit`, `#onReplay`, `#onResult`): when both merge, keep this accounting for the status and D44's `QueueTracker` for the clocks, and drop D44's conditional decrement
- ASSUMED stuck-run-replay · a replay echo means the CLI took the message up (it is answered by the `result` of the turn open then, or of the turn it opens); a message whose echo never comes still counts as to come unless a non-task-notification `result` ends a turn without any echo (the old rule, kept as a fallback) · no timeout added · revert: `#onReplay` / `#onResult` in src/server/supervisor/recorder.ts
- ASSUMED stuck-run-live-session · the developer's stuck session un-sticks after they rebuild and restart the service: the restart stops it (`shutdown` keeps `run`), recovery resumes a `run` session with `Switchboard restarted. Continue.`, which spends one turn, and the fresh recorder starts with no count. On the old build a message does not clear it (the count only grows); a restart, or Pause then Resume, does

## D45 · Loading a session (2026-09-29)
- VERIFIED D45-before · on master 980751d the session view is keyed by the session id (`Shell.tsx`, since M1.4), so switching never showed the previous session's data; but while the new session loaded (reproduced with its detail and events held back 1.5 s) the view was a blank frame: the raw id as the title, an idle dot, no root line or chips, `Diff · 0` / `Artifacts · 0`, the actions disabled, an empty chat with "Message …", an empty right panel, no `aria-busy`; the chat could show the detail's question batches before its events; every revisit refetched into that blank frame · recorded in docs/session-panel.md → *Loading a session*
- ASSUMED D45-scope · the loading state covers the session view's header, chat (the main chat and a subagent's chat, D36) and right panel; the Timeline, Diff and Artifacts tabs keep their own loading as before (they fetch their own data), while on them the header and panel still get placeholders · the ruling names header, chat and right panel · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D45-data · the chat's events are now loaded by the session view (`useSessionData`), not by the chat tab, so the view knows when the chat waits (one delay, `aria-busy`) and can cache them; they are fetched only while the chat tab is open (as before) and `/hub` events are applied on every tab; everything the hook holds is tagged with the session id, so another id's data never renders even without the `key` · "the view drops the previous session's data at once" · revert: move the fetch back into ChatTab.tsx
- ASSUMED D45-before-delay · before the 150 ms delay the waiting parts are empty: the header no longer shows the raw id while loading (only after a 404 or a failed load), and the chat shows nothing half-loaded (the detail's question batches wait for the events) · a raw id flashing before the title is itself a flicker · revert: the name fallback in SessionHeader.tsx, the `eventsState !== 'loading'` guard in MainChat
- ASSUMED D45-delay · one delay per wait (`PlaceholderDelay`): it starts when the view starts waiting (a switch, a first load, or the chat tab opened for a session whose events were never loaded), is dropped when the wait ends or the session changes, and the next wait gets its own; once it passed, only the parts still waiting show placeholders (e.g. only the chat when the detail is there) · "about 150 ms" · revert: `PlaceholderDelay` in session-loading.ts
- ASSUMED D45-aria · `aria-busy="true"` on the view for the whole wait (from the first moment, so assistive tech waits too), the visually hidden `role="status"` "Loading session…" only together with the placeholders (after the delay, so a fast load says nothing) · "mark it aria-busy with a visually hidden Loading session…" · revert: SessionView.tsx
- ASSUMED D45-shapes · no frame exists, so the shapes follow the loaded parts: header a 180×16 title bar where the title starts + a 240×11 root line block one row gap after it (4 px, `--radius-chip`), the idle dot and the disabled actions stay (chips: see RULING D45-chips); chat four bubbles user / agent / user / agent (44 % × 42, 72 % × 84, 30 % × 42, 56 % × 62 px; 12 px, SPEC's chat-bubble radius) in the conversation's padding and gap, the composer stays; panel the overview's block (the table's x and width, 96 px) and two one-line card blocks (56 px) where the loaded cards start, 8 px radius · SPEC tokens only · revert: session-loading.css
- ASSUMED D45-shimmer · a `--bg-selected-nav` band over the `--bg-card` fill sweeping left to right (1.6 s, ease-in-out, infinite); with `prefers-reduced-motion` no animation and no band (plain `--bg-card`) · "a light shimmer", "static with prefers-reduced-motion" · revert: `.sb-skel` in session-loading.css
- ASSUMED D45-cache · a module-level LRU of 20 sessions ordered by storing (a load or a `/hub` update of the shown session makes it the most recent; reading does not), holding the last detail and the last complete event list (whole lists, no per-session cap); `/hub` `event`s of other cached sessions are merged into their lists while any session view is open (on other views, e.g. the Inbox, they are not, and the revisit's background refresh catches up); a list is never started from `/hub` events alone; a 404 drops the entry; a failed background refresh keeps what is shown · "keep the cache small (e.g. the last 20 sessions) and correct: hub updates keep applying" · revert: `SessionCache` / `useSessionData`
- ASSUMED D45-merge · after a fetch the fetched list replaces what was shown (a cached list may hold older versions of an event) and the events streamed while it ran are merged on top by id (the chat's rule for its first load, kept) · revert: `mergeFetchedEvents`
- ASSUMED D45-errors · a 404 shows the existing missing state ("no such session", the id as the title); any other failed detail load shows the header's existing error line (`session-error`) with `actionErrorText` (the service's message, else "Switchboard is not reachable." / "The request failed (HTTP n).") and the id as the title, the chat and panel empty; failed events leave the conversation empty without placeholders (as before) · the view's only error state was the 404's, and a 500 reading "no such session" would be wrong · revert: `loadError` in SessionView.tsx / SessionHeader.tsx
- ASSUMED D45-visual · the prototype has no loading state, so `tests/e2e/visual/session-loading.spec.ts` checks the placeholders on their own against the same view loaded (frame kept, places, tokens, motion, reduced motion, `aria-busy`, the hidden text); `docs/visual/session-loading.md` + PNG generated; the other reports are not regenerated (the loaded views are unchanged, their specs and the full pass stay green) · revert: n/a
