## D44 · Queued messages (2026-09-29)
- VERIFIED D44-signal · the CLI's `isReplay` echo marks the **pickup**, never the receipt: the M0 recordings (every message was sent idle; the replay comes after the turn's `init` and thinking ticks, right before its first assistant line) plus the 2.1.284 binary, read-only `strings` (a turn's echoes are built from the messages it starts on and written lazily before its first assistant output; `replayMerged` echoes prompts merged into one turn; a running turn absorbs queued `next` prompts at a tool boundary as `queued_command` attachments, which `--replay-user-messages` writes as the message's `isReplay` line; the SDK's `peer_message_hold` doc: "when it drains, its user replay echo follow") · recorded in docs/derivations.md → *Queued messages (D44)* · no real-CLI run (D11 not needed)
- ASSUMED D44-pickup · the clock goes at the first of the next turn's `system/init` (it starts on the oldest message no turn started on) and the message's own replay: the head message's replay comes only before the turn's first assistant line, seconds into a thinking turn, so the replay alone would keep the clock after the agent started · D44 "the replay/ack, or the next turn's start, whichever marks the real pickup" · revert: drop the `turnStarted()` branch in `#onInit` (replay only)
- ASSUMED D44-paused · D44's "sent to a paused or stopped session, so it waits in the outbox until the session resumes" read together with D33 ("sending a message resumes it"), the contract ("its next message resumes it") and docs/chat.md: a chat message to a session without a live process still resumes it at once, and is queued `resume` from the write until the resumed process takes it up (the CLI's startup: SessionStart hooks, MCP servers, ~1.6–3.5 s in M0); nothing waits for the Resume button. The outbox proper holds only the stale answers (and service notes), whose answers bubble shows the clock until the outbox is written · the most conservative reading: no behaviour change against D33 · revert: see OPEN D44-outbox-for-chat
- ASSUMED D44-origins · `turn` applies to any message Switchboard writes while a turn runs (typed messages; stale answers sent to a live process); `resume` only to the message that itself resumed the session (`sendMessage` spawning); a new session's task, the Resume button's "Continue." and the restart note are never queued (they are the start or the resume itself) · "the developer's own chat messages" · revert: the `resuming` flag in `SessionSupervisor.sendMessage`
- ASSUMED D44-exit · a message still queued when its process ends (killed before the CLI took it) loses the clock, since the CLI never read it and nothing resends it; so does whatever a service crash left marked (`settleAfterCrash`, before restart recovery) · no clock outlives its process · revert: `closeQueued` in `#onExit` / the crash clean-up branch
- SUPERSEDED D44-absorbed · 39446d3's conditional pending-turn decrement (an absorbed message's replay ended its pending turn while another turn was counted) was dropped in the master merge: fix/stuck-running's turn accounting (messages not yet echoed + an open turn closed by its `result`) replaces it and also covers the race it missed (a message written just before the CLI's own turn's `init`, then folded into it); the queue tracker keeps only the clocks · integrator's decision
- ASSUMED D44-look · the clock is an inline 12×12 SVG (a circle and two hands, 1.2 px strokes, `currentColor`) in `--muted-3` (`#76756f`), beside the bubble at its bottom left (6 px gap, centred on the last line), absolutely positioned inside the message box so the bubble keeps its size and place and no text is added; the tooltip is the ruling's copy as `title` + `aria-label` (`role="img"`); the queued answers bubble gets the same · no prototype frame, SPEC tokens only · revert: `.sb-chat-queued` in session.css, `QueuedClock` in ChatItems.tsx
- ASSUMED D44-fake · fake-claude mirrors the reading: nothing is echoed for a message written while a turn runs; at the next main-chain `tool_result` the turn absorbs the plain ones (echoed there, no turn of their own; a `[fake:…]` message keeps its own turn; not written to the transcript, where the CLI writes an attachment); without a tool boundary it runs as its own turn after the `result`. `[fake:hold <s>]` holds a turn with no tool boundary; `FAKE_CLAUDE_STARTUP_MS` delays taking up messages like the CLI's startup · revert: `absorbAt` / `holdToken` / `startupDelayMs` in tools/fake-claude
- ASSUMED D44-answers-note · under queued outbox answers the answers bubble keeps its note "● Answers written into the briefs. Blocked agents are resuming…" (D44 changes only the clock) · revert: n/a
- OPEN D44-outbox-for-chat (for the developer) · if D44 meant that a chat message to a paused session should **wait** for Resume instead of resuming the session itself, that reverses D33 and the contract's "its next message resumes it": say so and it becomes a `pending_messages` row (kind `user`) shown as a queued bubble ("Queued: sent when the session resumes") and sent ahead of "Continue." on Resume · no change made
- OPEN D44-real-cli (for the developer) · the mid-turn absorption and the merging of queued prompts are read in the 2.1.284 code, never recorded; one D11 probe (a message sent while a Haiku turn runs a slow Bash, `--replay-user-messages`) would confirm where the echo lands and that the session ends `done` after the turn's one `result` · no change made
- OPEN D44-lost (for the developer) · a message the CLI never takes up (its process killed first) now shows nothing special (as before D44); marking it "not delivered", or resending it, would need a ruling · no change made

## D47 · Stacked task branches (2026-09-29)
- ASSUMED D47-key-match · a typed key matches, per repo, the origin branches whose name starts with `<KEY>-` exactly as the ruling says (the key is upper-cased, branch names are compared as they are: `proj-3013-x`, a branch named just `PROJ-3013` or `feature/PROJ-3013-x` do not match); the task branch itself is left out; one match = the parent, none = not in this repo, several = an error row / 409 `parent-ambiguous` · the ruling's wording · revert: `parentMatches` in src/core/stacking.ts
- ASSUMED D47-parse · the Parent field takes a key in any case (upper-cased) or a full branch name checked with `isValidBranchName`; a leading `origin/` is dropped (the parent is always looked up on origin) · convenience for a pasted remote ref · revert: `parseParent`
- ASSUMED D47-validation · refused (422 `branching.parent`, a `⚠` line in the form): the task branch itself, the task's own key, and, with an epic, the epic's base (the parent must be a task branch); the epic branch itself (or blank) means "not stacked" and is not stored, so such a body is exactly D40 · conservative · revert: `parentConflict` / `effectiveParent`
- ASSUMED D47-merged-at-start · a merged or closed parent is a warning in the preflight ("parent merged — base on its target instead" / "parent closed — …") and never blocks Start; if its branch is still on origin the worktree is still cut from it (the developer was warned and chose) · the ruling: warning, not a block; no strong reason found to block · revert: add the parent-status warnings to `branchingWarnings` / refuse in `#planTask`
- ASSUMED D47-prune · a stacked session's Start fetches with `git fetch origin --prune` (the spec), so a parent deleted on origin after its merge is not taken for present; a session that is not stacked keeps D40's `git fetch origin` (its expectations unchanged); the preflight already pruned · revert: `#planTask`
- ASSUMED D47-pr-target · the PR target of a repo without the parent: with an epic, the epic branch (even while it is not on origin: "created lazily", also with a base override); without an epic, the branch cut from (the origin default branch, or the repo's override when one is set) · the ruling names the default branch; an override is the developer's explicit base for that repo · revert: `resolveRepoBase`
- ASSUMED D47-old-tip · "the old parent tip" of `git rebase --onto` is the parent PR's `headRefOid` as gh reports it (read at Start, on every poll, and in the MERGED answer, which still carries the last head), else the commit `origin/<parent>` pointed at when the worktree was cut; stored in `worktrees.parent_head_oid` · gh's head is the latest tip the child may have followed; the cut commit covers a parent without a PR · revert: `#planTask` / `#checkParent` in src/server/worktrees/manager.ts
- ASSUMED D47-merge-kind · squash or not: after a `git fetch origin --prune`, `git merge-base --is-ancestor <old tip> origin/<parent's base>` (exit 0 → a merge commit: normal rebase; exit 1 → squash: `--onto`); a GitHub "rebase and merge" also lands as not-an-ancestor, so it gets `--onto`, which is right for it too; a failed fetch or an unknown commit = `unknown`, and the message then tells the agent how to check and gives both commands · the ruling leaves how to tell open · revert: `#mergeKind`
- ASSUMED D47-base-ref · a stacked worktree's `base_ref` is `origin/<parent>` (Diff, unpushed count), and becomes `origin/<parent's base>` when the merge is seen (where the branch is rebased to; `origin/<parent>` may be pruned after the merge) · revert: drop `patch.baseRef` in `#checkParent`
- ASSUMED D47-watch · only `MERGED` triggers rule 5 (a `CLOSED` parent is stored, nothing is raised: the ruling's trigger); no PR or a gh failure changes nothing (a failure goes to `onError`); a row is watched until its merge is seen or the worktree is removed; the watch rides on the existing 5-minute PR poll (first check 15 s after start) · revert: `#checkParent`
- ASSUMED D47-delivery · the message goes out only from the call that raised the Inbox item (so once per parent per repo, i.e. per worktree row, even across restarts: `sync()` raises a missed item and sends then); through `SessionSupervisor.sendMessage` (origin `service`), which resumes a paused / done session and queues behind a running turn (D44); refused (a terminal owns the session, the service is closing) → the session's outbox (`pending_messages` kind `parent-merged`, sent with its next message); a closed session gets only the item, whose detail says nothing was sent and names the steps · the ruling: "send the session a message … if the session is closed, only the Inbox item" · revert: `#raiseParent` in src/server/inbox/system-items.ts
- ASSUMED D47-force-push · the rule-5 instruction and message end "ask the developer before force-pushing the rebased branch": a rebased branch already on origin needs `git push --force-with-lease`, a destructive remote write the ruling does not grant (Switchboard itself never pushes) · see OPEN D47-force-push · revert: the last step in `parentMergedMessage` / `parentMergeRule`
- ASSUMED D47-item · the Inbox item: kind `parent-merged`, label `Parent merged`, status `need`, source `worktrees`, the `<repo> ⎇ <task>` chip, the ruling's title verbatim ("Parent <parent> merged — retarget and rebase <task>"), one action Dismiss (it informs; the agent acts), dated when the merge was seen · revert: `parentMergedItem`
- ASSUMED D47-handoff · the stacked lines follow the spec's block verbatim (`— ` in the Epic line, nested `  - ` items, the per-repo lines named by the solution names as posted, like the spec's `acme-app-front`), completed with: the fallback target `<epic> (epic, created lazily)` (the developer's confirmed completion), `(epic missing; parent not in repo)` / `(parent not in repo)` / `(base override; …)` notes, `its current HEAD (no origin remote) → no PR target (no origin remote)` for a repo without origin, a stacked Rule line (push the task branch only at a repo's first change; in a repo without the parent create the epic lazily as D40 says; PR into each repo's target) and a `When the parent merges:` line with rule 5; the Parent line names the one full branch every repo resolved (the typed value plus the names when they differ, the typed value when none has it) and the parent PRs by state (`PR #306/#1080 open`, `no PR`, `PR #306 open, no PR in <repo>`, `PR status unknown in <repo>`); without an epic `Branching model: task only, stacked` and no Epic line; with no picked solutions (D38) one per-repo rule line and the worktree instruction "cut from the base the Per-repo line above names"; non-stacked sessions keep D40's lines byte for byte · the spec shows the block, not every case · revert: `stackedLines` and helpers in src/core/stacking.ts
- ASSUMED D47-gh-at-start · Start runs `gh pr view <parent> --json number,state,url,baseRefName,headRefOid` in each repo that has the parent (for the Parent line and the watcher's first state); a gh failure never blocks the start (the line says `PR status unknown in <repo>`) · revert: `#planTask`
- ASSUMED D47-adoption · a D38 session's agent-made worktree of a stacked session is adopted with `base_ref` `origin/<parent>` and `parent_branch` when exactly one origin branch matches in that repo, read in the worktree (no fetch, no gh; the next poll reads the parent's PR) · revert: `#adoptedBase`
- ASSUMED D47-columns · the preflight table's Resolved base / PR target / Parent status columns show only while the task is stacked, so D40's table, E2E and visual expectations stay as they were; the API returns `prTarget` (and `parent: null`) for every row, stacked or not · the spec lists the columns for stacking · revert: the `stacked` condition in BranchingSection.tsx
- ASSUMED D47-ambiguous-blocks · a key naming several branches in a repo blocks Start in the form (`⚠ <repo>: … type the parent's full branch name`) and is refused by the server (409 `parent-ambiguous`, nothing created); dropping that repo unblocks · the ruling: "an error row … asking for the full name" · revert: `ambiguousRows` / `#planTask`
- ASSUMED D47-prefill · the phrases that pre-fill the Parent field: "create / cut / branch / start (it | this | the branch | the task) from / off (of)", "stack(ed) (it | this) on / onto / on top of", "based on", "on top of", "build (it) on", optionally "the branch / task / ticket / PR (of)" and a quote before the key; the field gets the key upper-cased (also when the text names the full branch, per the ruling "pre-fill the field with that key"); it follows the text only while untouched: anything typed, even clearing it, is kept · revert: `STACK_PHRASE` / `formParent`
- ASSUMED D47-placeholder · without an epic the Parent field's placeholder reads "Origin default branch (independent)" (the ruling's "Epic branch (independent)" names no branch that exists then) · revert: BranchingSection.tsx
- ASSUMED D47-fake-gh · fake-gh answers a `<repo>:<branch>` key (the folder of the cwd's repository, its main checkout for a worktree) before `<branch>`, so tests can give each repo its own PR number for one parent; nothing else changed · revert: `repoName` in tools/fake-gh/main.ts
- RULING D47-force-push (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D47-force-push) · keep as built: the agent asks the developer before force-pushing the rebased branch (confirms ASSUMED D47-force-push)
- RULING D47-closed-parent (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D47-closed-parent) · when the parent PR becomes CLOSED (not merged), raise an Inbox item "Parent <parent> closed — retarget <task> to <epic or default branch>", label "Parent closed", Dismiss only, once per worktree (survives restarts, raised from `sync()` if missed); Inbox item only, no message to the session · `#checkParent` sets `worktrees.parent_closed_at` (migration 0014), `onParentClosed` → `SystemItemService.parentClosed` / `sync()` → `parentClosedItem`
- RULING D47-columns (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D47-columns) · the PR target column shows always in the preflight table for every branching session (stacked or not); Resolved base / Parent status stay stacked-only · `prTargetCell` in branching-form.ts; supersedes the PR-target part of ASSUMED D47-columns
- ASSUMED D47-closed-transition · "becomes CLOSED" = the poll reads CLOSED while the row's stored parent state was anything else (OPEN, or unknown / no PR); a parent already CLOSED when the worktree was made (read at Start) raises nothing (the preflight warned about it), and a parent that is reopened and closed again raises no second item (once per worktree); a closed-then-merged parent still gets the merged item and message; the item's sync skips rows whose parent merged · revert: `closed` in `#checkParent`, `parentClosedWorktreesWithoutItem`
- ASSUMED D47-closed-target · the "retarget to" branch is the session's epic branch when it has one, else the parent PR's base (`baseRefName`, the repo's default branch for a bug fix), else "the repo's default branch"; the detail names the `gh pr edit <task> --base <target>` step and says nothing was sent to the session; the worktree's `base_ref` stays `origin/<parent>` · revert: `parentClosedItem` in src/server/inbox/system-items.ts
- ASSUMED D47-target-column-place · the PR target column sits after the task column (stacked: task · resolved base · PR target · parent status, as before); its cell reads `<epic> (epic)` / `<epic> (epic, created lazily)` / the default branch / `—` when nothing can be cut · revert: BranchingSection.tsx

## D48 · Switchboard peers (2026-09-29)
- ASSUMED D48-both-directions · one pairing creates both directions: each side makes the token the other presents to it and stores only its sha256; A can call B as soon as it knows B's listener address (sent at pairing, or with any later hello once B's listener is on); until then A shows B as `no address` · the ruling leaves it to us; fewer steps for the developer and still one explicit code per pair · revert: `PeerService.pair` / `addMachine` (store no outbound token on the host side and require a second pairing)
- ASSUMED D48-code · pairing code = 8 characters of Crockford base32 shown `XXXX-XXXX` (40 bits), valid 10 minutes, single use, burned after 5 wrong tries, only one waiting at a time (a new one replaces it), in memory only (a restart forgets it); typed codes read leniently (case, spaces, `O`→`0`, `I`/`L`→`1`) · "short-lived, single use" · revert: `PAIRING_*` in src/core/peers.ts, `PairingCodes`
- ASSUMED D48-port · the peer listener's port defaults to 13002 (the UI keeps 13001), changeable in the listener settings (`PUT /api/machines/listener { port }`, not the UI's port); the switch, the optional address and the port are database settings (`peers.listener`), not environment variables · revert: `DEFAULT_PEER_PORT`, `LISTENER_SETTING`
- ASSUMED D48-address · the listener binds `tailscale ip -4`'s first IPv4 line, or a typed address that must itself be a Tailscale IPv4 (100.64.0.0/10); "Add machine" takes an IPv4 literal with an optional port (no MagicDNS names: a name could resolve anywhere, and a literal is what the listener binds); on macOS the Tailscale app's own CLI is tried when `tailscale` is not on PATH · revert: `parsePeerAddress`, `assertPeerBind`, `tailscaleIPv4`
- ASSUMED D48-outbound-plaintext · the token a machine presents to its peer is stored as is (it has to be sent) in the `machines` table of the user's own database (data folder 0700); the token it checks is stored only as a sha256 · "stored hashed if feasible": feasible only on the checking side · revert: migration 0016 + `MachineRepository`
- ASSUMED D48-hello-address · a peer's listener address is taken from its authenticated hello (`POST /peer/v1/hello { address }`) whenever it changes, so switching a listener on or moving it needs no re-pairing; a hello without an address never clears the stored one · revert: `PeerService.hello`
- ASSUMED D48-names · machine names are local tags: the peer's own name at pairing, renamable on each side (`PUT /api/machines/{id}`); this machine's own name is the host name (renamable through `PUT /api/machines/self`, no UI yet) and reaches peers only at their next pairing · revert: `renameMachine`, `renameSelf`
- ASSUMED D48-remove · Remove revokes this side at once and tells the other machine (`DELETE /peer/v1/pair`, 5 s, best effort) to forget its side too, closing its streams; if the other machine is offline its row stays until it next calls (then 401 → `auth failed`) · revert: `removeMachine` / `unpair`
- ASSUMED D48-transport · the peer API is plain HTTP on the tailnet address: Tailscale already encrypts and authenticates the path (WireGuard), and the ruling chose a direct bind over `tailscale serve` (which would have given HTTPS) · revert: n/a (a TLS layer would need certificates per machine)
- ASSUMED D48-status · Settings → Machines polls `GET /api/machines` every 3 s while open (the state has no `/hub` event of its own; sessions and Inbox items of a machine are re-published on each state change, so the sidebar follows) · revert: `REFRESH_MS` in MachinesSection.tsx
- ASSUMED D48-remote-ids · a peer's session, question batch and Inbox item ids become `r~<machine id>~<id>` (and an AskUserQuestion event's `payload.requestId`, its batch id); question, agent, event and artifact ids stay raw (they only matter inside one answer; answers name questions by their raw id) · the ruling: "remote ids must never collide with local ones"; the fewest rewrites that keep every route addressable · revert: src/core/peer-wire.ts
- ASSUMED D48-cache · a peer's open sessions are cached in memory per connection (the list at each connect, then its `sessionUpdated` events; closed ones leave), so lists never wait on the network and an offline peer's sessions stay listed with `machine.state` offline; the cache is not persisted: after a restart of this service an unreachable peer's sessions show only once it is reached again · revert: `PeerConnection` (persist the snapshot in a table)
- ASSUMED D48-inbox-offline · an unreachable peer's Inbox items are not listed (they cannot be answered); they come back with the connection · revert: `PeerConnection.inbox`
- ASSUMED D48-not-proxied · besides the ruling's list (settings, folder management, schedules, embedded tools) also not proxied: History, the global Artifacts / Solutions / Schedules views, teleport, and the terminal handoff: a peer's session header has no "Continue in terminal" / "Attach here" (the terminal and its `claude --resume` would be on the other machine); Remote Control of a peer's session is proxied (the bridge runs on the peer) · revert: `PEER_API_ALLOW` + SessionHeader.tsx
- ASSUMED D48-no-chains · A sees B's sessions, never C's through B: the peer API answers local data only (`x-switchboard-peer`), refuses remote ids, and events that came from a peer are never streamed on (this also stops two listening machines from echoing `inboxChanged` to each other forever, which the first P2 test run showed) · revert: `PeerService.api` / `#publishFromPeer`
- ASSUMED D48-closed-remote · only a peer's open sessions are listed (its closed ones are not in History or the New-session name check here; its own History lists them) · revert: `PeerConnection.#refreshSessions` (ask `?closed=include`)
- ASSUMED D48-fix-session · "Open fix session" of a peer's failed-run system item runs the action on the peer and opens this machine's New-session form with the item's prefill (its folder is the peer's, so the form falls back to this machine's default folder); the Machine row can then be switched · revert: InboxView.tsx `newSessionAfter`
- ASSUMED D48-errors · forwarding answers: unknown machine 404 `not-found`, unreachable 502 `peer-unreachable` (with the reason), refused token 502 `peer-auth-failed`; every other peer answer (409, 422 with fields, 403 `peer-forbidden`) is passed on unchanged; time limits 30 s, 180 s for a start, the branching preflight and the hooks · revert: `PeerService.forward`
- ASSUMED D48-p3-form · the Machine row shows only when a machine is paired and never for a scheduled run (schedules stay local); a peer's Browse…, "From a remote session" and "Resume a terminal conversation" are hidden (folder management, teleport and History are not proxied); an unreachable machine is listed but disabled; switching the machine clears the folder, the picked solutions and a picked conversation; the name check uses that machine's open sessions; the start's answer (a remote id) opens the session view · revert: NewSessionModal.tsx
- VERIFIED D48-ask-hook · real-CLI probe (a), 2026-09-29, CLI 2.1.284, `--model haiku`, 1 user turn: in the interactive TUI the `PermissionRequest` hook fires for AskUserQuestion (input keys `session_id, transcript_path, cwd, permission_mode, hook_event_name, tool_name, tool_input, prompt_id, scratchpad_dir`; `permission_suggestions` null), and `{ behavior: "allow", updatedInput: { ...tool_input, answers: { "<question>": "<label>" } } }` answers it: PostToolUse's `tool_response.answers` held the answer and the model replied with it · question cards for hooked sessions go through the PermissionRequest hook
- VERIFIED D48-midturn · real-CLI probe (b), 2026-09-29, CLI 2.1.284, `--model haiku`, 1 user turn: a waiter that exits 2 while a tool runs is queued (`queue-operation` enqueue) and absorbed into the running turn at the next tool boundary (`queue-operation` remove `absorbed_mid_turn`, a `queued_command` attachment, UserPromptSubmit fires again), not a new turn; the model then replied only to the typed prompt ("DONE") and ignored the message · so messages are held until the turn ends (Stop) and released to the next waiter; the transcript reader also takes the `queued_command` form
- VERIFIED D48-rewake-format · probe (b): with `rewakeMessage: "The developer sent this message from Switchboard:"` and `rewakeSummary: "Message from Switchboard"` the prompt reads `<task-notification>\n<summary>Message from Switchboard</summary>\n</task-notification>\n<system-reminder>\nThe developer sent this message from Switchboard: <stderr text>\n</system-reminder>` on 2.1.284 · `switchboardMessageText`
- ASSUMED D48-probe-count · three real-CLI runs were used (the limit): the first one typed nothing (the driver's screen matching failed on the TUI's cursor-positioned text; 0 turns, killed; it lingered as an exiting process until the driver was stopped, which the fixed driver prevents with an independent watchdog and by closing the pty before reaping), then (a) and (b); the usage line never showed ≥ 95 % · no further probes
- ASSUMED D48-hook-events · installed: SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PostToolUse, Stop, SessionEnd → `event` (async, 30 s, never delaying the terminal); SessionStart + Stop → `waiter` (asyncRewake); PermissionRequest → `permission` (3600 s); no PreToolUse (the spike's pairing is done on PostToolUse by tool name + input, so one fewer node start per tool call) and no Notification · revert: `HOOK_PLAN` in src/core/hooks.ts
- ASSUMED D48-posix-command · exec form (`command` + `args`) only on Windows (the ruling); on macOS / Linux one command string with each path single-quoted (exec form there was not verified by the spike) · revert: `hookEntry`
- ASSUMED D48-script-path · the entries name this checkout's `src/hook/sb-hook.ts`, this node (`process.execPath`) and this UI port; moving any of them turns the state `outdated` (Install again); the script lives in the repo (no copy under the data dir) so it updates with the checkout · revert: `HookService.hookCommand`
- ASSUMED D48-waiter-everyone · every terminal session with the hooks keeps one waiter, hooked or not (so hooking later can wake it at once; unlimited life per the ruling), except Switchboard's own sessions (`entrypoint: sdk-cli`: answered at once) · revert: `onWaiter` (answer 204 when not hooked)
- ASSUMED D48-batch · several messages waiting for one wake-up go out together, a blank line apart (each exactly once) · revert: `#pumpNow`
- ASSUMED D48-at-most-once · a message is marked delivered before the waiter is answered; if the waiter's connection died at that moment it is lost rather than sent twice (its bubble keeps the clock); the spike's runaway ruled out duplicates first · revert: `#pumpNow`
- ASSUMED D48-one-per-turn · after a wake-up the next waits for the turn it started (UserPromptSubmit, then Stop), or 120 s without that turn; plus at most 3 wake-ups per 60 s per session (the spike's example numbers) · revert: `WOKEN_TURN_TIMEOUT_MS`, `DELIVERY_MAX`, `DELIVERY_WINDOW_MS`
- ASSUMED D48-unhook · Close unhooks (Reopen follows again); held permission calls then get no decision (the terminal dialog stays); messages still queued wait for the next hook (not dropped) · revert: `HookService.unhooked`
- ASSUMED D48-hooked-session · a hooked session is named `term-<CLI session name or folder>` (unique), titled with the CLI's name, origin `terminal`, `attached: false`, root = its cwd, no saved folder; statuses `run` (UserPromptSubmit), `need` (a held permission), `idle` (Stop), `done` (SessionEnd, or 15 s unlisted by `agents --json`) · revert: `HookService.hook` / `#statusOf`
- ASSUMED D48-transcript-import · on every hook event and whenever the file grows (a 1.5 s size/mtime poll) the whole file is read with the D16 importer (it skips stored entries), not tailed by offset as the spike suggested: simpler, and transcripts are read only on change · revert: `#pollTranscripts` / `#importOnce`
- ASSUMED D48-slash · a message that looks like a slash command (`/word …`) to a hooked session is refused (409 `hooked-unavailable`): it would reach the model as text · revert: `HookService.sendMessage`
- ASSUMED D48-hook-actions · Always allow (the CLI's `permission_suggestions` as `updatedPermissions`) is offered only when the CLI suggested rules; Deny with a message (1–2000 characters) and Always allow only for hooked sessions' requests: supervised sessions keep D6's Allow once / Deny · revert: `permissionActions` in src/server/inbox/pipeline.ts
- ASSUMED D48-answered-terminal · PostToolUse of the same tool and input closes a held request as answered at the terminal (a question batch shows "Answered in the terminal"); Stop and SessionEnd close every request the session still held; a hook call that goes away makes its item stale · revert: `#answeredInTerminal` / `#withdrawAll`
- ASSUMED D48-rewake-range · the internal fields are used for `claude --version` 2.1.284 ≤ v < 2.2.0 (tested: 2.1.284); else the documented `asyncRewake` with the same words in the text · revert: `REWAKE_TESTED`
- ASSUMED D48-peer-install · Install / Remove hooks and Hook into… are part of the peer API (the ruling: "an explicit Install hooks / Remove hooks action per machine"), so the Mac can set up the PC; the hook token never leaves the PC · revert: `PEER_API_ALLOW`
- ASSUMED D48-plan-display · plan approval (ExitPlanMode) is shown as a permission item with the plan in its input (JSON, as every tool input), no dedicated plan view · revert: n/a (a later view)
- OPEN D48-windows-live · nothing ran on Windows: `claude agents --json` there, exec-form hooks, the waiter's exit 2 wake-up (Windows ends async hooks with TerminateProcess), the Firewall prompt for the peer listener; the checklist is `docs/peers.md` → *Windows setup and live test (P5)*
- RULING D48-midturn-policy (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D48-midturn-policy) · "Deliver mid-turn": a message to a hooked session goes out as soon as possible, also while a turn runs (the CLI folds it in at the next tool boundary, VERIFIED D48-midturn); exactly once and the rate limit stay (one wake-up in flight, ≤ 3 a minute); the D44 clock clears when the transcript shows it taken up · `#pumpNow` no longer waits for the turn's end
- ASSUMED D48-midturn-details · a wake-up goes to the live waiter whether the session is idle or running; "in flight" lasts until the turn that took it up ends (its UserPromptSubmit, then Stop), so a Stop of the turn that was already running when it was sent does not end it: a turn that ends before its next tool boundary leaves the message queued in the CLI, which starts the next turn on it, and the next wake-up waits for that turn; 120 s without that turn lift the block; the bubble's clock clears when the transcript shows the `queued_command` attachment (the fold) or the wake-up's `user` line · the ruling's wording; the CLI's queue does the rest (probe b) · revert: the `terminal?.running` check in `#pumpNow`
- RULING D48-hooked-subagents (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D48-hooked-subagents) · "Import them too": a hooked session's plain subagents (Agent / Task, background ones included, `<session>/subagents/agent-*.jsonl` + `.meta.json`) appear in its agent overview / cards and their chats open; workflow agents are D51's (a clear seam) · `HookService.#importSubagents`, `subagentFiles`, `importTerminalTurns({ subagent })`
- ASSUMED D48-subagent-details · an agent row per `agent-<id>.jsonl` directly in `subagents/` (kind `subagent`, `taskId` = the file's agent id, `toolUseId` / `subagentType` / `description` from the meta, name = `agentType` or `agent`); its first prompt is its brief (`agent-prompt`), then its messages and tool calls, the whole file read on every sync (stored entries skipped) and the transcript poll also watching these files; status `done` once the file ends with an assistant message with `stop_reason: end_turn`, `idle` when the session ended first, else `run` (a file's last line is the only signal: no task notifications are read); files under `subagents/workflows/…` and `<session>/workflows/…` are left to D51 · revert: `#importSubagents`
- RULING D48-cache-persist (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D48-cache-persist; supersedes ASSUMED D48-cache) · "keep snapshot but block interaction until reconnection": each peer's last known open sessions and the detail the view needs are persisted; after a restart an unreachable peer's sessions stay listed (unreachable), readable on the snapshot, every interaction blocked with "<machine> is offline — reconnect to continue" (the server refuses with 502 `peer-unreachable`); reconnection replaces the snapshot · migration 0018 `peer_snapshots`, `PeerService.forward`, `offlineReason`
- ASSUMED D48-snapshot-details · stored per machine: its open sessions (every change of the list, unchanged lists not rewritten) and, for each session opened here, its detail and its whole event list (`GET /api/sessions/{id}/events` without `since`), raw as the peer answered; snapshots of sessions no longer open are dropped when the list changes; a machine that is not connected (any state but `online`) is never contacted: detail and events come from the snapshot (a 502 when none was taken), everything else, the Diff included, is 502 `peer-unreachable` with the ruling's text at once; the UI blocks the composer (with the reason as its note and placeholder), Stop / Esc, question cards (read-only with the reason), Pause, Close / Reopen and the model picker; a title rename or a sidebar × still reach the server and show its 502 · revert: `snapshotOf` / `offline` in `PeerService.forward`, `offlineReason` in ChatTab.tsx / SessionHeader.tsx
- ASSUMED D48-inbox-offline-kept · an unreachable peer's Inbox items stay hidden (as built, ASSUMED D48-inbox-offline): they cannot be answered, and the session view shows the waiting question read-only anyway · revert: `PeerConnection.inbox`
- ASSUMED D48-hello-wakes · a hello from a machine this service is not connected to cuts its reconnect backoff short (an attempt in progress is left alone, so two machines never keep restarting each other's attempts) · revert: `PeerService.hello` / `PeerConnection.wake`

## D49 · Context window meter (2026-09-29)
- VERIFIED D49-formula · CLI 2.1.284 binary (read with `strings`, never run): the status line's `context_window` = `qEe(usage, window)` → `bmn`: `used = round((input_tokens + cache_creation_input_tokens + cache_read_input_tokens) / window × 100)`, clamped 0–100; `output_tokens` are reported separately (`total_output_tokens`) and not counted; the usage is `kTe(usage)`: the last `iterations[]` entry that is not `compaction` / `advisor_message` when present; a total of 0 → no percentage (`xgt`). Switchboard matches it exactly (`usageContextTokens`, `contextPercent`). (The auto-compact trigger's own count `Ox` does add output tokens; the displayed indicator does not.)
- VERIFIED D49-window · binary: `modelUsage[<key>].contextWindow = qg(key, betas)`; `qg`/`Kh`: `/\[1m\]/i` in the model → 1e6; some models are natively 1M (capability `native_1m`); default `J$e = 200000`. The key is the raw model string (may differ from the API's `message.model`).
- VERIFIED D49-boundary · binary: stream-json `{type:"system", subtype:"compact_boundary", session_id, uuid, compact_metadata:{trigger, pre_tokens, post_tokens?, cumulative_dropped_tokens?, duration_ms?, user_context?, messages_summarized?, …}, logical_parent_uuid?}` (`gkt`); the transcript entry is `{type:"system", subtype:"compact_boundary", content:"Conversation compacted", level:"info", compactMetadata:{trigger, preTokens, postTokens?, …}, logicalParentUuid}`; `post_tokens` is set (an estimate) on the main compaction paths before the boundary is yielded. No M0 recording has a boundary; the fake's `[fake:compact]` follows the binary.
- ASSUMED D49-output · output tokens are not counted (the CLI's own indicator leaves them out) · the ruling: "+ output_tokens of that message if that matches how the CLI computes its indicator" — it does not · revert: `usageContextTokens`
- ASSUMED D49-compacted-shown · "compacted HH:MM" shows beside the text from the boundary until the next turn starts (the first `system/init` after the compaction turn's `result`; in a transcript import, the next typed prompt, and an imported compaction counts as finished), and permanently in the tooltip (`Last compacted: HH:MM (auto)`) · the ruling offered both; "until the next turn" keeps the bar short · revert: `compactedRecently` in src/core/context-meter.ts
- ASSUMED D49-reset · after a boundary the reading is its `post_tokens` (the CLI's estimate) until the next main-agent usage replaces it; a boundary without `post_tokens` → `Context —` until then (the CLI itself shows no percentage then) · revert: `reduceContext` `compact`
- ASSUMED D49-subagent-boundary · a boundary carrying a `parent_tool_use_id` (not seen in the binary) is a subagent's and ignored; subagent usages never count · revert: `#contextInput`
- ASSUMED D49-window-choice · the window follows the reading's model: its reported `modelUsage` window (the key equal to the model, or with `[1m]`, preferring the key whose `[1m]` matches the session's choice, else a plain key); a report under 1M never answers a `[1m]` choice, a `[1m]` key never answers a plain one; else derived from `[1m]` in the choice (no choice / `default`: the process's `init` model). A pick in the header re-resolves at once; a change to another model family shows its window from that model's first reply · revert: `contextWindow`
- ASSUMED D49-band-rounding · the band follows the shown (rounded) percentage, so `60%` is yellow and `80%` red as the text reads (59.5 % → `60%` yellow) · revert: `contextBand` input
- ASSUMED D49-storage · a JSON column `sessions.context` (migration 0015) instead of deriving from events: the events do not keep usages or compactions, and a per-message event would clutter chat and timeline; it is resolved per read against `sessions.model` · the migration number may clash with a parallel lane's 0015 (rename to the next free number at merge; the migrate test checks `version === 15` / `name: session_context`) · revert: the migration, `SessionRecord.context`, `toSessionContext`
- ASSUMED D49-old-sessions · sessions from before D49 show `Context —` until their next reply (or an Attach, which replays the transcript); Switchboard does not read transcripts on a GET to backfill · revert: add a backfill in `toSessionContext`'s caller
- ASSUMED D49-demo · no bar for the demo seed (`Session.context` is `null` when Switchboard never ran a process for the session and nothing is stored, like D31's pickers), so the D10 gate is unchanged; the bar has its own visual check · revert: `toSessionContext`
- ASSUMED D49-subagent-chats · subagent chats show no bar (they have no composer; the meter is the main agent's) · revert: SubagentChat.tsx
- ASSUMED D49-publish · every stored change publishes `sessionUpdated` (repeated lines of one message with the same usage write nothing; no throttle), and the session view reloads its detail on it (its existing 500 ms throttle) · revert: `onContext` in supervisor.ts
- ASSUMED D49-layout · one row: the track (flex), then the text, then "compacted HH:MM"; the composer grows upwards by 22 px so nothing under it moves; tokens formatted like the activity line (`formatTokens`: rounded down, `124k`, `1M`); the tooltip's window is grouped `200,000` (en-US) with the model · revert: `ContextBar` / context-bar.ts
- RULING D49-backfill (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D49-backfill) · yes: a session without a stored meter reads it once from its transcript (the main chain through the boundary), when it is opened / its detail first requested, asynchronously, then `sessionUpdated`; stored so it is never read on every GET; missing / unreadable → `Context —` · `SessionSupervisor.backfillContext` (from `GET /api/sessions/{id}`) and `#transcriptContext` at spawn; supersedes ASSUMED D49-old-sessions
- RULING D49-autocompact-mark (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D49-autocompact-mark) · yes: a small tick on the track where the CLI will auto-compact, the tooltip adds "Auto-compact at N%", the threshold exactly as the CLI computes it, no tick when auto-compact is off · `autoCompactThreshold` / `autoCompactConfig` in src/core/context-meter.ts, config read at each spawn
- VERIFIED D49-autocompact-formula · CLI 2.1.284 binary (read-only): `kK(e,n){let r=Math.min(cst(e.model),fct)…return g-r}` with `fct=20000` and `cst` = the model's default max output tokens (`A5(model).default`, 32 000 fallback `Fh`) as overridden by `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`; `_Q(e,n){let r=e-13000,s=n.testPctOverride;if(s!==void 0&&!isNaN(s)&&s>0&&s<=100)return Math.min(Math.floor(e*(s/100)),r);return r}`; `RTe` reads `testPctOverride` = `parseFloat(process.env.CLAUDE_AUTOCOMPACT_PCT_OVERRIDE)` and `enabled: Bf()`; `Bf(){if(Wdt())return!1;return $o("autoCompactEnabled",!0).value}`, `Wdt(){return Boolean(Le(process.env.DISABLE_COMPACT)||a.DISABLE_AUTO_COMPACT)}`; the "% until auto-compact" indicator (`dTe`) counts down to `_Q(effective)`; `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` (`Oxt`: `auto`, `k` / `m` suffixes, 100–1000 = thousands) sets the window "clamped to the model window" · e.g. 200k window, Haiku (`maxOutputTokens` 32 000): 200 000 − 20 000 − 13 000 = 167 000 = 83.5 %; a 1M window: 967 000 = 96.7 %
- VERIFIED D49-tick-scale · the CLI compares its threshold with `yy(messages)` = `Ox(last usage)` (input + cache creation + cache read **+ output** of the last reply) + an estimate of the messages after it, while the bar (and the CLI's own `used_percentage`) leaves the output out; the tick sits at `threshold / window` on the bar's scale, so a reply's output tokens (and a pending tool result) can trigger the compaction a little before the fill reaches the tick, never after it
- ASSUMED D49-precompute · the tick uses `_Q` (what the CLI's indicator counts down to), not `lTe` = `min(e − round(e × precomputeBufferFraction), _Q)`: the precompute buffer fraction is a server experiment (`tengu_amber_rokovoko`) Switchboard cannot read; with it the background precompute may start earlier, the threshold itself is `_Q` · revert: `autoCompactThreshold`
- ASSUMED D49-config-sources · the config is read at each spawn from the child's env (Switchboard's own env, minus the scrubbed `CLAUDECODE` / `CLAUDE_CODE_*`) plus the user (`<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR or ~/.claude>/settings.json`), project (`<cwd>/.claude/settings.json`) and local (`<cwd>/.claude/settings.local.json`) settings, later files winning, each file's `autoCompactEnabled` and its `env` block; managed (policy) settings, `/autocompact` typed in a session, server-side experiments and `--settings` flags are not read; a truthy env value is `1` / `true` / `yes` / `on`; a session never spawned since the rulings (e.g. backfilled only) shows the CLI's defaults (on, no overrides) · revert: `readClaudeSettings` / `autoCompactConfig`
- ASSUMED D49-window-override · `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` counts only when smaller than the model's window (the CLI's text: "clamped to the model window"); it comes only from a settings `env` block (Switchboard scrubs `CLAUDE_CODE_*` from the child env) · revert: `autoCompactThreshold`
- ASSUMED D49-tick-label · the tooltip's line is `Auto-compact at N%` with N = the threshold's percentage rounded (83.5 → 84), between the window line and the compaction line; with auto-compact off, `Auto-compact off` and no tick; the tick is 2 px `--muted-3` (the usage rows' allowance marker), positioned at one decimal · revert: context-bar.ts / session.css
- ASSUMED D49-backfill-scope · the backfill runs on the detail GET only (not the session list), skips a live session (its recorder writes the meter) and a session Switchboard never ran a process for (`remoteAvailable` null: the demo seed, so the D10 gate stays unchanged); two requests at once read once (in-memory guard); a resume / attach of a pre-D49 session reads the transcript at spawn instead · revert: `backfillContext`

## D50 · Stop the current turn (2026-09-29)
- VERIFIED D50-interrupt · CLI 2.1.284 binary (read-only `strings`, never run): the stdin `control_request` `interrupt` takes `{subtype:"interrupt", reason?, cancel_queued?}` ("Interrupts the currently running conversation turn"); the success reply is the receipt `{still_queued: string[], cancelled?: string[]}`; `system/init.capabilities` advertises `interrupt_receipt_v1` and `interrupt_cancel_queued_v1` (the list `SYr = [interrupt_receipt_v1, interrupt_cancel_queued_v1, msg_lifecycle_v1]`); the Agent SDK's `interrupt({cancelQueued})` sends `cancel_queued: true`. The schema text: "A Stop-means-stop-everything client (a remote UI's Stop button) sets this true so one round-trip halts the session"; "on a clean interrupt this receipt is written before the interrupted turn result" (as recorded in M0.1: the ack before the marker and the `aborted_*` result).
- VERIFIED D50-cancel-queued · binary, the receipt builder (`Ng`): with `cancel_queued` it calls `messageQueue.removeByFilter(hc)` where `hc(e) = e.agentId === <main agent id>`: **every main-thread command still in the queue is removed**, whether or not it carries a `uuid`; only uuid-stamped ones are listed under `cancelled`, and each gets a terminal `command_lifecycle` `cancelled`. Without it the queued commands "survive the interrupt … These WILL run". A batch already dequeued for the next turn is marked cancel-pending when it has a uuid, and the abort (`drain.abortController.abort("remote-cancel")`) stops the turn that runs. So Switchboard sends `cancel_queued: true` and the messages it withdraws never run.
- ASSUMED D50-no-uuid · Switchboard's stdin user lines stay without `uuid` (unchanged since M2.1): the CLI removes them anyway (above), the receipt lists nothing, and Switchboard decides what it withdrew from its own queue tracker (every message no turn has started on). Stamping uuids would let the receipt confirm each one, but it changes every message line (and the CLI's dedup by uuid) for a case the removal already covers · revert: add `uuid` in `userMessageLine` and read `cancelled` from the receipt
- ASSUMED D50-order · stdin lines are read in order, so every message written before the interrupt line is in the CLI's queue when the interrupt is handled (the binary handles control requests as they are read; never observed live) · see OPEN D50-probe
- ASSUMED D50-idle · after the stopped turn's `result` the session is `idle` (new outcome `stopped`), not `done`: nothing was finished, and it is ready for the next message; the main agent follows; after an exit the outcome counts as `done` · the ruling: "idle/done" · revert: `deriveSessionStatus` (map `stopped` to `done`)
- ASSUMED D50-which-messages · withdrawn = every message no turn has started on yet (the D44 queued ones, plus a message written idle whose `init` has not come): `cancel_queued` removes those from the CLI's queue too, so leaving one would show a bubble the agent never answers; a message a turn already started on stays (the interrupt stops that turn) · revert: `QueueTracker.withdraw` (filter `queued !== null`)
- ASSUMED D50-origins · messages of every origin that were not taken up are withdrawn (the CLI drops them all) and all their texts go back into the composer, including a service message (stale answers, D47's parent-merged note) or text that went out with an outbox note: the developer sees everything and decides; nothing is resent by itself · revert: route non-`user` origins back to `pending_messages` in `SessionSupervisor.#interruptNow`
- ASSUMED D50-bubbles · a withdrawn message's event stays in the database with `withdrawn: true` and the chat (and `chatMessages`) leaves it out: the bubble with the clock disappears, History / the transcript import are unaffected · the ruling allowed "disappear (or marked withdrawn)"; disappearing avoids a second copy next to the composer's text · revert: `if (user.withdrawn) continue` in chat.ts
- ASSUMED D50-combine · the withdrawn texts go into the field oldest first, separated by a blank line, and text already in the field comes after them (it was typed after they were sent), also after a blank line; the field gets the focus · revert: `withdrawnDraft`
- ASSUMED D50-late-echo · when the CLI had taken a withdrawn message up just before the interrupt (its echo arrives after the withdrawal), its bubble comes back as delivered and its text stays in the composer (Switchboard cannot take the text back out of the field the developer may be editing); the stopped turn aborted it anyway · revert: the `taken.withdrawn` branch in `#onReplay`
- ASSUMED D50-requests · a question batch or permission request the CLI withdraws while the Stop is in flight (`control_cancel_request`, not a D24 phone answer) leaves the Inbox: the batch closes without answers with `closedReason` `turn stopped` (D33's mechanism; its chat card shows "Closed · turn stopped"), a permission request goes `stale`; a request the CLI did not withdraw although the stopped turn ended is closed the same way. Unlike a Pause (whose stale batches stay answerable in the Inbox), a Stop means the developer does not want that turn's questions answered · revert: `QuestionPipeline.stopped` → `#markStale`
- ASSUMED D50-background · background tasks (D30 / D43: background Bash, background agents, workflows, wake-ups, monitors) are not the turn: the interrupt leaves them running (the CLI's interrupt aborts the turn's controller; only SIGINT / exit killed a background Bash in M0.1), and the session keeps showing their wait (D43: "working while any task is alive"). While only background work is pending (activity `background`) there is no Stop button and Esc does nothing · see OPEN D50-background
- ASSUMED D50-foreground-subagent · a foreground subagent cut off by the Stop (its Agent call returns the interrupt's error while it still runs) becomes `idle` (like a subagent a stopped process leaves behind) and no longer keeps the session `run` · revert: the `#stopRequested` branch in `#onAgentToolResult`
- ASSUMED D50-timeout · the Stop waits 5 s for the CLI's receipt and then, while a turn is still open, up to 15 s from the write for the result (Pause's own ack / result timeouts); on timeout: outcome `timeout`, an error line in the chat ("Stop: the agent did not stop within 5 s. Pause ends the process."), and under the composer "The agent did not stop in time. Pause ends the process." with a **Pause** button; nothing is killed without the developer · revert: `#interruptNow` / `stopTimedOut` in ChatTab.tsx
- ASSUMED D50-second-press · a second Stop (another click, Esc, another tab) while one waits shares its outcome and writes nothing; the button reads "Stopping…" (disabled) until the reply; only the call that did the withdrawal gets the texts (so one tab gets them, the one that pressed Stop first) · revert: `live.interrupting` in `SessionSupervisor.interrupt`
- ASSUMED D50-send-during-stop · a message sent while the Stop waits is written after it (`sendMessage` waits for the Stop), so the Stop never takes it back and it runs normally · revert: the `live.interrupting` wait in `sendMessage`
- ASSUMED D50-when-stoppable · Stop applies while the view's session is live with status `run` or `need` and its activity is not `background` (`canStop`); the server decides again (no turn → `idle`, nothing sent). Right after a Stop, Send comes back from the reply before the view's throttled refresh lands · revert: `canStop` in src/web/views/session/stop.ts
- ASSUMED D50-esc · Esc is read in the window's capture phase (like the subagent chat's, D36): it stops the turn when no element with `role="dialog"` / `"alertdialog"` / `aria-modal` is open (the model / Remote / printed-table popovers, the close confirmation, modals, the palette), focus is not in a text field other than the composer's (renaming a title, an own answer, a setting keep their Esc), and no modifier / IME / earlier handler; the composer's own field counts as "nothing else open" (typing and pressing Esc stops, as in the terminal) · revert: `escStops`
- ASSUMED D50-look · the Stop button takes Send's place, size and colors with the text "■ Stop" and the tooltip "Stop the current turn (Esc)"; the stopped turn is a step line `■ Stopped` in the step lines' mono muted look (`data-mark="■"`), the timed-out Stop a `✕` step line · no prototype frame, SPEC tokens only · revert: `.sb-chat-stop`, `stepMark`
- ASSUMED D50-pause · Pause (D7) keeps the plain interrupt (no `cancel_queued`): its process ends anyway, and D44 already clears the clocks on exit · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D50-peers · peer / hooked sessions (D48, another lane) are out of scope: the Stop works on Switchboard's own supervised processes only · per the brief
- ASSUMED D50-fake · fake-claude models the binary's receipt (taken when the interrupt arrives; `cancel_queued` removes queued stdin messages and lists the uuid-stamped ones under `cancelled`; without it they are listed under `still_queued` and run afterwards); `FAKE_CLAUDE_IGNORE_INTERRUPT=1` is a CLI that never acknowledges; the first-command "prewait latch" is not modelled · revert: `interruptReceipt` in tools/fake-claude/session.ts
- RULING D50-probe (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D50-probe) · yes: one D11 probe on the real CLI (below: VERIFIED D50-probe-*)
- RULING D50-background (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D50-background) · "offer when only background runs": with no running turn but background tasks (D30 / D43, workflows and background agents included), the Stop control offers "Stop background tasks" with a confirm dialog listing them; confirming sends `stop_task` for each; the tasks end as stopped; Esc does NOT trigger it · built: `POST /api/sessions/{id}/background/stop`, `SessionSupervisor.stopBackground`, `StopBackground.tsx`; supersedes the "no Stop while only background work runs" part of ASSUMED D50-background
- RULING D50-other-tabs (developer, 2026-09-29, answers OPEN D50-other-tabs) · keep as built: only the tab that pressed Stop gets the withdrawn texts (confirms ASSUMED D50-second-press)
- VERIFIED D50-probe-receipt · real CLI 2.1.284 (one D11 run, 2026-09-29: `claude -p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --model haiku --max-turns 3 --replay-user-messages --setting-sources project,local --allowedTools "Bash(sleep:*)"`, cwd `.spike/sandbox/stop-probe/`, CLAUDE* env removed, driver `.spike/stop-probe/driver.mjs`, 90 s watchdog, 25.5 s in all): `system/init.capabilities` = `interrupt_receipt_v1, interrupt_cancel_queued_v1, msg_lifecycle_v1, mcp_read_resource_v1, mcp_tool_ui_meta_v1`. The turn called Bash (`sleep 20`) at +2.6 s; a user message was written at +4.1 s, `{"subtype":"interrupt","cancel_queued":true}` at +5.1 s; at +5.13 s `control_response` `{"subtype":"success","request_id":"probe_stop_1","response":{"still_queued":[],"cancelled":[]}}` (the message had no `uuid`, so it is not listed), then the rejected `tool_result` ("The user doesn't want to proceed with this tool use. …"), `[Request interrupted by user for tool use]`, and `result` `{"subtype":"error_during_execution","is_error":true,"terminal_reason":"aborted_tools"}` (+5.18 s). The receipt came before the result, as the binary says.
- VERIFIED D50-probe-dropped · same run: the queued message never ran: no replay echo, no `init`, no second result in the 20 s that followed; stdin was then closed and the process exited 1 (its last result an error, as fake-claude models). Confirms VERIFIED D50-cancel-queued and ASSUMED D50-order on the real CLI; Switchboard's code and fake-claude needed no change (the fake answers the same receipt shape for messages without a uuid).
- VERIFIED D50-stop-task · binary (read-only): `{subtype:"stop_task", task_id}` ("Stops a running task"); a non-string `task_id` → error "stop_task: task_id must be a string"; the handler (`B0e`) stops the task, and a task not found (or already finished) is still a success; a user stop reports the task's end with status `stopped` (`wi(id,"stopped",{toolUseId,summary})`). Not run live (the probe was limited to the one interrupt session).
- ASSUMED D50-bg-where · "Stop background tasks" is an outlined button **beside** Send (Send stays: the developer may still write while background work runs), shown only while the session is live, runs no turn (Stop does not apply) and its activity is `background` with at least one stoppable task; a hooked session never shows it · revert: `stoppableBackground` / `StopBackground` in ChatTab.tsx
- ASSUMED D50-bg-dialog · the confirmation reuses D33's dialog look (overlay, `role="alertdialog"`, Cancel focused, Esc or a click outside cancels): title "Stop these background tasks?", one line per task in the activity line's words (`backgroundText`), a wake-up listed with "a timer the CLI cannot stop; it stays"; buttons "Stop tasks" / "Cancel"; a task the CLI refused shows "Not stopped: <id> (<error>)" and the dialog stays · revert: StopBackground.tsx
- ASSUMED D50-bg-wakeup · a `ScheduleWakeup` has no CLI task (its id is the tool call's), so `stop_task` cannot end it: it is not sent and the wake-up stays (listed as such); with only wake-ups pending there is no offer · revert: `stoppableTask`
- ASSUMED D50-bg-end · `stop_task` goes out one task at a time (5 s for each reply); a task whose reply is a success ends when the CLI reports it (`task_notification` / a terminal `task_updated`), else after 5 s more Switchboard ends it itself as stopped (the CLI said it stopped it); a background agent's card turns idle (`stopped`); a refusal or no reply leaves the task and is reported · revert: `SessionSupervisor.stopBackground`, `StreamRecorder.endBackgroundTask`
- ASSUMED D50-bg-route · `POST /api/sessions/{id}/background/stop` with an optional `{ taskIds }` (absent = every stoppable task; a malformed body is 422 `invalid` on `taskIds`); it answers `{ session, stopped, failed }`, nothing sent without a live process; the server does not refuse it while a turn runs (the UI offers it only without one) · revert: the route in src/server/api/sessions.ts
- ASSUMED D50-bg-fake · fake-claude answers `stop_task` like the binary (empty success, also for an unknown task; an error without `task_id`) and ends a pending `[fake:background]` / `[fake:workflow]` / `[fake:bg-task]` task at once with its recorded `task_updated` (status `killed`) and `task_notification` (status `stopped`), with no turn of its own afterwards · revert: `stopTask` in tools/fake-claude/session.ts
- ASSUMED D50-peers · both routes are on the peer API allow-list (`PEER_API_ALLOW`) and their answers are mapped as `wrapped` (the `session` inside gets its remote id and machine, `peerAnswerKind`), so B stops A's turns and background tasks as its own; the withdrawn texts reach the tab on B that pressed Stop · revert: src/server/peers/service.ts, src/core/peer-wire.ts
- ASSUMED D50-hooked · a hooked terminal session (D48 P4) answers 409 `hooked-unavailable` on both routes ("Stop stays in the terminal (Esc there): hooks cannot interrupt a turn of a process Switchboard does not run." / "Background tasks are stopped in the terminal: hooks cannot stop them."); its composer shows neither button (`canStop` / `stoppableBackground` check `hooked`; it has no live process anyway) and Esc does nothing · revert: `HOOKED_UNAVAILABLE` in src/server/api/sessions.ts

## D51 · Workflow agents are visible (2026-09-29)
- VERIFIED D51-stream · CLI 2.1.284 binary (`~/.local/share/claude/versions/2.1.284`, `strings` read-only, never run): `Ove` builds `{type:"system", subtype:"task_progress", task_id, tool_use_id, description, usage, last_tool_name, summary, workflow_progress}`; a `local_workflow` task calls it from its progress batcher (`onSdkEmit`) with `workflowProgress: Pe.workflowProgress.filter(…)` (its `workflow_phase` / `workflow_agent` entries, the whole list) whenever an entry is not a plain `progress` update, else at most once per interval (otherwise the line has no list), `description` = `<phaseTitle>: <label>` of the newest agent entry, `last_tool_name` = its label; the agent entries' `state` is one of `start` (queued: `queuedAt` only; spawned: `startedAt` + `agentId`), `progress`, `done`, `error` (`St("start"|"progress"|"done"|"error")`, a blocked agent `state:"error", blocked:true`), with `index`, `label`, `phaseIndex`, `phaseTitle`, `agentId`, `model`, `startedAt` / `queuedAt` / `lastProgressAt` (epoch ms), `attempt`, `lastToolName`, `lastToolSummary`, `tokens`, `toolCalls`, `durationMs`, `resultPreview`, `promptPreview`, `error`. `task_updated`'s patch never carries the list (status, description, end_time, error, is_backgrounded only).
- VERIFIED D51-files-live · real session folders (read-only): the developer's session 533d8f68 had a run in flight (wf_5bf13727-e69, 14:12–14:31): `subagents/workflows/<runId>/journal.jsonl` and 14 `agent-<id>.jsonl` + `.meta.json` growing, and **no** `workflows/<runId>.json` yet; the 10 finished runs of that session and 3 of 4dea115b each have their run file (written at the end, `status: "completed"`). Journal lines seen: `launched`, `started` (`key`, `agentId`, `label`, `phase`), `result` (`key`, `agentId`, `result`); the binary also appends `failed` (`key`, `agentId`). Meta: `{agentType: "workflow-subagent", description: <label>, workflowPhase, spawnDepth: 1, requestShape, requestNonInteractive}`. Agent transcripts: sidechain lines with `agentId`, the first `user` line is the brief in the frame "[Workflow harness — computed task] … The computed task text follows:\n" + the brief indented by two spaces; the last line is often the `StructuredOutput` tool result.
- VERIFIED D51-locations · in all 13 runs the run folder and the run file sit under the session's own project folder (the transcript's, `-…-workspace/<sessionId>/`); the other cwd slugs (`-…-workspace-microfrontends/<sessionId>/`, a worktree's) hold only `workflows/scripts/<name>-<runId>.js` (the script lands under the project folder of the cwd the session is in when it launches). Switchboard searches every project folder holding the session id, so either works.
- VERIFIED D51-launch · the Workflow call's `toolUseResult` (transcript) is `{status: "async_launched", taskId, taskType: "local_workflow", workflowName, runId, summary, transcriptDir, scriptPath}` and its text names `Transcript dir:` / `Script file:` / `Run ID:`; Switchboard takes only the run id from it (checked `wf_[A-Za-z0-9_-]+`).
- ASSUMED D51-no-store · nothing is stored and no migration: the runs and agents are derived on read from the CLI's files (the record that survives a restart and exists for History, attached, hooked and peer sessions too) plus this process's stream, cached in memory per session; the `agents` table keeps main agents and subagents only · storing would duplicate the files and need a migration while two other lanes add some · revert: write `kind: 'workflow'` rows from `WorkflowService`
- ASSUMED D51-states · `start` without a start → queued = status `idle` with status text `queued` (`need` is reserved for "waits for you"); `start` with one / `progress` → `run`; `done`; `error` / the journal's `failed` → `fail`; once the run is not running, agents still running or queued → `idle` (cut off, like subagents a stopped process leaves behind) · the brief's "run/done/failed/waiting" · revert: `progressStatus` / `deriveWorkflowRun`
- ASSUMED D51-running · a run "may still run" while this session's live process launched it or streamed its progress and has not reported its end, or (no process of Switchboard's: a terminal's, a hooked session's) while its files changed in the last 2 minutes; the moment the process that ran it ends (pause, exit, a Switchboard restart) it reads stopped (`idle`), however fresh its files are · the CLI kills a workflow with its process · revert: `running` in `WorkflowService.#derive`, `RECENT_MS`
- ASSUMED D51-ids · a workflow agent's id is `<runId>--<index>` (the script's agent number: the same while queued and once running, so its row, card and chat address do not change when it starts), else `<runId>--<agentId>` (the journal alone, after a restart mid-run; such a run's ids change once its run file appears); no dot, because the web server treats a last path segment with a dot as a file (the first E2E run caught `/sessions/…/agents/wf_x.a1` answered 404) · revert: `workflowAgentKey`
- SUPERSEDED (RULING D51-solution) ASSUMED D51-solution · Solution = the solution folder of the agent transcript's `cwd` (the brief's rule), else `—`; the transcript's writes are not scanned (only the head and new lines are read). In the real runs every agent's cwd is the workspace root, so real orchestrator runs read `—` · see OPEN D51-solution · revert: add a write scan to `scanAgentEntries`
- ASSUMED D51-columns · a workflow agent's row: Agent = its label, Description = its phase (the model is in `Agent.workflow.model`, not shown), Status = its live action (its last tool, else `Thinking…` since it started), `queued`, or the status words; the run's row: its name, summary, `—`, `● d/n done · phase P` / `✓ n/n done` / `✕ failed · d/n done` / `stopped · d/n done` · revert: `workflow-agents.ts`
- ASSUMED D51-order · the overview lists the other agents first (main, subagents, as before), then each run oldest first with its agents indented in script order; the cards likewise (workflow agents after the others) · revert: `overviewEntries`
- ASSUMED D51-fold · D37 applies: done workflow agents leave the overview and the cards (counted in "✓ N finished"); a run's row leaves once the run is done and none of its agents is left; a stopped or failed run keeps its row · "finished ones fold like finished subagents" · revert: `overviewEntries`
- SUPERSEDED (RULING D51-card-cap: the line is now a button) ASSUMED D51-card-cap · at most 6 workflow agent cards per run (running ones first), the rest one line "+N more in <workflow>" (not clickable; the overview lists them all); expanding "✓ N finished" shows every card · "sensible cap/grouping when a workflow fans out many agents" (the developer's runs fan out 14–15 agents) · revert: `WORKFLOW_CARD_CAP` / `cappedCards`
- ASSUMED D51-background-line · D43's line reads "Running a workflow: <summary> · 3/7 agents done · phase Review" once the run has agents (the counts as the brief's example; `BackgroundTask.workflow` added by the supervisor from the task's run); before that it is unchanged (so D43's no-grid fake and its E2E read as before) · revert: `backgroundText`
- ASSUMED D51-timeline · workflow agents get no Timeline lanes (their messages are not events of the session; 15 empty lanes per run would bury the others) · revert: the filter in `TimelineTab.tsx`
- ASSUMED D51-chat · a workflow agent's chat reads its whole transcript on open (at most its last 32 MB) and again whenever its `workflow.version` (transcript size) or status changes in the session detail (which follows `sessionUpdated`, throttled), no polling of its own; its first prompt is the brief, labelled "Brief from the workflow", without the CLI's frame and dedented; its result is its journal `result` (a string as it is, else a JSON code block) or its error ("The agent failed." when the CLI gave none); the note reads "Workflow agents take no messages · reply in the main chat" · revert: `useWorkflowChat`, `cleanWorkflowBrief`, `resultText`
- ASSUMED D51-bounds · a transcript's head is read once (16 KB), then only appended complete lines (at most the last 64 KB of an unread middle); the project folders are listed at most every 10 s (shared by all sessions); running runs are re-read every 1.5 s and on every stream signal; a settled run (run file + every transcript read once) is never read again · "bounded and cheap" · revert: the constants in `src/server/workflows/service.ts`
- ASSUMED D51-empty-run · a run this process launched whose folder does not exist (yet) shows as a row with 0 agents; D43's `[fake:workflow <s>]` (no files) therefore shows "fake-audit ● 0/0 done" in the overview · revert: skip runs without a folder in `#derive`
- ASSUMED D51-script-meta · a run without its run file or a launch seen (after a restart, a terminal's run) takes its name / summary / phases from the script's `export const meta = {…}` read as text (quoted literals only), else the script file's name (`<name>-<runId>.js`), else the run id · revert: `parseScriptMeta`

No code change was needed: the parsers read all of it (the fake's shapes match; its metas omit `model`)
- VERIFIED D51-resume-call · binary: the CLI's own hints read `Workflow({scriptPath: '…', resumeFromRunId: '…', args: …})` ("To resume after editing the script", "Resume the paused workflow by calling"); the run file keeps `scriptPath` and `args`
- ASSUMED D51-write-scan · the first-write scan reads each transcript from its start in order, at most 4 MB per read (a line longer than that is skipped) and 32 MB per transcript in all, then gives up (the cwd's solution stands); it remembers at most 32 write calls waiting for their results; relative paths resolve against the line's `cwd`; a finished run is settled only once every scan found a write or reached the end · "cap work per transcript" · revert: `WRITE_SCAN_CHUNK` / `WRITE_SCAN_MAX` / `MAX_PENDING_WRITES`
- ASSUMED D51-solution-fallback · without a successful write the Solution falls back to the solution of the agent's cwd (a worktree cwd names its solution), else `—`; no branch chip for workflow agents (their worktrees are not registered to the session) · revert: `workflowAgents`
- ASSUMED D51-resume-failed · a **failed** run gets Resume run too (not only a stopped one): resuming replays its finished agents from the CLI's cache and runs the failed ones again, which is what a retry needs; a done run never · revert: the `resume` condition in `deriveWorkflowRun`
- ASSUMED D51-resume-where · Resume run sits on its own actions row under the run's overview row (the Status column is too narrow for a button); not on D43's line, which shows only while a workflow task runs (a stopped run has none) · revert: `ResumeRunRow` in AgentOverview.tsx
- ASSUMED D51-resume-script · the script path is the one Switchboard found by listing `workflows/scripts/<name>-<runId>.js` in the session's project folders, else the run file's / launch's `scriptPath` only when it is such a file of this run under the projects folder and exists; without one, no Resume run · revert: `WorkflowService.#scriptPath`
- ASSUMED D51-resume-message · the message is plain text asking for exactly that call and not to edit the script (the agent makes the call; Switchboard runs nothing); it sends at once, no confirmation (it is an ordinary message the developer could have typed); after sending the button stays disabled and reads "Asked the agent to resume it" for this page's life; blocked with its reason for a closed session ("Reopen the session to resume the run") and an unreachable peer ("<machine> is unreachable") · revert: `resumeRunMessage` / `resumeBlocked`
- ASSUMED D51-relist · a run the live stream announced whose folder is not there yet (the CLI creates it right after the launch) makes the session's folders be listed again at the poll interval instead of the 10 s TTL, until the folder appears or the run ends (the E2E caught a chat answering 404 for the first seconds) · revert: `missing` in `WorkflowService.#load`

## D52 · A peer's schedules and loops (2026-09-29)
- RULING D52-schedules (developer, 2026-09-29) · full management: a peer's schedules appear with a machine tag and are created, edited, deleted, run now, paused / resumed from here; the schedule lives and runs on the peer; the schedule editor gets D48's Machine row · built
- RULING D52-loops (developer, 2026-09-29) · all terminal sessions: loops from the peer's Switchboard sessions, hooked terminal sessions and un-hooked terminal sessions (read-only from their transcripts), with a machine tag; cards link to their session, un-hooked ones offer "Hook into…" · built
- ASSUMED D52-local-terminals · this machine's un-hooked terminal sessions' loops show too (untagged): the same route serves them, so it fell out naturally · revert: return only `peers.remoteTerminalLoops()` from `GET /api/terminal-loops` for non-peer requests
- ASSUMED D52-delete · Delete is new for every schedule (the contract had no delete route): `DELETE /api/schedules/{id}` removes the schedule and its runs (cascade), leaves the sessions its runs started and its failed-run Inbox items (unlinked), and is refused with 409 `running` while a run is in progress; in the UI it sits in the Edit form ("Delete schedule", a second click on "Delete it?" confirms) rather than as a third row button (the row's action column is sized for two, and the visual oracle covers it) · revert: the route in `src/server/api/schedules.ts`, `Scheduler.delete`, the button in `NewSessionModal.tsx`
- ASSUMED D52-lists-cache · a peer's schedules and terminal loops are cached here (and snapshotted, `peer_snapshots` kinds `schedules` / `terminal-loops`, no migration) and refreshed on connect, on a read that finds them older than 10 s (in the background), after each schedule change forwarded to it, and (schedules) on the peer's `scheduleRun`; the lists never wait on the network; the schedule table and the loop cards poll every 15 s. So a change made on the peer's own UI (save, pause, delete) reaches this page within ~10–25 s; no new `/hub` event name was added (the contract's list is locked) · revert: `PEER_LIST_STALE_MS`, `PEERS_RELOAD_MS`, `TERMINAL_LOOPS_MS`
- ASSUMED D52-schedule-run-event · `scheduleRun` joins the events a peer's stream forwards (`PEER_HUB_EVENTS`); the receiver refetches that peer's schedules first, then publishes it with the remote schedule id (marked, never echoed back) · revert: the set in `src/core/peer-wire.ts`, `#onPeerEvent`
- ASSUMED D52-edit-locked · a schedule stays on the machine it was saved on: Edit opens the form on its machine with the Machine row disabled ("A schedule stays on the machine it was saved on"); moving a schedule means deleting it and saving it on the other machine · revert: `scheduleMachine` / `lockedMachine`
- ASSUMED D52-names · schedule names are unique per machine (the form checks the chosen machine's schedules; the peer validates again) · revert: `takenScheduleNames`
- ASSUMED D52-row-tag · a peer's row shows its machine tag instead of the folder tag (its folder ids are that machine's and have no local name) · revert: `ScheduleTable.tsx`
- ASSUMED D52-offline-actions · with its machine offline a peer's row disables Edit (the form needs that machine's folders), Run now and Pause / Resume, each with the D48 reason as tooltip; the server refuses them anyway (502 `peer-unreachable`) · revert: `blocked` in `schedule-table.ts`
- ASSUMED D52-open-offline · a peer session's loop card keeps "Open session" while the machine is offline (the session opens read-only on its snapshot, D48-cache-persist); a terminal loop's "Hook into…" is disabled offline with the reason · revert: `blocked` in `loops.ts`
- ASSUMED D52-fix-session · "Open fix session" of a peer's failed-run item opens the New-session form on that machine (the prefill's folder is that machine's), so the fix session starts there · revert: `machine` in `InboxView.tsx` / `ModalOptions`
- ASSUMED D52-model · a schedule on a peer uses that machine's model list and last choice in the form's Model row (D42), and its runs start there with them · revert: n/a (the form reads `machineApi(peer).models()`)
- ASSUMED D52-badge · the sidebar's `<n> failed` badge counts a peer's schedules whose last run failed too (it reads the same list) · revert: filter `machine` in the sidebar
- ASSUMED D52-hooked-loops · a hooked terminal session's loops are derived by the loop tracker from the events the hook service imports (a new `HookService.on('event')` feed); its transcript has no `result` lines, so a `/loop` there keeps its first iteration open and its firings are not counted (CronCreate / ScheduleWakeup / Workflow cards work) · revert: `loops.listen(hooks)` in `app.ts`
- ASSUMED D52-terminal-results · for un-hooked terminal sessions a main-chain assistant message with `stop_reason: end_turn` counts as a turn's `result`, so a turn the CLI ran on its own after a `/loop` / cron is a firing (see OPEN D52-terminal-firings) · revert: `transcriptLoopEvents`
- ASSUMED D52-terminal-bounds · at most 32 un-followed terminal sessions per list; each transcript is re-read only when its size or mtime changed, at most its last 32 MB (a loop started before that is missed); `claude agents --json` is reused for 3 s (`listTerminals({ fresh: false })`); a machine whose `claude agents --json` cannot be read lists none (no error shown); nothing is stored · "bounded, cheap" · revert: `MAX_TERMINALS`, `MAX_TRANSCRIPT_BYTES`
- ASSUMED D52-terminal-ids · a terminal loop's id is `term:<claude session id>:<key>` (a remote id on a peer's); its terminal's claude session id stays raw (D48 P4's picker hooks it by that id through `/api/machines/{id}/api/terminal-sessions/{id}/hook`) · revert: `peerTerminalLoop`
- ASSUMED D52-old-peer · a paired machine without D52 answers the new routes 403 `peer-forbidden`: its schedule and terminal-loop lists stay empty here, silently · revert: n/a
