- OPEN D52-windows · nothing of D52 ran on Windows (as D48-windows-live): the terminal-loop reader uses the same projects-dir lookup as the hooks; check a `/loop` in a PC terminal shows on the Mac
- RULING D52-terminal-firings (developer, 2026-09-29) · yes, one D11 probe · done, see the VERIFIED D52-probe-* lines; OPEN D52-terminal-firings is answered
- VERIFIED D52-probe-run · one interactive session (CLI 2.1.284, `claude --model haiku --setting-sources project,local --allowedTools CronCreate CronDelete CronList ScheduleWakeup`, CLAUDE* env removed, cwd `.spike/sandbox/loop-probe/` (gitignored), a Python pty driver `.spike/loop-probe/driver.py` with an independent `sleep 240; kill -9` watchdog; 2 model turns, killed by the driver after 52 s, no process left): `/loop 1m say hi`. The driver counted a turn per `end_turn` line, so it stopped after the **first** firing instead of the second (see D52-probe-blocks); the second firing was not observed and the probe was not repeated (one probe, ≤ 3 turns)
- VERIFIED D52-probe-command · the typed command is a user line `<command-message>loop</command-message>\n<command-name>/loop</command-name>\n<command-args>1m say hi</command-args>` (`turnOrigin: "human"`), then the skill's body as an `isMeta` user line; the model called `ToolSearch` then `CronCreate {cron: "*/1 * * * *", prompt: "say hi", recurring: true}` (result `Scheduled recurring job a5207d74 (Every minute). Session-only … Auto-expires after 7 days …`) and ended its turn **without** running the prompt at once
- VERIFIED D52-probe-fire · a firing (≈ 28 s later, on the minute) is written as `queue-operation` enqueue / dequeue (`content: "say hi"`), `system` `subtype: "scheduled_task_fire"` (`content: "Running scheduled task (Sep 29 8:48pm)"`), then the prompt as a **user line with `isMeta: true`, `promptSource: "system"`, `turnOrigin: "scheduled"`**, then the assistant's reply; transcriptItems skips `isMeta` user lines (and the queue / system lines), so the firing's turn reads as self-started: it counts as a firing, as assumed (ASSUMED D52-terminal-results holds)
- VERIFIED D52-probe-blocks · each assistant message is written as **one line per content block** (a `thinking` line, then the `text` line; a `tool_use` line), each with the message's `id` and its `stop_reason`: a turn's end had two `end_turn` lines, so the first build made two `result` events per turn and counted the second as a firing. Fixed: `transcriptLoopEvents` writes one `result` per message id, after its last line (test: `tests/core/peer-schedules.test.ts` → *D52 probe shapes*, fixtures `assistantBlockLines` / `scheduledFireLines` in `tests/helpers/transcripts.ts`)
- SUPERSEDED (probe) ASSUMED D52-hooked-loops · a hooked session's loops now come from its transcript through the same `transcriptLoopEvents` (`HookService.loopEvents`, `LoopTracker.useEventsOf`), so its `/loop` firings count; the imported events still trigger the refresh · revert: `loops.useEventsOf(…)` in `app.ts`
- RULING D52-peer-edits-live (developer, 2026-09-29) · "Push at once": an additive `/hub` event · built: `schedulesChanged { scheduleId, change: saved | paused | resumed | deleted | run }` published by the scheduler on every save / pause / resume / delete and with every `scheduleRun`, on the peer event stream (`PEER_HUB_EVENTS`), republished after a refetch with the remote id; the schedule table and the sidebar reload on it; the 10 s staleness refresh and the 15 s table reload stay as a fallback; tested with two processes (`tests/server/peers/schedules-loops.test.ts` → *D52-peer-edits-live*); OPEN D52-peer-edits-live is answered
- ASSUMED D52-changed-run · `schedulesChanged` also goes out (`change: "run"`) with every `scheduleRun`, so a client listening to the new event alone sees every change; a peer then refetches twice per run change (coalesced while one is in flight) · revert: `#changed` in `Scheduler.#publish`
- ASSUMED D52-changed-names · the event's `change` words are `saved` (create and Edit alike), `paused`, `resumed`, `deleted`, `run` · revert: `ScheduleChange` in `src/core/api.ts`

## D53 · Live activity for remote and hooked sessions (2026-09-29)
- VERIFIED D53-transcript · real CLI 2.1.284 transcripts under `~/.claude/projects` read only (never copied): one line per content block with its own `timestamp` and the message's `stop_reason`; the assistant `tool_use` line precedes its `user` `tool_result` line (so it marks the tool's start); a thinking block's line comes only when it is done; turns end with `system` `stop_hook_summary` / `turn_duration` (or `[Request interrupted by user…]`); subagent files are `isSidechain: true` and their lines' `stop_reason` can be `null` while they stream · hence no `PreToolUse` hook
- VERIFIED D53-peer · a peer's supervised session's activity already reached this machine (`activity` is in `PEER_HUB_EVENTS`, forwarded namespaced); fixed: this machine's cached peer session kept the activity of its last `sessionUpdated`, so the sidebar list (reloaded on every `sessionUpdated`) fell back to a stale state → the cache follows the `activity` events (not snapshotted) · `tests/server/peers/live-activity.test.ts`, E2E `tests/e2e/hooked-activity.spec.ts`
- ASSUMED D53-poll · while a hooked session's turn runs its transcript (and running subagents' files) is checked every 500 ms (one `stat` each; read again only when size or mtime changed, its last 256 KB); 1.5 s otherwise as before · revert: `ACTIVE_POLL_MS` / the active timer in `HookService.start`
- ASSUMED D53-stale · the staleness hint shows after 3 minutes without a transcript change or hook call (`STALE_AFTER_MS`), on the chat line and the sidebar row, as "· no activity for 3m" (whole minutes, then `1h 5m`), not while waiting for the developer; it also shows during a long tool or thinking block (nothing observable changed) · revert: `src/core/derive/hooked-status.ts`
- ASSUMED D53-start · a turn start (UserPromptSubmit, the transcript's prompt / first line of an open turn) after the newest turn end (Stop, the transcript's end line, an interrupt) runs; the earliest such start is the turn's start; Stop ends a turn even before its end line is written · revert: `hookedActivity`
- ASSUMED D53-thinking-words · a hooked turn's thinking uses the local line's rotating verbs (`Pondering…`) timed from the turn's start (the D19 look); `Writing…` after a text block; no token count (the transcript has no ticks) · revert: n/a
- ASSUMED D53-permission-words · a held PermissionRequest reads `⏸ Waiting for permission: <tool>` (the tool name only; the summary is in `activity.summary`); the supervised session's `Waiting for you` is unchanged (its `tool` stays `null`) · revert: `waitingText`
- ASSUMED D53-commands · a slash command's own user lines (`<command-name>…`, `<local-command-stdout>…`) open no turn: a local command runs none; a prompt command's turn opens at its first assistant line (a few seconds late) · revert: the `<command-` check in `transcriptTurn`
- ASSUMED D53-delivery · `Session.hookStatus.delivery`: ended → `ended`; a wake-up released to a waiter whose turn was not seen to start → `turn` while a turn runs, else `handed`; mailbox messages → `turn` with a waiter or while a turn runs (its Stop arms the next waiter), else `no-waiter`; nothing waiting → `no-waiter` when idle without a waiter (so the header warns before a message is sent), else `null` (the D44 tooltip) · "running" is the live line, not the pump's `terminal.running` (set when a wake-up is released) · revert: `hookDelivery`
- ASSUMED D53-note-look · the delivery words show as the clock's tooltip and a muted 11.5 px line under the bubble (right-aligned); the header's hooked note adds the no-waiter / ended words in bold · revert: `ChatItems.tsx`, `SessionHeader.tsx`
- ASSUMED D53-throttle · a hooked session's `activity` events go out at most once a second per session (the contract's D19 limit, `LatestThrottle`), the newest value always · revert: `ACTIVITY_EVENT_MS`
- ASSUMED D53-offline · a peer session's `activity` is `null` while its machine is not online (`peerSession`), and the chat hides the line for a blocked session · revert: `peerSession`, `ChatTab.tsx`
- ASSUMED D53-clock · the live times of a hooked session are the terminal's own transcript timestamps and its Switchboard's clock (the same machine); on a peer the Mac ticks them with its own clock, so a clock skew between the machines shifts the times and the staleness hint by that skew (Tailscale machines are normally NTP-synced)
- OPEN D53-background · a hooked session's background work (a `Bash` with `run_in_background`, background agents, workflows: D30 / D43 `background` state) is not derived from the transcript yet: after its turn ends the line goes (D51's workflow cards still show running workflow agents) · would need the transcript's background-task lines mapped like D30's stream signals
- OPEN D53-windows · nothing of D53 ran on Windows or with the real CLI (developer instruction: no real CLI in this lane); check on the PC that a hooked session's `● Bash` and thinking verb show on the Mac live, and that a session started before the hooks went in shows "No hook listening yet"

- ASSUMED D54-peers · a paired machine's session can be pinned / foldered here while its machine is paired (the id's machine must be in `machines`; the peer is not asked whether the session exists); an offline machine's sessions keep their places; **forgetting** the machine removes its sessions' places (trigger), so pairing again starts clean · revert: the `machines_sidebar_places` trigger (a new migration)

## D55 · Updates from GitHub releases (2026-09-30)
- ASSUMED D55-layout · versions side by side under `<dataDir>/versions/<v>`; the login service's definition is the stable pointer (re-registered for the new folder with M9.1's plan and file rollback); `<dataDir>/updates/installs.json` records current + previous; at start leftovers (downloads, staging) and `versions/*` other than the running and the previous one are removed, and only when the running install is itself under `versions/` (the folder the first release was unpacked into is never removed) · revert: `src/server/updates/install.ts`, `UpdateService.#run`
- ASSUMED D55-restart · checked that no login service restarts Switchboard on exit (launchd `KeepAlive` false, systemd `Restart=no`, no Task Scheduler restart settings) and left it so (a crash loop would resume sessions over and over); the update asks the manager for one start instead: macOS a detached helper (`relaunch.ts`) runs `launchctl bootout` + `bootstrap` after this process exited (launchd keeps the loaded plist), Linux `systemctl --user restart --no-block`, Windows a detached helper runs `schtasks /Run` after the exit; this process closes the SIGTERM way (sessions paused for restart recovery) · revert: `src/server/updates/restart.ts`
- ASSUMED D55-service-detect · "running as the login service" = macOS `XPC_SERVICE_NAME=local.switchboard`; Linux `INVOCATION_ID` + the unit's `MainPID` is this pid; Windows the task's `--env-file=<dataDir>\service\switchboard.env` in `execArgv` · revert: `detectRestartMode`
- ASSUMED D55-manual · started by hand (`npm start`, `npm run dev`), Switchboard never exits for an update: it installs into `versions/<v>`, re-points Start at login when that is on ("Start at login now starts <v>."), and shows `cd "<dir>" && npm start` · revert: `UpdateService.#run` step 6
- ASSUMED D55-checksum · the `.sha256` asset is required (missing → refused), one line `<64 hex>  switchboard-<v>.tar.gz` (`*` binary marker accepted), the name must be the tarball's; it is downloaded first · revert: `parseChecksum`, `#run` step 1
- ASSUMED D55-links · every symbolic and hard link in a package is refused, also one that would stay inside the folder (a release has none; links are the usual escape and cannot be created on Windows without privileges) · revert: `extractRelease`
- ASSUMED D55-limits · tarball 200 MiB, checksum 4 KiB, unpacked 512 MiB / 50 000 entries (1.0.0: 3.3 MB, 692 entries); at most 5 redirects; API 15 s, download 10 min, gh view 30 s / download 10 min, npm ci 15 min · revert: constants in `service.ts`, `extract.ts`, `github.ts`, `npm.ts`
- ASSUMED D55-npm · `npm ci --omit=dev --no-audit --no-fund` (the ruling's command plus two quiet flags), run as `node <npm-cli.js>` (npm_execpath, next to node; plain `npm` last on macOS / Linux) because `npm.cmd` needs a shell on Windows; the parent `npm start`'s `npm_*` variables are dropped; dependencies' install scripts run as in a manual install (no `--ignore-scripts`: the ruling's command, and a future dependency may need one) · revert: `src/server/updates/npm.ts`
- ASSUMED D55-fallback · gh is tried only after 401 / 403 / 404 / 429 (the ruling's cases plus the rate-limit status); a 5xx or a network error is reported without gh; "release not found" from gh = no release yet (not an error) · revert: `FALLBACK_STATUSES`, `#ghLatest`
- ASSUMED D55-item · one Inbox item per version (`payload.version`), status `idle`, source `switchboard`; What's new closes it and opens Settings → Updates (this machine's item only; a peer's item only closes); a newer release closes the older item (`superseded`); a start on that version or newer closes it (`updated`); the banner's × and the item's Dismiss are separate (the × hides that version's banner on every tab; Dismiss closes the item) · revert: `updateAvailableItem`, `updatesResolved`, `routeAfter`
- ASSUMED D55-banner · a fixed bar at the top center (no layout shift, so the visual oracle's pages are unchanged; the demo runs no updater); it shows a running update's step (no ×), a failed or manual-restart outcome (× hides it on this page), and "Switchboard was updated to <v> · Reload" when the service runs another version than the page was loaded with · revert: `UpdateBanner.tsx`, `bannerKind`
- ASSUMED D55-confirm · Update always goes through a confirmation (banner's Update opens the dialog at it); "N sessions will be resumed after the restart." counts sessions with a live process and shows only when the restart is automatic · revert: `UpdateDialog.tsx`
- ASSUMED D55-schedule · the first check runs right after the port is bound (after pruning), then every 60 min from start; the hourly one is skipped while an update runs; checks never overlap · revert: `UpdateService.start`
- ASSUMED D55-no-db-backup · no copy of the database before an update (`VACUUM INTO` is synchronous in node:sqlite and AGENTS.md forbids sync I/O on request paths); a rollback over a version that added a migration is refused at start until the database is restored · revert: add a backup step before the switch
- ASSUMED D55-git · git checkout = `.git` present (folder or worktree file); commands shown: `git fetch --tags origin`, `git merge --ff-only <tag>` (or `git pull --ff-only`), `npm ci`, `npm run build`, restart · revert: `detectInstallKind`, `gitUpdateCommands`
- ASSUMED D55-state · stored in the settings key `updates.state` (last check, latest release with notes, dismissed version), not a known setting; no migration · revert: `UPDATE_STATE_SETTING`
- ASSUMED D55-release-script · `npm run release:package` packs `git archive <ref>` (default HEAD) minus the excluded paths plus `dist/web`, refuses a dirty tree unless `--allow-dirty`, builds the UI unless `--skip-build`, writes into `dist/release/`, ustar (prefix split, pax header when a path does not fit), folders 0755, files 0644 / 0755 as git has them, mtime = the commit's; it only prints the `gh release create` command · revert: `tools/release/package.ts`
- ASSUMED D55-linux-watchdog · after `systemctl --user restart --no-block`, 60 s without the SIGTERM → the update reports "the login service did not restart Switchboard …" · revert: `restartTimeoutMs`
- ASSUMED D55-test-redirects · `SWITCHBOARD_UPDATE_API` (loopback http only), `SWITCHBOARD_UPDATE_TEST_INSTALL` (needs the API redirect), `SWITCHBOARD_UPDATE_TEST_UNDER_SERVICE` (needs the API redirect and the service redirect), `SWITCHBOARD_NPM_BIN`; every test server has `SWITCHBOARD_UPDATES=off` (also `tools/smoke/real-cli.ts`); `npm run dev` keeps the updater on (a git checkout: notify only) · revert: `loadUpdateConfig`, `testServerDefaults`
- ASSUMED D55-first-update · 1.0.0 has no updater, so installs of 1.0.0 move to the first version with D55 by hand (unpack, `npm ci --omit=dev`, `npm run service:install`); from then on the updater takes over · revert: n/a
- OPEN D55-windows · unverified on Windows: `schtasks /Create /F` while the task's instance runs, the detached helper surviving the end of the task's `node.exe` and its `schtasks /Run`, `npm-cli.js` next to `node.exe`, the rename of the staged folder under a virus scanner (retried 5×) · check on the PC with the first real update
- OPEN D55-launchd-helper · on a real launchd job the helper (detached = its own session and process group) is expected to survive the job's exit; verified only against tools/fake-servicectl (D12: nothing installed here) · check with the first real update on a Mac release install, or switch macOS to a `KeepAlive { SuccessfulExit }` design
- OPEN D55-peer-version · a peer's version in Settings → Machines was not added (optional in the request; the peer API has no version field yet) · would need an additive field on the peer's machine info
- OPEN D55-signing · the checksum comes from the same release, so it catches corrupt or swapped downloads, not a compromised repository or account; signing (minisign, GitHub artifact attestations) would close that · developer's call
- OPEN D55-db-backup · want a database copy before each update (see D55-no-db-backup)?
- VERIFIED D55-1.0.0 · the real 1.0.0 assets (read with `gh release download`, read-only) unpack with the new extractor exactly like system `tar` (621 files, 71 folders, same bytes); `npm run release:package` on this branch writes the same layout (only D55's new files and the UI's hashed asset names differ) and its `.sha256` passes `shasum -a 256 -c`

- ASSUMED win-ci-host-resolve · `loadConfig` keeps resolving `SWITCHBOARD_DATA_DIR` with the host's `path` also when `platform` simulates another OS (a real config always runs on its host); the carried-environment test expects `path.resolve('/', '/data/sb')` (`/data/sb` on macOS / Linux, unchanged) · revert: the test's expectation

- VERIFIED win-ci-local · macOS: typecheck green; the workflow's unit test files 57/57; the CLI dry runs with a simulated non-macOS host without `getuid` (a `--import` preload) print `gui/<uid>` and exit 0 (they exited 1 before the fix); the new test helpers round-trip the Windows runner's exact paths and node path through `systemdUnit`

## D57 · Paste and attach images and files (2026-09-30)
- VERIFIED D57-cli-blocks · read-only in the CLI binary (`~/.local/bin/claude` → 2.1.285; 2.1.284 has the same code): a stream-json user message's `content` may be an array; the submission maps its `image` blocks (`source: {type: "base64", media_type, data}`, `media_type` described as "image/jpeg, image/png, image/gif or image/webp") and `document` blocks (`source: {type: "base64", media_type: "application/pdf", data}`) into its attachments; stdin image blocks are validated ("image block has no source object", "base64 image media_type must be a string") and resized by the CLI itself or replaced by "[Image could not be processed: …]"; limits `{maxWidth: 2000, maxHeight: 2000, maxBase64Size: 5242880, targetRawSize: 3932160}`; a PDF the CLI reads is at most 20 MiB / 100 pages ("PDF too large (max 100 pages, 20 MB)"); a request 32 MiB (`33554432`, with a `32 MiB − 8 MiB` budget constant). Never run.
- ASSUMED D57-caps · 20 MiB per file (413), 50 MiB and 20 files per message (422); constants in `src/core/attachments.ts`, not settings · revert: `ATTACHMENT_FILE_MAX`, `ATTACHMENT_MESSAGE_MAX`, `ATTACHMENTS_PER_MESSAGE_MAX`
- ASSUMED D57-inline-budget · what the CLI would not take inline goes as a file instead of being refused: an image over 3.75 MB raw (the CLI's `targetRawSize`), a PDF over 20 MiB or 100 pages (a rough `/Type /Page` count), and whatever no longer fits 24 MiB of base64 in one message (in order) · revert: `planDelivery`
- ASSUMED D57-downscale · the browser redraws a PNG / JPEG / WebP with an edge over 2000 px or over 3.75 MB to fit 2000 px (PNG stays PNG, others JPEG 0.9; still too large → JPEG 0.85 / 0.7; the name's extension follows the type); GIFs (animation) are never touched; the server never re-encodes · revert: `downscalePlan`, `readForUpload`
- ASSUMED D57-retention · 30 days from upload, checked at every start (rows + files), plus folders of sessions that no longer exist and files no row owns; a deleted session's rows go at once (cascade), its files at the next start; staged uploads follow the same 30 days · revert: `AttachmentService.cleanup`, `ATTACHMENT_RETENTION_DAYS`
- ASSUMED D57-hooked · a hooked terminal session gets every attachment as a path on its machine (hooks carry text only; images too); its event lists them with `delivery: file` · revert: `inline: !hooked` in the messages route
- ASSUMED D57-paste · a paste that carries files attaches them and inserts no text (a Finder copy also carries the file's name as text, which would otherwise be typed); a paste without files is untouched · revert: `pasteFiles`
- ASSUMED D57-upload-json · an upload is JSON `{ name, data }` with the file as base64, one file per call (not multipart), so it passes the JSON peer proxy unchanged; the route's body limit is the base64 of 20 MiB + 64 KiB, also on the peer listener's two upload routes (everything else keeps 1 MiB) · revert: `registerAttachmentRoutes`, `buildPeerApp`
- ASSUMED D57-upload-timing · the composer uploads each file as soon as it is added (chips show progress, Send waits); the New-session forms upload at Start to the chosen machine (a changed machine or a cancelled dialog leaves nothing staged) · revert: `useAttachmentDraft(upload?)`
- ASSUMED D57-empty-text · a chat message may be only attachments (no text block is sent; the event's label is `Attached <name>` / `Attached <n> files`); a start's attachments need a first message (422 otherwise), since an empty task starts the process idle · revert: the messages route, `startNewSession`
- ASSUMED D57-placement · 📎 sits at the right end of the quick replies' row (inside the field it would change the field's padding, which the D10 visual gate compares); in the Full form inside the task field's right end; in the Simple form a row under the message; the file `<input>` is made on click (the Full form's visual gate counts inputs) · revert: `AttachButton` placements, `attachments.css`
- ASSUMED D57-serving · images and PDFs inline, everything else `application/octet-stream` + `attachment`; `nosniff` always; `default-src 'none'; img-src 'self' data:; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox` on all but PDFs (a sandboxed PDF does not open in Chrome's viewer); `?download` for the file chips; `Cache-Control: private, max-age=3600` · revert: `attachmentHeaders`

- ASSUMED D57-transcript-images · an imported terminal prompt's base64 image is stored as the session's attachment (`image-<n>.<ext>`, sniffed; not an image → placeholder), one without bytes lists as the placeholder "image"; History reads an image-only prompt as `[image]` (`TRANSCRIPT_FACTS_VERSION` 3, so cached facts are read again once) · revert: `saveImage` in `importTerminalTurns`, `humanPromptText`

## D58 · Subfolders in the sidebar (2026-09-30)
- ASSUMED D58-depth · at most 5 levels (a top-level folder is level 1), counting a moved folder's own subfolders; enforced by the service (422 on `parentId`) and mirrored in the UI (New subfolder disabled at level 5, too-deep targets disabled / no drop indicator); 5 levels fit the 256 px sidebar (10 px per level) · revert: `SIDEBAR_FOLDER_DEPTH_MAX`
- ASSUMED D58-delete · deleting a folder never deletes what is inside: its subfolders move up one level into its place among its siblings (in their order), its sessions go to the end of its parent folder, or become loose when it was a top-level folder (D54's behaviour); the folder menu asks first only when the folder has subfolders ("Delete folder…" → "Delete: N subfolders and the sessions move to …" / Cancel), a folder without subfolders is deleted at once as in D54 · revert: `removeFolder`, `deleteConfirm` in `SidebarSessions.tsx`

- ASSUMED D58-count · a folder's count is always its open sessions plus those of its subfolders at any depth (expanded too, not only collapsed), so the number does not change when it is folded; the amber dot stays a collapsed-folder signal (D54) and covers any depth · revert: `total` vs `sessions` in `renderFolder`
- ASSUMED D58-drop-zones · on a folder head a dragged folder uses three zones: the upper / lower quarter = before / after it at its level, the middle half = into it (D54 used halves for before / after); a dragged session still goes into the folder on any part of the head; a subfolder goes out to the top level (at the end) on the "drop here to move to the top level" zone (shown while a subfolder is dragged) or a loose row; a top-level folder dropped there does nothing · revert: `folderSideOf`, `resolveDrop`
- ASSUMED D58-new-subfolder-collapsed · "New subfolder" on a collapsed folder expands it first (one extra write), so the new folder is seen · revert: `startSubfolder`
- ASSUMED D58-menus · "Move to folder ▸" shows the whole tree indented (12 px per level); the session's own folder / a folder's present parent stays listed as "<name> (here)", disabled (test id `sidebar-menu-current-folder-<id>`), so the tree reads right; a folder's own subfolders are left out (no loops); "Top level" is offered only to a subfolder · revert: `folderTargets`, `folderMoveTargets`
- ASSUMED D58-move-body · `parentId` absent on `PUT …/position` keeps the folder at its level (D54's body re-orders among its siblings), `null` = the top level; `index` is the final position among that level's folders · revert: `parseFolderMove`, `moveFolder`
- ASSUMED D58-payload · the layout keeps its flat `folders` array, now in tree order with `parentId` on each folder (not a nested `children` tree), so a D54 client still reads it; the UI builds the tree from `parentId` · revert: n/a (a nested shape would be a breaking change)
- ASSUMED D58-migration · 0021 is `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN parent_id TEXT REFERENCES sidebar_folders (id) ON DELETE CASCADE CHECK (parent_id IS NULL OR parent_id <> id)` + an index on `(parent_id, position)`; no table rebuild was needed; loops and depth are the service's checks; the cascade only meets rows the service has already moved up (the repository upserts parents before children, then deletes the gone folders) · revert: a new migration
- ASSUMED D58-stale-tree · a stored loop or a missing parent (never written so) is read as top level (`normalizeFolders`), so nothing disappears from the sidebar · revert: `normalizeFolders`
- OPEN D58-touch · as D54-touch: on a phone / iPad (installed app) native drag and drop does not start from touch; the folder menu's Move to folder ▸ / Move up / down is the way to nest there
- OPEN D58-palette · no palette commands for folders / subfolders (none for D54 either) · add if wanted
- VERIFIED D58-tests · typecheck green; vitest (targeted, `--maxWorkers=4`, after merging master b9f03b4): tests/core/sidebar-layout.test.ts, tests/web/sidebar-dnd.test.ts, tests/server/api/sidebar.test.ts, tests/server/db/migrate.test.ts, tests/server/db/repos.test.ts, tests/server/hub/hub.test.ts, tests/server/api/routes.test.ts (139 passed); E2E (`SWITCHBOARD_TEST_PORTS=4970-4979`): tests/e2e/sidebar-subfolders.spec.ts (new), sidebar-layout, shell, visual/shell, visual/full-pass, palette, panes, close-sessions, session-titles, peers (29 passed). - ASSUMED D60-name-check · `existingBranch` is refused with 422 when git could read it as an option (leading `-`) or it holds spaces, control characters, `..` or `~^:?*[\`; everything else is git's to resolve (`refs/heads/<x>`, then `refs/remotes/<x>`; `<remote>/HEAD` is not a branch) · revert: `existingBranchName`
- ASSUMED D60-peers · the new route is not added to `PEER_API_ALLOW`: `POST /api/solutions/{repo}/isolate` was never on it (the Solutions view and its conflict card work on this machine only), so there is no peer isolate to pair it with · revert: add both routes to `PEER_API_ALLOW` together

## D61 · MCP servers page (2026-09-30)
- VERIFIED D61-cli-surface · CLI 2.1.285 help (read-only): `claude mcp add|add-json|get|list|login|logout|remove|reset-project-choices|serve`; `add-json <name> <json> -s local|user|project` (default local), `remove <name> [-s scope]`, `login <name> [--no-browser]`, `get` / `list` health-check approved servers and show unapproved `.mcp.json` servers as `⏸ Pending approval`. `mcp list` / `get` were NOT run against the real config (they connect to the developer's servers); their formats were read in the binary
- VERIFIED D61-get-list-format · (binary code) `mcp get`: `<name>:` / `  Scope: <label>` / `  Status: <text>` / `  Issue: …` / `  Type: …` / URL + `Headers:` (values in clear) or `Command:` / `Args:` / `Environment:` (`K=v` in clear), then the remove hint; `mcp list`: `Checking MCP server health…` then `<name>: <url> (HTTP|SSE|WS) - <status>[ — <issue>]` or `<name>: <command> <args> - <status>`; status texts `✓ Connected`, `! Connected · tools fetch failed`, `! Needs authentication`, `✗ Failed to connect`, `✗ Connection error`, `- Not configured`, `⏸ Pending approval (run \`claude\` to approve)`, `✗ Rejected (see disabledMcpjsonServers in settings)`, `⊘ Disabled for this project (re-enable via /mcp)`
- VERIFIED D61-control-protocol · (binary: SDK schema + print-mode handlers) `mcp_status` → `{mcpServers:[{name,status: connected|failed|needs-auth|pending|disabled,serverInfo?,error?,error_code?,config?,scope?,source?,tools?[]}]}`; `mcp_reconnect {serverName}`; `mcp_toggle {serverName, enabled}` writes the current project's `disabledMcpServers` (built-ins: `enabledMcpServers`) and reconnects on enable; `mcp_clear_auth {serverName}` revokes the tokens; `mcp_authenticate {serverName, redirectUri?}` runs the OAuth flow with `skipBrowserOpen` and answers `{authUrl, requiresUserAction:true, callbackExpected:true, redirectScheme:"localhost", state, callbackPort}` (claude.ai connectors: `callbackExpected:false`; already signed in: `requiresUserAction:false`), completes through its localhost callback and reconnects; `mcp_oauth_callback_url {serverName, callbackUrl}` takes a pasted redirect → **authentication can be driven headlessly**, implemented
- VERIFIED D61-no-model-call · the helper sends only `initialize` + MCP control requests, never a user message: as the usage poller (M0.3 recording: no model call, no transcript without a user message); the MCP handlers answer in the control loop without a turn (code read). Not re-probed against the real CLI (no real-CLI run in this item)
- VERIFIED D61-enable-mechanism · there is no `claude mcp enable/disable`; the CLI's own mechanism is `disabledMcpServers` in the folder's project entry of `~/.claude.json`, written by `mcp_toggle` (what /mcp → Disable does); used as is. Project-server approval (`enabledMcpjsonServers` / `disabledMcpjsonServers`) is not changed from the page
- ASSUMED D61-list-source · the list reads the config files (user / local from `.claude.json`, project from `.mcp.json`, approval from the settings) instead of running `claude mcp list` (which connects to every server and has no scopes); plugin / claude.ai / managed servers appear after a Check all (from `mcp_status`), read-only · revert: `src/server/mcp/config.ts` + `McpService.view`
- ASSUMED D61-project-key · the folder's entry in `projects` is looked up by the saved path and its realpath (the CLI's key is the project path; a subfolder of a git repo may be keyed by the repo root in some CLI versions: then local servers / disabled marks would not show) · revert: `readConfiguredServers`
- ASSUMED D61-global-file · `<config dir>/.config.json` wins when it exists, else `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/.claude.json` / `~/.claude.json` (the binary's `getGlobalClaudeFile`); a suffixed file name (`.claude-<x>.json`, an internal build variant) is not looked for
- ASSUMED D61-cache · check results live in memory per folder (lost at a restart; no migration); a config change drops that server's cached status; a server's cached status never overrides its disabled / pending / rejected state from the files
- ASSUMED D61-check-all · Check all = the helper's `mcp_status`, asked again every second while a server is still `pending`, up to 30 s; `claude mcp list` only when the helper fails · revert: `McpService.check`
- ASSUMED D61-reauth · Re-authenticate (a server that is not in `needs-auth`) sends `mcp_clear_auth` first, then `mcp_authenticate`; Authenticate (needs-auth) only `mcp_authenticate`. Abandoning a re-authentication leaves the server signed out
- ASSUMED D61-auth-wait · a sign-in is done when `mcp_status` reports the server `connected` (polled every second); after 5 minutes it fails with the terminal instructions; the tab is opened at the click (`about:blank`, then the URL) so popup blockers let it through
- ASSUMED D61-edit-order · same name and scope: remove, then add-json, restoring the old definition with add-json if the add fails; a rename / new scope: add first, then remove. Keys the form does not show (`oauth`, `timeout`, …) are carried over when the transport stays
- ASSUMED D61-masking · env and header values never leave the service (names only); args are masked after a secret-looking flag, in `NAME=value` with a secret-looking name, `Bearer x` and URLs; URLs lose the password and secret-looking query values (token, secret, password, key, auth, session, signature, code, …); a secret in a plain positional arg is not detected
- ASSUMED D61-url-rule · http / sse need an http(s) URL and ws a ws(s) URL, env names `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*`: stricter than or equal to the CLI's schema (not read in full); the CLI re-checks and its error is shown verbatim
- ASSUMED D61-dash-names · a name starting with "-" is refused (the CLI's regex allows it, but its argv parser would take it for an option); Check takes only a server the page knows (config or last check)
- ASSUMED D61-argv-secrets · the `add-json` argv carries the definition with its secret values (the CLI has no stdin / file input for it), visible to this user's processes while it runs; documented in security.md
- ASSUMED D61-peers · the MCP routes are on `PEER_API_ALLOW` (read and write): a paired machine may add stdio servers here, the same trust as starting a session on it; a peer's sign-in needs the redirect URL pasted back (its callback is on that machine's localhost)

- ASSUMED D61-visual · the prototype has five nav items: visual/shell compares the later nav items and the Tools label relative to the item above them and the footer with the sidebar scrolled to its end (the demo seed's sidebar now scrolls by 24 px at 900 px); full-pass measures the sidebar with the MCP item hidden and gates the item against its neighbour; artifacts / history compare their selected nav item by size

## Fix · sidebar scrolling (2026-09-30)
- ASSUMED sidebar-scroll-header · the SESSIONS label (count, new-folder +) stays outside the scrolling list, directly above it, so it is always in view (the request's "sticky header", without `position: sticky`); the DOM is unchanged, so the prototype's child paths (visual oracle) hold
- ASSUMED sidebar-scroll-tools · a long TOOLS list scrolls on its own after a quarter of the window (`max-height: 25vh`) and gives way down to about two rows (64 px) before the SESSIONS list goes under its minimum
- ASSUMED sidebar-scroll-min · the SESSIONS list's minimum is 160 px (the prototype's own `min-height`); a window too short for the fixed parts + 64 px of TOOLS + 160 px (below about 760 px of window height with the demo seed) falls back to the whole sidebar scrolling, as before
- ASSUMED sidebar-scroll-reveal · the opened session's row is scrolled into the list once per opened session (palette, Inbox, links, notifications, its own row), by the least scroll and only within the list; a later layout change or a scroll by hand is not undone; in a collapsed folder the outermost collapsed folder's head is revealed and the folder stays collapsed
- ASSUMED sidebar-scroll-menu · the ⋯ menus (already portals, `position: fixed`) open under their ⋯, above it when the window has no room below, else inside the window with their own scroll; scrolling the list, the sidebar or the page closes an open menu (a chat scrolling in the main area does not)

## Fix · peer reconnects (2026-10-01)
- ASSUMED reconnect-schedule · after a drop the first attempt starts at once, failed attempts then wait 1 s, 2 s, 5 s, 10 s and every 15 s (the report's "0 s, 1 s, 2 s, 5 s, then a cap of 15 s", with a 10 s step before the cap), ±20 % jitter (none on the immediate attempt); a successful stream resets the count; retrying never stops while the machine is paired (offline, auth failed and no address included, at the 15 s cadence)
- ASSUMED reconnect-grace · the grace period is 20 s (`PEER_GRACE_MS`); a new connection (this service starting, a new pairing) starts `reconnecting` too, so a restart shows "Reconnecting to …" for its first attempt instead of "unreachable"; a 401 is `auth failed` at once, no grace
- ASSUMED reconnect-hold · while `reconnecting`, actions are **held** (the least surprising: a message, an answer or a Pause typed during a 1–3 s relay drop just goes through, with the composer's usual sending state) for at most 10 s (`PEER_HOLD_MS`) or until the grace ends, then refused with 502 `peer-unreachable` "<machine> is still reconnecting — try again in a moment"; reads with a snapshot answer from it at once, reads without one (folders, models, the diff) are held like actions; once `offline`, everything is refused at once as before (D48-cache-persist)
- ASSUMED reconnect-stall · a stream counts as stalled after 25 s with nothing on it (the peer's keepalive is every 10 s: 2.5 intervals rather than exactly 2×, so one late keepalive on a slow relay does not cut a healthy stream); the hello and the stream's answer (its headers) have the 15 s request limit each (before, the stream's answer had none)
- ASSUMED reconnect-inbox · a `reconnecting` machine's Inbox items stay listed (an answer is held); they are hidden once it is `offline`, as before (ASSUMED D48-inbox-offline)
- ASSUMED reconnect-activity · no live activity line while `reconnecting`, as while offline (nothing current is known; D53's "none offline" kept)
- ASSUMED reconnect-words · the 502's message is "<machine> is unreachable — it retries by itself; Reconnect now tries at once"; the UI line is its own ("studio-pc is unreachable · retrying in 8 s" + the Reconnect now button, "Reconnecting to studio-pc… · attempt 3 · next try in 8 s"); the blocking reason in tooltips (schedules, loops) is "<machine> is unreachable"; tags read "· reconnecting…", "· unreachable", "· auth failed", "· no address" (auth failed used to read "· unreachable" too)
- ASSUMED reconnect-button · Reconnect now is offered while `reconnecting` and `offline`, not for auth failed (pair again) or no address (nothing to reach); it answers when the attempt is over (at most 30 s); a second press (or a second tab) joins the running attempt; a machine's hello still cuts the wait by itself (D48-hello-wakes)
- ASSUMED reconnect-log · one info / warn line per state change and per failed attempt while reconnecting, then only the first five failed attempts and every 20th (a night-long outage at 15 s stays readable: ~12 lines an hour after the first minutes); prefix `switchboard peers:`, the machine's name and id, never a token or an address with a token
- ASSUMED reconnect-settings-poll · Settings → Machines reloads on every `machineState` event and every 15 s as a fallback (was every 3 s); the schedules table and the loop cards read their lists again when a machine's state changes; the New-session form lets a `reconnecting` machine be chosen (its calls are held)
- ASSUMED reconnect-removed · `machineState` also goes out on pairing (the new connection's first status), rename and removal (`removed: true`, an additive field of the event only), so Settings → Machines on the other machine drops a revoked pairing at once (it used to be found by the 3 s poll)
- ASSUMED reconnect-test-hooks · the two-process tests simulate drops with `SWITCHBOARD_PEER_TEST_HOOKS=1` routes on the peer that serves (`POST /api/test/peers/drop` destroys its open peer streams, `POST|DELETE /api/test/peers/outage` stops its peer listener: connection refused), never a real network; test servers default to a 3 s grace (`SWITCHBOARD_PEER_GRACE_MS`) so the existing offline tests need not wait 20 s
- OPEN reconnect-tailscale-path · showing in Settings → Machines whether Tailscale reaches the peer directly or through a relay (`tailscale status --json`, read-only, bounded) was optional and is not done: it needs the status JSON's peer fields (`CurAddr` / `Relay`) checked on the real CLI on both platforms and a `status --json` mode in `tools/fake-tailscale`; the log and the hint already tell a relay's symptoms (cut / stalled / timeouts)
- VERIFIED reconnect-tests · typecheck green; vitest (`--maxWorkers=4`, `SWITCHBOARD_TEST_PORTS=4950-4959`): tests/server/peers/reconnect.test.ts, tests/server/peers/reconnect-world.test.ts, tests/core/peer-reconnect.test.ts, tests/web/machine-status.test.ts, tests/server/config.test.ts, and the related tests/server/peers/* (proxy, schedules-loops, pairing, attachments, live-activity, stop-turn, workflow-agents, units), tests/server/hooks/remote-hooks.test.ts, tests/core/peers.test.ts, tests/web/loops.test.ts, tests/web/schedule-table.test.ts, tests/web/remote-session.test.ts, tests/server/hub/hub.test.ts; E2E: peer-reconnect (new), peers, peer-schedules, hooked-activity (attachments, mcp and visual/settings have no peer / Machines parts) · full suites not run (developer instruction)

## Fix · long messages (2026-10-01)
- VERIFIED long-messages-cause · `clip()` (`src/core/event-payload.ts`, 4,000 characters) was applied to `assistant` text (recorder: new and merged blocks; attach.ts: imported and merged blocks) and `agent-prompt` text (recorder, attach.ts subagent files, a Workflow agent's chat), so every message over 4,000 characters was stored and shown cut; the full text stayed in the CLI transcript. The web cuts nothing: no max-height / line clamp on bubbles, no length limit in `ChatMarkdown` (react-markdown renders the whole string)
- ASSUMED long-messages-cap · the safety cap is 1,000,000 characters (UTF-16 code units, ~1–2 MB) per message text (`MESSAGE_TEXT_LIMIT`), the brief's "e.g. 1 MB per text block" read as characters; it applies to the merged message (the event), not to each block; past it the text is stored cut with `truncated: true` and offers "Message cut at 1,000,000 characters · Show full message"
- ASSUMED long-messages-tool-limit · still cut at 4,000: tool inputs and results, permission-request inputs, a turn's `result` text (it repeats the last reply and the chat shows it only as a label); user messages were never cut and stay whole
- ASSUMED long-messages-legacy-key · events stored before the fix carry no flag; a message event without `truncated` whose text is exactly 4,000 characters reads as cut (`textCutAt`). A message that was naturally exactly 4,000 characters long offers the button once; restoring it changes nothing but marks it `truncated: false`, so it is not offered again
- ASSUMED long-messages-match · the whole text is found in the transcript (the main file, then `<session>/subagents/agent-*.jsonl`, sidechain lines included) by the event's `message_id` (text blocks of that message merged with a blank line, as the recorder merged them), else its `uuid`, else the stored text as a prefix (the candidate closest to the event's time); every match must start with the stored text (a prompt's leading spaces aside), else it is no match
- ASSUMED long-messages-write-back · a restored message is written back into the stored event (`truncated: false`), and published as a `/hub` `event`, so it stays whole across reloads, tabs and peers and needs the transcript only once; the label is unchanged (it was always taken from the whole text)
- ASSUMED long-messages-tool-output · a tool call's whole input / result is answered by the same route (`saved: false`) and shown for the page's life, never stored (tool output is the bulk of the bytes); in the chat that is a subagent's Result ("Output truncated at 4,000 characters · Show full output") and a brief that came from the Agent call's cut `prompt` (cut exactly at 4,000 with `inputTruncated`). The timeline and step lines show no tool output, so they have no note
- ASSUMED long-messages-route · `GET /api/sessions/{id}/events/{eventId}/full`: 404 `not-found`, 422 `not-restorable`, 410 `transcript-gone`, 410 `not-in-transcript`, an uncut event answered as it is; on the peer API (answer kind `full-event`); it reads only the CLI's files, never writes them
- ASSUMED long-messages-workflow · a Workflow agent's chat (D51) is derived from its files on every request, so its brief, messages and result are now whole (to the cap) and it offers no note (its event ids are local to the answer); its tool results stay cut like everywhere else
- ASSUMED long-messages-no-repair · no one-time background repair at start: the on-demand route is enough, a repair would read every recent session's transcript at startup and rewrite events the developer may never open; OPEN long-messages-repair if a sweep is wanted later (bounded to recent sessions, idle-time)
- ASSUMED long-messages-fake · fake-claude gains `[fake:say-long <chars>]` (30–2,000,000): the `default` turn with a deterministic reply of exactly that many characters ending `END OF THE LONG REPLY.`; the E2E seeds a pre-fix event by cutting a stored reply in the test server's database (the transcript fake-claude wrote is the fixture)
- ASSUMED long-messages-hooked · hooked sessions (D48 P4) import through the same `importTerminalTurns` and are found by the same `claudeSessionId` lookup, so they are covered by the attach and route tests rather than a hooked-specific one
- VERIFIED long-messages-db-size · a read-only count over 97 local CLI transcripts (aggregates only, no content kept): 2,729 assistant messages, median 167 characters, p90 2.3 K, p99 4.8 K, longest 12.4 K; 52 (1.9 %) over 4,000, about 80 K extra characters in total, at most 22 K in one session
- VERIFIED long-messages-tests · typecheck green; vitest (`--maxWorkers=4`, `SWITCHBOARD_TEST_PORTS=4960-4969`): tests/web/long-messages.test.ts, tests/web/subagent-chat.test.ts, tests/web/chat.test.ts, tests/web/workflow-agents.test.ts, tests/core/full-text.test.ts, tests/core/workflows.test.ts, tests/core/peers.test.ts, tests/core/derive.test.ts, tests/server/supervisor/long-messages.test.ts, tests/server/supervisor/attach.test.ts, tests/server/peers/long-messages.test.ts, tests/server/peers/workflow-agents.test.ts, tests/server/workflows/workflow-agents.test.ts, tests/server/peers/units.test.ts, tests/server/peers/proxy.test.ts, tests/server/hooks/service.test.ts, tests/tools/fake-claude.test.ts (17 files, 197 tests); E2E (4960–4969): long-messages, session-chat, chat-markdown, visual/session-chat, history, move-conversations, hooked-activity, workflow-agents, peers, subagent-chat (26 tests) · full suites not run (developer instruction)

- VERIFIED D62-codex-source · Codex CLI read at tag `rust-v0.159.3` (latest release, 2026-09-30), never run (developer instruction: not installed): app-server transport, handshake, v2 thread / turn / item / approval / question shapes, `model/list`, `account/*`, `login status`, `mcp list --json`, rollout files, `notify` (`docs/providers.md` → *Evidence* has the source paths)

- ASSUMED D62-codex-env-key · Codex signed out per `login status` but `OPENAI_API_KEY` / `CODEX_API_KEY` set → sign-in "unknown", choosable (an env key works without a login)
- ASSUMED D62-opencode-auth · `opencode auth list` with 0 credentials and no provider env → sign-in "unknown", choosable (a provider that needs no key, e.g. a local model, still works)
- ASSUMED D62-schedule-default · a schedule template without `provider` (every schedule saved before D62) runs on Claude Code even after the default CLI changes; new templates store the form's CLI · revert: `templateProvider`
- ASSUMED D62-model-settings · each CLI keeps its own last model list and last choice (`models.options.<cli>`, `models.last.<cli>`; Claude Code keeps D42's `models.options` / `models.last`); picking another CLI in a form clears the model choice
- ASSUMED D62-form-cli-row · the Full form's CLI choice is its own Launch row **under** D42's Model row (the visual oracle keeps the Model row one gap below Ultracode; the generic toggles-height offset absorbs the added row); the Simple form has a CLI row above Model; the summary's `model` line names the CLI when it is not Claude Code (`model     Codex CLI · Default`)
- ASSUMED D62-settings-nav · Settings → **CLIs** sits after the prototype's seven sections, before Machines (the visual settings oracle compares nav items 1–7)
- ASSUMED D62-pdfs-codex · Codex takes images only (`localImage` / `image`): a PDF to a Codex session goes as a file path line (D57's file delivery)
- ASSUMED D62-demo · in the demo (`SWITCHBOARD_DEMO=1`) `GET /api/clis` answers fixed data (Claude Code and Codex installed, OpenCode not) and runs nothing
- ASSUMED D62-codex-experimental · the Codex bridge opts into `capabilities.experimentalApi: true` at `initialize`, because `item/tool/requestUserInput` (questions) is marked EXPERIMENTAL; probe 5 in `docs/spike-providers.md` checks whether questions arrive without it · revert: `CodexBridge.#start`
- ASSUMED D62-codex-policy · Codex threads start with `approvalPolicy: "on-request"` + `sandbox: "workspace-write"` (Codex's own "Auto": edits and commands inside the workspace run, leaving the sandbox asks), the nearest to D6's `auto` / `acceptEdits`; D6's fallback (`set_permission_mode`) is a no-op there
- ASSUMED D62-codex-context · Codex's `tokenUsage.last.inputTokens` already includes `cachedInputTokens` (OpenAI's usage convention), so the D49 meter's tokens = `inputTokens`; the window = `modelContextWindow`; no auto-compact tick for another CLI than Claude Code (its threshold is unknown) · probe 3
- ASSUMED D62-codex-eof · at EOF (Pause, Detach, shutdown) the bridge starts no queued message, waits for the running turn (the supervisor interrupted it first) and then closes the app-server's stdin; the supervisor's own escalation (10 s, then signals) applies when it does not exit
- ASSUMED D62-codex-resume-fallback · a thread `thread/resume` refuses (its rollout gone) → a new thread starts and the chat gets an error step "Codex could not reopen its thread <id> (…); a new thread started"; the new id is stored
- ASSUMED D62-attach-warning · Attach here on a Codex / OpenCode session always asks first (liveness unknown: no CLI but Claude Code can say whether a terminal holds its conversation), and imports no terminal turns (marked in the capability matrix)
- ASSUMED D62-codex-files · a Codex file change shows as Write (add) / Edit (update) / Delete with `file_path` (one tool step per changed file, ids `<item>#<n>` when several), so D38's "solution written", artifacts and the activity line read it like Claude Code's
- ASSUMED D62-codex-subagent · a `collabAgentToolCall` `spawnAgent` is an Agent call (one subagent card, its prompt as the description, the agents' final messages as its result); the subagent threads' items carry it as `parent_tool_use_id`
- FOUND D62-preexisting-inbox-labels · `tests/server/inbox/inbox-list.test.ts` › "system items: the kind label…" fails on master too (D55 added `update-available`); untouched here
- ASSUMED D62-opencode-server · each OpenCode session runs its own `opencode serve --hostname 127.0.0.1 --port <free port>` in the session's cwd (one server per session, like one CLI process per Claude session), the port picked by binding port 0 and releasing it just before the spawn (a small race with another program taking it: the server then fails to start and the spawn ends with its stderr); `OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD` is 24 random bytes per server, never stored or logged
- ASSUMED D62-opencode-permissions · Switchboard's rules go into `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` (`edit: allow`, `bash` / `webfetch` / `external_directory`: `ask`), merged over any value the environment already has; this sits above the user's own opencode.json for those four keys (the docs' precedence) · probe 6 checks the merge

## D63 · CLI accounts (2026-10-01)
- VERIFIED D63-claude-config-dir · Claude Code 2.1.285 binary, read-only (`strings`): with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set the macOS Keychain service name gets the suffix `-<first 8 hex of sha256(config dir, NFC)>` (function `wN`; `CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR` overrides the folder hashed), so each config folder holds its own subscription login; transcripts live under `<config dir>/projects/<cwd slug>/<session id>.jsonl`
- VERIFIED D63-claude-auth-cli · `claude auth login [--claudeai (default) | --console] [--email <e>] [--sso]`, `auth logout`, `auth status [--json (default) | --text]` (`--help`, read-only); the login prints "Opening browser to sign in…", "If the browser didn't open, visit: <url>", "Paste code here if prompted > " and, on success, "Login successful." (exit 0); the status JSON has `loggedIn`, `authMethod`, `apiProvider`, `configDirectory`, and for claude.ai `email`, `orgName`, `subscriptionType`; none of them was run against a real login
- VERIFIED D63-claude-limit-text · the binary holds "You've hit your <session | weekly | model> limit · resets …" style texts, an assistant `error: "rate_limit"` kind and `billing_error`; monthly-spend / team-budget / credits messages are different texts (left alone by `parseLimitError`); the exact `result` line of a real limit was not captured (probe 2 in `docs/spike-providers.md`)
- VERIFIED D63-opencode-auth-api · OpenCode `v1.18.34` OpenAPI (`packages/sdk/openapi.json`): `GET /provider/auth`, `POST /provider/{id}/oauth/authorize {method}` → `{url, method: auto | code, instructions}`, `POST /provider/{id}/oauth/callback {method, code?}` → boolean, `PUT /auth/{id}` (`Auth`: oauth / api / wellknown), `DELETE /auth/{id}`, `GET /provider` → `{all, default, connected}`
- ASSUMED D63-default-readonly · the built-in **Default** profile (the developer's own login) can be ordered, enabled / disabled and checked in Settings → Accounts, but is never signed in, signed out, renamed or deleted from the UI: those would change the developer's real login · revert: drop the `builtin` guards in `SignInManager.start/signOut` and `AccountService.update/remove`
- ASSUMED D63-share-default-on · "same settings / instructions / MCP as Default" is **on** by default (a switched session behaves like the one before): symlinks (copy where links are refused) of `settings.json`, `CLAUDE.md`, `agents/`, `commands/`, `skills/`, `keybindings.json` (Claude Code), `config.toml`, `AGENTS.md`, `prompts/`, `skills/` (Codex); Claude Code's user-level MCP servers are **merged** (only `mcpServers` of `<config dir>/.claude.json`, the profile's own win), never the file; credentials, `projects/`, `sessions/` and the account identity never; OpenCode shares its config by itself
- ASSUMED D63-opencode-data · an OpenCode profile overrides only `XDG_DATA_HOME` (its data folder is `<dir>/opencode`: `auth.json`, the SQLite store); `~/.config/opencode` stays shared, `XDG_STATE_HOME` / `XDG_CACHE_HOME` are not overridden
- ASSUMED D63-browser-env · `BROWSER=true` on the login processes so the CLI does not open a second tab beside the one Switchboard opens (best effort: Claude Code reads `process.env.BROWSER` in its remote-attach path; whether its login honors it was not run); a second tab with the same URL is harmless
- ASSUMED D63-codex-login-output · Codex's login output (a URL line after "navigate to this URL to authenticate", the device code `[A-Z0-9]{4,5}-[A-Z0-9]{4,5}`, its local callback on `http://localhost:<port>`) is unverified (Codex was not installed): the sign-in reads the first non-loopback URL and a code pattern · probe 3 in `docs/spike-providers.md`
- ASSUMED D63-codex-resume · a Codex rollout copied to the same `sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/` place under another `CODEX_HOME` resumes with `thread/resume {threadId}`; if that fails the D62 handover inside Codex is used (the app-server's error is not parsed: a resume that fails after the copy shows as an error step) · unverified
- ASSUMED D63-oc-limit · OpenCode reports no plan windows, only the provider's error: a text with `429`, "too many requests", "rate limit(ed)", "insufficient_quota" / "quota exceeded" is a limit (the loosest rule; Claude Code's and Codex's are stricter)
- ASSUMED D63-continue · after a switch that interrupted a turn (a limit error, or a running turn stopped by the switch) the session gets one service message, "Continue where you left off: …", so the turn is picked up; an idle or paused session gets nothing
- ASSUMED D63-reset-zone · a reset time of day in a limit text ("resets 2pm") has no zone (or one in parentheses that is ignored): it is read in this machine's zone, the next such time; a wrong zone only shifts when the spent mark ends
- ASSUMED D63-reset-fallback · without a readable reset the spent mark lasts until the usage reading's reset for that window, else 5 h (session), 24 h (weekly) or 1 h (unknown window)
- ASSUMED D63-defaults · master and per-CLI toggles **on** (adding a second account is the opt-in, and a CLI with a single account is never touched: "dormant"), thresholds on at 98 % / 98 %, after a reset **stay**, new sessions "first with allowance", every account spent **stop and notify**, cooldown 120 s (API only)
- ASSUMED D63-delete · deleting a profile never signs it out and keeps its folder unless asked ("Delete and remove its folder", only inside `<dataDir>/profiles/`); its sessions go back to the Default (a paused Claude Code session whose conversation lives only in the deleted profile's folder cannot resume on the Default: switch it to another account first)

- ASSUMED D63-paste-back · the paste-back of a peer machine's sign-in: a loopback `http(s)` URL (the browser's final redirect) is fetched from that machine, delivering it to the CLI's callback server; anything else is written to the CLI's stdin as the code Claude Code asks for ("Paste code here"), or to OpenCode's `code` callback; the exact format Claude Code takes (the bare code, `code#state`) was not verified
- ASSUMED D63-peer-routes · the account routes are on `PEER_API_ALLOW` (the one place a peer manages another machine's configuration: pairing is the trust, as for the MCP page)
- ASSUMED D63-pick-at-start · a new session takes the rule's profile (`AccountService.pick`: the first enabled, signed-in, not spent profile under the thresholds, else under 100 %, else the Default); a mid-session CLI switch (D62) re-picks for the new CLI and clears the pin
- OPEN D63-history-workflows · History's terminal-conversation listing, Workflow runs' files (D51: the agents of a Workflow on a session running on another profile are not followed) and the hooked-terminal sessions read the Default's Claude Code folder only; transcripts for Attach, the long-message restore, the context meter and History's "continue" are found across every profile folder
- OPEN D63-threshold-readings · threshold switching needs fresh usage readings: the poller reads only while a `/hub` client is connected (D17); `rate_limit_event`s of turns arrive anyway; the limit error always acts
- OPEN D63-codex-usage · a Codex profile's windows are known only while one of its sessions has reported them (bridge `onUsage`), so thresholds for Codex work once a session of it ran; OpenCode has none
- OPEN D63-workflow-notes · the auto-switcher's periodic check is 30 s and runs for live sessions only; a session that hit the limit while paused is not switched until resumed (then its next limit error switches it)
- OPEN D63-running-turn · a manual Switch account or a threshold switch interrupts a running turn (no wait for its end); it is picked up with the continue message
- OPEN D63-real-login · no real login of any CLI was run (developer instruction): every flow is tested against the fakes; `docs/spike-providers.md` → *Accounts (D63)* lists the probes

## Fix · hook waiter expiring (2026-10-02)
- VERIFIED waiter-default-timeout · CLI 2.1.285 binary (`strings`, read-only): in the hook runner `un = e.timeout ? e.timeout * 1000 : Wa` with module constant `Wa = 600000` (10 minutes); an `async` / `asyncRewake` hook is registered with `asyncResponse: { async: true, asyncTimeout: e.timeout ? e.timeout * 1000 : Wa }` and logged "Registering async hook … with timeout ${F}ms" where `F = asyncTimeout || 15000` (the 15000 is only a fallback, never reached); the shell command's timer (`setTimeout(…, timeoutMs)`) kills the process when it fires (status `killed`, removed from the registry); nothing re-arms it before the next SessionStart / Stop
- VERIFIED waiter-timeout-max · the hook schema is `timeout: number().positive().optional()` ("Timeout in seconds for this specific command"): no maximum; the practical ceiling is `setTimeout`'s 2^31-1 ms (about 24.8 days; a larger value would fire at once), 604800 s = 7 days is far below
- ASSUMED waiter-timeout-7d · 7 days (`WAITER_HOOK_TIMEOUT_S`): "unlimited" within the CLI's reach; a waiter killed after 7 days re-arms at the next turn, and the outdated check would not flag it (the entry is current)
- ASSUMED waiter-stop-204 · the explicit stop is HTTP 204 (what hosts answered for superseded / ended already, so old scripts behave as before); shutdown now answers 503 so a restarting host is not a stop; unhooking a session stops its waiter (a re-hook is armed by the session's next turn)
- ASSUMED waiter-backoff · 1 s doubling to 30 s, reset after a connection held 30 s; the script also exits when `CLAUDE_PID` is gone
- ASSUMED waiter-stopped-wording · `waiter-stopped` when a waiter was held, or the hooks reported, since Switchboard started; after a Switchboard restart, with no hook reported yet, a killed waiter still reads "No hook listening yet" (cannot be told apart)
- OPEN waiter-old-script · a machine whose Switchboard is older keeps the old script (no retry) until updated; its hooks then show "installed" there; only an update of that machine fixes it
- VERIFIED waiter-tests · typecheck green; vitest (`--maxWorkers=4`, `SWITCHBOARD_TEST_PORTS=4960-4969`): tests/server/hooks (all 5 files), tests/core/hooks.test.ts, tests/core/hooked-activity.test.ts, tests/web/hooked-activity.test.ts; E2E: peers.spec, hooked-activity.spec · full suites not run (developer instruction)

## D65 · Session take-over (2026-10-04)
- ASSUMED D65-wip-plumbing · the WIP commit is **not** made on the session's branch: a throw-away index (`GIT_INDEX_FILE`) is filled from HEAD, `git add -A` runs there, `git write-tree` + `git commit-tree -p HEAD` write the commit. The ruling's "WIP commit on the session's branch, soft-reset on rollback" ends in the same state on the target; on the source it is strictly safer (HEAD, the branch, the real index and the working tree are never touched, no hooks run, no signing, no identity needed), so the rollback is "delete the temp branches and the remote-tracking ref the push made". Consequence: staged changes arrive unstaged (the WIP tree is flat)
- ASSUMED D65-reset-mixed · the target undoes the WIP commit with `git reset --mixed <base>` (not `--soft`): modified files stay modified, files that were untracked become untracked again; `--soft` would leave everything staged, which is not "exactly as it was" either
- ASSUMED D65-initiator · the machine whose UI started the take-over drives both ends (a peer's session comes **to** it, its own session goes **to** a paired machine); the progress is polled (400 ms), no `/hub` event; a session of a third machine cannot be moved by this one
- ASSUMED D65-remotes · the source pushes to the branch's configured remote, else `origin`, else the only remote; the target finds the remote whose URL **normalises** to the same key (`normalizeRemoteUrl`); a repo without a remote is blocked (there is no shared place to carry the files through); the temp branch is pushed with `+` (the namespace is ours), the real branch never
- ASSUMED D65-hooked-order · for a hooked terminal session the stop comes **right after the capture** (the ruling's literal order: stop after capture), before the transfer: so the transcript and the files are final, but a failure after it cannot start the terminal's `claude` again (the run's undo notes say `claude --resume <id>`); the stop touches only the process `claude agents --json` (asked fresh) lists with that session id, and nothing is stopped when the registry cannot be read
- ASSUMED D65-stop-approach · macOS / Linux: SIGTERM, SIGKILL after 10 s; Windows: `taskkill /PID <pid> /T`, `/F` after 10 s. Claude Code's handling of SIGTERM / taskkill (a clean exit, the transcript flushed) was **not** run against the real CLI (developer instruction: nothing real); a process that ignores it is force-killed after the grace time, losing at most what the CLI had not written yet
- ASSUMED D65-workspace-clone · a workspace session needs a saved workspace folder on the target that holds at least one of its repos; the other repos resolve in it or are cloned into its layout (the first place `solutionCandidates` names), unless the dialog's path says otherwise; no matching workspace at all: blocked ("add the workspace folder first") rather than guessing one
- ASSUMED D65-single-repo-outside · a non-workspace session that works in several repos (more than one worktree outside a workspace) is refused; a session in no git repository (a plain folder) is refused (no way to carry the files); a repo folder or a hooked session's cwd inside a saved workspace is placed in that workspace folder with the repo as its cwd
- ASSUMED D65-clean-target · a main-checkout session needs a **clean** checkout on the target (uncommitted or untracked files: blocked; nothing is stashed); a target branch with commits the source does not have ("diverged"), or a branch checked out in another worktree, refuses; the first is only found at the apply (after the push) and is rolled back
- ASSUMED D65-tombstone · taking a conversation back to a machine that still has its moved-away session (closed, `moved_to` set) replaces that record (the new session imports the chat again); a live session with the same conversation id is a blocker
- ASSUMED D65-conversation · Claude Code: the transcript and its whole `<id>/` folder (subagents, Workflow runs) are copied to the target cwd's project folder and resumed under the **same** id on the account the D63 rules pick there; Codex: its rollout file under `CODEX_HOME` + `thread/resume`, else the D62 handover (chat exported under `<dataDir>/handovers/takeover-<op>/chat.md`, the chat imported as events); OpenCode: the handover. The conversation's first message is a service message with the D44 `resuming` mark; messages that were queued on the source are listed in it instead of re-queued
- ASSUMED D65-limits · 512 KiB per chunk (base64 JSON under the peer API's 1 MiB body limit), sha256 per file verified on the target, 200 MB per file refused up front; transfer is sequential
- ASSUMED D65-leftovers · temp branches are persisted in the settings key `takeover.leftovers` at the push and removed when deleted; after a successful take-over the target deletes them, then the source; what is left shows in the dialog with **Delete it** (and in `GET /api/takeover/leftovers`)
- ASSUMED D65-stale-op · a source side nobody finished (the initiating machine went away) is rolled back by the next call about the session after 30 minutes; staging folders of a crashed target (`<dataDir>/takeover/`) are not swept at start
- ASSUMED D65-moved-state · the source session is closed (not deleted): it leaves the sidebar, History lists it as Closed, its header says **Moved to <machine>** with a link, and **Reopen** is refused (it would fork the conversation)
- ASSUMED D65-validation · everything the other machine sends that reaches git (branch, temp branch, repo key, commit ids, clone URL) is validated first; a clone is `git clone -- <url>` and `ext::` / `fd::` style URLs are refused; the clone path is trusted like `POST /api/sessions`' cwd (pairing is the trust)
- OPEN D65-real-cli · no real CLI was run (developer instruction): `claude --resume <id>` of a transcript copied from another machine (its project slug differs, its `cwd` fields name the old path), a real Codex `thread/resume` of a copied rollout (D63-codex-resume) and SIGTERM on a real terminal `claude` are untested; the fakes follow `docs/fake-claude.md`
- OPEN D65-git-edge · submodules (their pointer changes travel, their own uncommitted work does not), Git LFS (the WIP push goes through LFS filters), `core.autocrlf` differences between Windows and macOS (the WIP tree is what `git add` produced on the source), very large uncommitted binaries, and a source branch without any commit are not handled specially; a detached HEAD is refused (a take-over carries a branch)
- OPEN D65-hooked-late-writes · a hooked terminal that is still typing between the WIP commit and its stop leaves those last edits on the source only (they are in its transcript copy, not in the pushed tree)
- OPEN D65-remote-control · Remote Control (D24) and background tasks / Workflow runs of the source are not carried over: the new process starts without them (a Workflow run's files travel with the conversation folder, its task does not)
- OPEN D65-unpushed-only-here · the pushed temp branch makes the unpushed commits and the uncommitted work visible on the shared remote for the length of the take-over (minutes); a private repo's remote is the only place they pass through
- FOUND D65-preexisting-red · `tests/core/accounts.test.ts` › "limit error: marks the profile spent until its reset and switches to the next one" fails on master cefc7d9 too (checked in a throw-away worktree of that commit): its fixed `NOW` is 2026-10-01 and the reset label becomes "resets 1 oct 14:00" once the real date is a later day, so `/^session limit, resets \d\d:\d\d$/` no longer matches; not touched here
- FOUND D65-load-flakes · in one run of the touched areas with `--maxWorkers=4` on the 10 test ports 4960–4969, `tests/server/peers/stop-turn.test.ts` ("could not bind a free test port") and `proxy.test.ts` (D48-cache-persist, 20 s timeout) failed and `tests/server/accounts/signin.test.ts` hit `ENOTEMPTY` on its temp dir; all passed alone (and signin twice more). The D65 test files take a lock folder in the temp dir (`tests/helpers/takeover.ts`) so only one two-process world runs at a time (a world holds four ports)

## D68 · Session todos (2026-10-04)
- ASSUMED D68-instruction-edited · the todo sentence is part of the **default** standing instruction (D64 has no versioning of the text): a stored text equal to the earlier default (saved unchanged or by Reset to default) reads as the new default and gets it; a text the developer edited is left alone and does not get it (they can add it, or Reset to default). The D64 tests' "short" bound went from 300 to 400 characters to make room (the text is 367).
- ASSUMED D68-token · the agent token is derived (HMAC-SHA256 of the session id under `sb_token`) instead of stored: a resume, restart or take-over needs no bookkeeping, and session A's token never opens session B. It reaches the CLI through a 0600 config file (Claude Code), the environment (Codex `env_vars`, OpenCode `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`), never the argv. The session's own agent can read it (same user, same environment); the scope, not secrecy, is the boundary.
- ASSUMED D68-allowed-tools · Claude Code gets `--allowedTools mcp__switchboard`, so the todo tools never raise a permission request in the Inbox (they only touch Switchboard's own list). Codex / OpenCode approval behaviour for MCP calls is unverified (`docs/spike-providers.md`).
- ASSUMED D68-name · the server is named `switchboard`; a developer's own MCP server with that name would be shadowed by Switchboard's in its sessions (`--mcp-config` servers are loaded alongside the user's; which wins on a name clash was not probed).
- ASSUMED D68-empty-add · with no items the strip is hidden and a small **+ Todo** pill sits just above the composer's top edge at the right, out of the layout flow (no prototype part moves; the visual oracle stays green). A first try in the quick-replies row wrapped the row onto two lines at 1440 px.
- ASSUMED D68-nav-place · **Todos** is the last nav item (after History), as D61 put MCP after the prototype's items, so the prototype's nav parts keep their places; its badge is the neutral count (Inbox's amber is for things waiting on the developer). Not added to the ⌘K palette (MCP is not there either).
- ASSUMED D68-strip-collapsed · the strip starts collapsed (one line: TODO (n) and the first open item) and remembers expanded / collapsed per browser, not per session; Done (n) starts collapsed each time.
- ASSUMED D68-limits · 1–1,000 characters per item, 200 items per session (409 `too-many`), ids 12 hex characters (short enough for an agent to quote).
- ASSUMED D68-closed · closed sessions' lists are kept (they come back on Reopen) but are left out of the Todos page and the nav count; a moved-away session (D65) keeps its copy, the new session gets the list.
- ASSUMED D68-reorder · ↑ ↓ (Settings → Accounts' pattern), no drag; open and done items share one order and move within their own group.
- OPEN D68-codex-opencode · Codex's root `-c` overrides before `app-server`, its `env_vars` forwarding and OpenCode's `mcp` entry in `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` are from their documentation as remembered, not run (neither CLI is installed): probes in `docs/spike-providers.md` → *Todo tools (D68)*.
- OPEN D68-real-tool-call · the real Claude Code probe (2.1.285, Haiku, an isolated `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `HOME`) confirmed the server loads (`connected`, the five `mcp__switchboard__todo_*` tools) but could not make a model call (the isolated folder has no login, and the developer's own config was not to be used), so an end-to-end "add it to the todo list" turn with a real model was not run.
