## D71 · Shared sidebar layout (2026-10-05)
Developer request: drag and drop remote sessions into folders / subfolders and re-order; sync the folder structure peer-to-peer. Rulings: bug = no drop indicator; loose sessions manually orderable; shared two-way layout; always merge on first sync.
- OPEN D71-m1-repro · the report (a paired machine's session dropped on a folder / subfolder: no drop indicator, nothing happens) did **not** reproduce in a fresh two-server world, in Chromium or WebKit: folder head, subfolder, a folder row, Pinned, the ⋯ menu, a busy peer (100 updates), a reconnecting peer, the session open on screen, a re-ordered row mid-drag all worked, and the server takes peer ids. Fixed what was found: (1) WebKit (Safari / its "Add to Dock" app) has no drag auto-scroll for the SESSIONS list and a paired machine's sessions are always listed last, so in a list taller than its box a folder could not be reached from them; the list now scrolls itself at its edge; (2) the peer session cache re-sorted oldest first on every update. **Question for the developer:** "Which browser or app was it (Chrome tab, Chrome app, Safari / Add to Dock), roughly how many sessions were in the list, and was the folder in view when you dropped? If it still happens on this build, a screen recording would pin it down."
- ASSUMED D71-unplaced · the loose list = the unplaced sessions in the service's order (newest first, so a new session shows at the top), then the stored loose order; a loose drop / Move up / down at a position gives the unplaced sessions above that position their places in the order shown (they are then part of the stored order), the ones below stay unplaced · the simplest model that keeps "new sessions on top" and "never placed = service order" · revert: `looseList` / `placeSession` / `arrangeSidebar` in src/core/sidebar-layout.ts
- ASSUMED D71-unpin · Unpin, Out of the folder and the "take out" zone make a session unplaced (top of the loose list, service order), not "where it was" in the loose order · D54's "loose again" read literally · revert: `placeSession` with an index from the UI
- ASSUMED D71-default-off · sharing is off for every pairing, including those made before D71, until switched on in Settings → Machines (as instructed: conservative)
- ASSUMED D71-both-on · a machine merges and answers records only while its own switch for the other is on, so the layout is shared only when both are on; one side on shows "waiting for <machine> to switch it on too" · conservative: switching on one machine never changes the other's layout · revert: `SidebarSync.receive`
- ASSUMED D71-collapsed · the collapsed state stays per machine (not synced); a folder arriving from a peer shows expanded · recommended in the brief
- ASSUMED D71-placement-rule · a session placed differently on the two machines at the first merge: the later placement wins (hybrid clocks); rows written before D71 (migration 0029: no time) are decided by their value, the same on both machines
- ASSUMED D71-same-name · "same-named" = exactly the same (trimmed) name, case-sensitive, at the same parent after merging; the folder with the smaller id stays (both machines pick the same), the other's subfolders and sessions move to its end in their order; combined top-down (inside a combined folder too) · only at the first merge with a machine (later same-named folders are kept apart: names may repeat, D54)
- ASSUMED D71-conflicts · last write wins per item: a folder's name, its place (parent + order) and its deletion each on their own clock, a session's place as one (the brief's "per folder: name / parent / position; per session placement"); a deletion is final (no undelete); a folder whose parent was deleted shows in the nearest live folder above; a session in a deleted folder too (unplaced when none); a session placed in a folder not known here yet shows unplaced until the folder arrives
- ASSUMED D71-transport · one new peer route `POST /peer/v1/sidebar` over the caller's outbound connection (full exchange when switched on and at each (re)connect, the changed records after each change); nothing added to `PEER_API_ALLOW` or `/peer/v1/events` · live changes need both machines' listeners on (each sends over its own connection; otherwise they arrive at the next full exchange), the same as seeing each other's sessions
- ASSUMED D71-relay · a merge that changed something is sent on to the other machines the layout is shared with (A–B–C: A's change reaches C through B); never back to its sender; a merge that changes nothing stops
- ASSUMED D71-forget · forgetting a machine removes its switch; the layout stays; D54's trigger still drops the forgotten machine's own sessions' places (they are no longer listed)
- ASSUMED D71-tombstones · folder tombstones and "unplaced" session rows are kept for good (small) · revert: a later cleanup once every peer has them
- ASSUMED D71-older-peer · an older peer answers 404 (route unknown) → "Update <machine> to sync folders" (`unsupported`); its sessions still go into this machine's folders as before
- VERIFIED D71-webkit · `tests/e2e/sidebar-peers.spec.ts` and `sidebar-scroll.spec.ts` also pass in Playwright's WebKit (run locally with a temporary config; the repo's config stays Chromium-only)

## D72 · Continue a hooked session (2026-10-05)
- ASSUMED D72-liveness · "is the terminal's claude still running?" (`judgeTerminal`): the fresh `claude agents --json` listing the session → running (the stop acts only on that pid, D65); not listed → gone, unless a hook call came < 15 s ago without SessionEnd and the hook's pid is alive or unknown (the registry may lag a session that just started) → unknown; registry unreadable → gone only after SessionEnd or when the hook's reported pid is dead, else unknown. Unknown is refused (409 `terminal-unknown`), never taken as gone.
- ANSWERED D72-pending (developer ruling 2026-10-05: show as not sent) · the mailbox's `hook-message`s (never handed to a waiter) still go to the new process as one message, their bubbles withdrawn; a bubble handed to a waiter that never reached the transcript is NOT re-sent: it gets `notSent: true` and the chat's "Not sent" note with **Resend** (`POST /api/sessions/{id}/events/{eventId}/resend`: queued to the session as a new message, the old bubble withdrawn). Split by text, each mailbox entry matching one bubble (`splitUnseen`).
- ASSUMED D72-gone-no-confirm · a terminal that is gone converts on the first click (no confirmation): the dialog only shows "Continuing…" and closes, opening the session.
- ANSWERED D72-closed (developer ruling 2026-10-05: offer it directly) · a closed (unhooked) hooked session offers Continue in Switchboard (header, History row next to Reopen); continuing reopens it (D33 `reopen`: `closedAt` cleared, the `reopened` line) right before the conversion, after the terminal checks / stop. Only a session taken over elsewhere answers 409 `closed`. ASSUMED D72-closed-failure: a resume that then fails leaves it reopened (still hooked).
- ASSUMED D72-origin · the continued session keeps origin `terminal` (mode line `terminal · moved`, as a D16 move) and its name / title.
- ASSUMED D72-wait · after the stop, at most 15 s for the registry to drop the process; still listed → 502 `stop-failed`, nothing else changed (the stop itself may have happened).
- ASSUMED D72-spawn-failure · a resume that cannot start (folder gone, CLI unavailable) puts the record back as it was (still hooked); the folder is checked before the terminal is stopped.
- ASSUMED D72-history-local · History lists this machine's sessions only (it is not on the peer API), so its row action is for this machine's hooked sessions; a peer's is continued from its header or sidebar row.
- ASSUMED D72-header · the header's action is a link-styled button inside the hooked note (where it says what stays in the terminal), not another header button (keeps the actions row as it is for every visual spec).
- OPEN D72-windows-live · not run against a real Windows terminal: the stop is D65's `taskkill /T` path (`terminal-stop.ts`), the liveness the same `claude agents --json`.
- RESOLVED D72-stale-test · `tests/server/history/continue.test.ts` › "moves a terminal conversation … --resume spawned with no message" expected no `--mcp-config`; it now expects D68's `--mcp-config <dataDir>/agent-mcp/<session id>.json --allowedTools mcp__switchboard` (developer request 2026-10-05).

## D73 · Devices (2026-10-08)
- ASSUMED D73-transport · `tailscale serve --bg --https=<port> http://127.0.0.1:<device port>` to a second loopback listener (shape a); a listener on the Tailscale IP with plain HTTP (shape b) is no secure context (no PWA install / push on iOS) · `docs/devices.md` → *Transport*
- ANSWERED D73-https-port (developer ruling 2026-10-08: keep 8443) · HTTPS port **8443** by default (Tailscale serves HTTPS on 443 / 8443 / 10000 only): leaves a 443 the developer may already serve untouched; 443 / 10000 selectable through `PUT /api/devices/access { httpsPort }` (no UI control yet) · revert: default 443 for a shorter URL
- ASSUMED D73-device-port · device listener default 127.0.0.1:**13003** (next to 13001 / 13002); changeable through the API, no UI control
- ASSUMED D73-serve-host · `tailscale serve` keeps the browser's `Host` (its reverse proxy copies the incoming Host); as a fallback a request with `Host: 127.0.0.1:<device port>` is accepted only when `X-Forwarded-Host` names the devices' origin. UNVERIFIED against a real `tailscaled` (manual checklist step 3)
- ASSUMED D73-serve-own · Switchboard turns off only the serve entry it made (`devices.serve`), refuses to overwrite a port served by something else (`tailscale serve status --json`), and leaves its own entry in place when it stops (re-applied at start; 502 from Tailscale meanwhile)
- ASSUMED D73-serve-consent · the first `tailscale serve` on a tailnet may need Serve enabled in the admin console: the CLI's link is shown ("Open Tailscale"); `--yes` is never passed
- ANSWERED D73-local-only (developer ruling 2026-10-08: an allow-list) · `DEVICE_ALLOWED` in `src/server/devices/local-only.ts`; everything else refused, `DEVICE_REFUSED` lists it; a test fails on an unclassified `/api` route. Now also refused (were allowed under the deny-list): MCP reconnect / check, the default CLI and CLI checks, terminal attach / detach, teleport, solution isolation, codebase-memory reindex
- ANSWERED D73-takeover-local (developer ruling 2026-10-08: stays desktop-only) · taking a session over to / from a paired machine (D65) is local-only for a device (it stops terminals and moves files between machines); a session's account switch and Reconnect stay allowed
- ASSUMED D73-rate-limit · one global limit (10 tries per 10 minutes on the device listener) because every caller is 127.0.0.1 behind `tailscaled`; a burst of wrong codes from anyone also blocks the right one until the window passes
- ASSUMED D73-cookie · `__Host-sb_device`, Max-Age 400 days (Chrome's cap), refreshed on every page load, so a device in use never expires; revoke is the way to end it
- ASSUMED D73-tailscale-login · `Tailscale-User-Login` recorded at pairing and required to match later when present; a device paired from a tagged node (no header) is checked by its credential only
- ANSWERED D73-push-scope (developer ruling 2026-10-08: include peers) · a paired machine's questions, permission requests and finished turns / errors notify this machine's devices through the peer stream (no echo); each item id once (24 h memory), only items created after start and within 10 minutes (a reconnect's backlog is not news); the peer client now refreshes its Inbox cache before passing `inboxChanged` on
- ASSUMED D73-push-fresh · the 10-minute freshness window: a question asked on a peer while it was unreachable for longer than that is not announced when it comes back (it is in the Inbox)
- ASSUMED D73-push-events · default toggles all on (permission, questions, turn finished, session errors, other Inbox items); "turn finished" = status `run` → `idle` / `done`; "errors" = status → `fail`; nothing is sent while device access is off
- ASSUMED D73-push-content · the payload carries the session name and up to 140 characters (the first question, the permission item's title) through the vendor's push service, end-to-end encrypted; `PRIVACY.md` says so
- ASSUMED D73-vapid-sub · VAPID `sub` = the project's GitHub page (no personal address)
- ASSUMED D73-push-crypto · RFC 8291 / 8292 with Node's crypto (no dependency): verified against RFC 8291 appendix A byte for byte and an independent decrypting fake; not yet against Apple / Google (manual checklist)
- ASSUMED D73-ios-separate · an iPhone's Home Screen app has its own cookies, so it is paired separately (the pairing page and docs say so); the Safari tab's pairing remains a separate device until revoked
- ASSUMED D73-pair-page · a self-contained server page (no app bundle) with a strict CSP; it shows this machine's name to whoever on the tailnet opens it
- ASSUMED D73-no-hub-event · no new `/hub` event: Settings → Devices re-reads the list every 2 s while a code waits
- RESOLVED D73-e2e-click · after merging D74 the phone E2E uses real taps (sidebar in the ☰ drawer, Settings list → Devices detail)
- RESOLVED D73-vapid-short-scalar · found by a flaky run after the merge: Node's `getPrivateKey()` returned 31 bytes for ~1/256 keys, which the key-file check then refused (new keys at every start, breaking subscriptions); the scalar is now padded to 32 bytes, with a test over 1500 keys
- OPEN D73-real-tailscale · not run against a real `tailscaled`, Apple or Google push: `docs/devices.md` → *Manual checklist*
- ANSWERED D73-question-https-port · keep 8443
- ANSWERED D73-question-push-peers · yes, include peers
- ANSWERED D73-question-takeover · stays desktop-only
- FLAKY (pre-existing) tests/server/peers › reconnect-world / schedules-loops unreachable-peer tests fail 1–2 per run under `--maxWorkers=2` on the D73 branch and equally on its base without the D73 changes (checked 2026-10-08) · load / port contention; watch for it

## D74 · Responsive layout (2026-10-08)
- ASSUMED D74-desktop-threshold · the desktop layout starts at 1280 px (not 1366): small laptop windows (1280 × 800) keep the prototype's layout; below 1280 the compact shell applies. The old shell had a 1100 px minimum width, so 1100–1279 was not designed for the desktop either. Every visual spec (1440 × 900) passes unchanged.
- ASSUMED D74-tablet-drawer · on tablets (768–1279) the sidebar is a slide-over drawer opened from the same top app bar as on phones (not a docked collapsible column): one pattern for every compact width, and the full width for the page. The D41 rail / handle is a desktop thing; the D41 state on compact layouts is the drawers' (closed at first, never saved), so a tablet never changes the stored desktop choice.
- ASSUMED D74-session-appbar · the session view has no app bar: its header starts with ☰ (with the Inbox count as a badge) so a phone does not spend a second row on the title.
- ASSUMED D74-header-menu-width · the header's actions move into ⋯ below 1024 px (tablet portrait and phones); at 1024–1279 they stay in the row and wrap. The menu holds the same elements (pickers, Close / Reopen, Remote, take-over, Pause / Resume, Continue in terminal / Attach here), not copies.
- ASSUMED D74-close-in-menu · on a touch screen the sidebar row's × (hover only) is hidden and **Close** is added to the row's ⋯ menu (coarse pointers only, so the desktop menu is unchanged).
- ASSUMED D74-settings-phone · on a phone `/settings` (no section) shows the list of sections and `/settings/<section>` the section with ‹ Settings; tablets and the desktop keep the nav beside the section (and `/settings` = Claude Code, as before).
- ASSUMED D74-long-press-numbers · hold 400 ms, slop 8 px (a scroll), tooltip after ~500 ms; no vibration ("haptic-free"); the lifted row's name follows the finger above it.
- ASSUMED D74-drawer-scroll · in the drawer the whole sidebar scrolls as one (the desktop's fixed top and bottom left the sessions too little room with 44 px targets); a held drag near the edge scrolls the sidebar there instead of the list.
- ASSUMED D74-short-landscape · a window ≤ 500 px high (a phone held sideways) hides the session's chips and the quick-reply label so the chat keeps room.
- ASSUMED D74-font-16 · on coarse pointers text fields are at least 16 px, so iOS does not zoom into them on focus.
- OPEN D74-real-devices · tested with Playwright's touch emulation (Chromium with CDP touch input; WebKit at the phone sizes with pointer events), not on a real iPhone, iPad or Android device; the keyboard behavior (`visualViewport`, `interactive-widget`) in particular wants a check on a real phone.

## D75 · Todo progress (2026-10-08)
- ASSUMED D75-touched · "the agent didn't touch it during that turn" = the agent did not change the item through `todo_update`, `todo_done` or `todo_remove` since the turn began; its own `todo_start` does **not** count (the instructions ask it to call `todo_start` first, so counting it would cancel exactly the reminder the ruling wants). The agent's changes are kept in memory: after a restart an unknown turn start counts every earlier change.
- ASSUMED D75-reminder-sources · only items started by ▶ Start or the agent's `todo_start` arm the reminder; an item the developer marks by hand (⋯ → Mark in progress, nothing sent) does not (the agent may never have heard of it). The agent's `todo_start` on such an item makes it the agent's start, so the reminder then applies.
- ASSUMED D75-turn-end · a turn ends when the session's status goes from `run` to `idle` or `done` (the D73 push notifier's "turn finished"); `run` → `need` → `run` is the same turn; a failed turn (`fail`) sends no reminder.
- ASSUMED D75-one-per-item · several unfinished items at one turn end get one reminder message each (the ruling's text names one item), sent in list order; each is recorded once delivered.
- ASSUMED D75-hooked-no-waiter · a hooked session without a held waiter gets no reminder and nothing is recorded, so the next turn end with a waiter may still send it (once).
- ASSUMED D75-restart-again · ▶ Start on an item already in progress sends the message again and counts as a new start (its time is renewed, its reminder re-armed); ▶ Start on a done item is refused (422: reopen it first); the button is not shown on done cards.
- ASSUMED D75-send-first-state · the item goes in progress before the message is sent (so a turn that ends at once already sees it started); a refused send puts it back exactly as it was.
- ASSUMED D75-older-peer-start · ▶ Start on a paired machine before D75 (403 / 404 for the route) sends the start message through `POST …/messages` and leaves the item open there (it has no third state). A paired machine before D75 shows our in-progress items as its UI can (it knows open / done only); nothing to do on this side.
- ASSUMED D75-counts · `openCount` / `openTodoCount` / the Todos nav keep meaning "not done" (open + in progress, per the ruling's sidebar count); `inProgressCount` is added; the header's `open` part counts the not-started ones only, as in the ruling's example, and the `in progress` part is left out when none is.
- ASSUMED D75-toast · the Todos page's ▶ Start toast is the existing toast (title **Sent to the agent**, the item and session, **Open**, **Later**), not auto-dismissed, like every toast.
- ASSUMED D75-strip-collapsed · a strip opened through **+ Todo** is not remembered as expanded (unchanged D68 behavior); the e2e opens it again after the Todos page's **Open**.
- VERIFIED D75-esc-menu · Esc in a card's ⋯ menu used to stop the running turn too (D50's capture-phase Esc did not count a `role="menu"`); fixed, covered in tests/e2e/todos.spec.ts.

## D76–D78 · Todo runs, review, board, actuals (2026-10-08)
- ANSWERED D76-workspace-in-place · ruling 2026-10-08: a workspace source's run gets a `todo/<slug>` worktree in each solution repo it uses (its `solutions`), each cut from that repo's current branch, like a Full start with worktrees per solution; in place (with the note) only when it uses no solution repo. Built: a source with router answers starts a Full run with the same answers (the session-start block lists the worktrees); a simple source a simple run whose first message adds a worktree note.
- ASSUMED D76-workspace-from · a solution's "current branch" for the source = the branch checked out in the source's own worktree of that repo, else in the main checkout (detached: the commit); a solution that does not resolve to a repo (read-only, gone) is left out of the run.
- ASSUMED D76-simple-start · the run is a D56 **simple** start (no router answers block, no D32 ticket rule for `todo/<slug>`), so its first message is the item's start message (plus the worktree note in a repo folder).
- ASSUMED D76-from-branch · "the source session's current branch" = what `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` says in the source's cwd (its worktree, else the repo); a detached HEAD cuts from that commit; an unreadable one runs in place with a note.
- ASSUMED D76-model-choice · a run's model / effort are the source's even when the model list changed meanwhile (a model no longer listed is refused like `POST /api/sessions` would); the run does not become the New-session form's last choice.
- ASSUMED D76-review-active-only · done → review only while the item's run is **active**; the source session's own agent marking it done also gives review; review → done (tick / Done) is done; a discarded run's item is done directly.
- ASSUMED D76-discarded · discarded keeps `runSessionId` (history, "Run (discarded)") with `runState: discarded`; the item can run again (a new run replaces the link).
- ASSUMED D76-review-counts · review is neither open nor done: not in the sidebar's ☐ / `openCount`, not removed by the done hour; `reviewCount` added; the strip and the list show review items after the open ones.
- ANSWERED D76-reminder · confirmed 2026-10-08 · the D75 finish reminder for a run item goes to the run session (its agent works on it), never to the source session.
- ASSUMED D76-already-running · a second run of an item whose run session is open and active is refused (409 `already-running`); once the run session is closed it may run again.
- ASSUMED D76-takeover · a take-over carries an item in review as done and a run's start as the developer's (the run session stays on the old machine).
- ASSUMED D76-linked-answer · the run agent's `PUT` on its linked item answers the item and the run session's own list (never the source's list), and `todo_list` shows the linked item under its own heading.
- ASSUMED D77-drag-no-message · dragging to In progress never sends the start message (▶ Start does); dragging to Done skips review (`skipReview`).
- ANSWERED D77-phone-picker · ruling 2026-10-08: swipeable columns (scroll snap, one per screen width) with a tab strip that follows the swipe and can be tapped; ⋯ → Move to, plus a long-press drag onto a tab (built: simple with the existing drag). Tablets and short windows scroll the whole board.
- ASSUMED D77-phone-menu · on the swipeable board a card's ⋯ menu is placed fixed in the window (the strip scrolls sideways, which clips it both ways) and closes when a scroll moves its ⋯.
- ASSUMED D77-order · within a column: priority, then the sessions in the page's order (most recently active first), then the session's position; no reordering by drag inside a column (Move up / down stays in ⋯).
- ANSWERED D78-tokens · confirmed 2026-10-08 (cache reads stay out) · tokens per turn = `usage.input_tokens + cache_creation_input_tokens + output_tokens` of the turn's result line (cache reads left out: they re-count the whole context on every call); a turn counts when it **ended** inside an in-progress span (a turn begun earlier counts whole); Codex / OpenCode results carry no `usage` there yet, so their tokens are unknown (shown without tokens).
- ASSUMED D78-history · `todo_actuals` keeps completed items' records (also after the done hour and the session), for the totals and the calibration; reopening deletes the record until the item completes again.
- ASSUMED D78-calibration · the ratio of the summed actual time to the summed estimates over the last 10 completed estimated items (this session's when it has ≥ 3, else the folder's); within 1.15× either way "about right"; the line comes on `todo_add` / `todo_update` answers and at the end of `todo_list`.
- FIXED D77-touch-title · a touch phone's card row reserved room for a 15 px box and a 32 px ⋯ while touch makes them 22 / 44 px, so ⋯ wrapped under the title (strip and board); the title's basis now accounts for that on coarse pointers.
- FIXED hub-listener-test · `tests/server/hub/hub.test.ts` › "a client that goes away is dropped" assumed the hub is the bus's only listener; it failed on master since D75's reminder (2 ≠ 1); it now counts the app's own listeners (reminder, review link).
- OPEN D76-real-cli · run sessions verified with fake-claude only; the linked-item token scope and the reminder have not been seen with a real Claude Code / Codex / OpenCode.

## D79 · Review queue (lane B, 2026-10-08)
- ASSUMED D79-one-card-per-session · one card per session covers all its repos with changes (a multi-repo session's Merge checks every repo before merging any; Commit commits each repo with changes); not one card per repo.
- ASSUMED D79-mode · a session with live worktrees is reviewed as `branch` (only its worktrees); without, as `folder` (its solutions resolved in its folder, else the repo its cwd is in). A session with both a worktree and in-place solutions shows only the worktrees.
- ASSUMED D79-folder-commits · "commits on its branch not merged into its base" for a session working in a folder = commits made since the session started (from one second before, commit times are whole seconds) that are on no remote (`git log --since … HEAD --not --remotes`). Pushed commits do not count.
- ASSUMED D79-fingerprint · the change set = each changed repo's HEAD + `git diff --binary HEAD` + its untracked files' names and blobs; a base that moves on does not count as a new change set. After an action the card keeps the change set as it is after the action, so e.g. a Commit does not raise a card for its own commit at the next turn end.
- ASSUMED D79-refresh · a pending card is read from git again when `GET /api/reviews` lists it (the Inbox reads it when one is picked; the header reads it with the session) and before every action; a card whose changes are gone is resolved `dismissed` as *Handled by the agent* and emits `reviewResolved` `dismissed` (see D79-gone-outcome).
- RESOLVED D79-gone-outcome · ruling 2026-10-08: it counts as done: the card closes as **Handled by the agent** (`handledByAgent: true` in its data, a distinct label, not "Dismissed") and `reviewResolved` is emitted with outcome `dismissed` (contract unchanged; lane A maps dismissed → todo done).
- ASSUMED D79-merge-uncommitted · Merge is refused while the worktree has uncommitted changes (they would not be part of the merge); the message says to send it back or commit them.
- ASSUMED D79-merge-how · where the base is checked out (usually the main checkout, which must have no uncommitted tracked changes) `git merge --no-edit` runs there (fast-forward when possible, hooks run, aborted on failure); elsewhere the ref is moved with `update-ref <new> <old>` (a fast-forward, or a merge commit `Merge branch '<b>' into <base>` from `git merge-tree --write-tree`; this path runs no hooks). A base that exists only as `origin/<b>` gets its local branch created by Merge.
- ASSUMED D79-discard-branch · "Discard … resets the worktree and deletes the branch": a branch checked out in its worktree cannot be deleted while the worktree exists, so Discard resets the worktree to its base (`reset --hard` + `clean -fd`, ignored files kept) and the branch is deleted by Clean up together with the worktree.
- ASSUMED D79-discard-folder · a folder session's Discard reverts only the card's uncommitted files (tracked restored from HEAD, new ones deleted); commits stay.
- ASSUMED D79-open-pr · Open PR pushes the branch with `git push -u origin <branch>` (never forced; refused without an `origin` remote), reuses an open PR for the branch, else `gh pr create --base <base> --head <branch>` titled with the session's title and the agent's summary as the body. The card stays pending with the PR link; Open PR is not offered again once every repo has one. It is not a resolution (no `reviewResolved`).
- ASSUMED D79-cleanup · Clean up is refused while the session's turn runs, while the worktree has uncommitted changes again, or when the branch has commits not in its base; it then runs `git worktree remove` (never forced) and `git branch -D` (safe: contained in the base, checked first) and marks the worktree row removed. Dismiss on a clean-up card keeps the worktree. Neither emits `reviewResolved` again.
- ASSUMED D79-send-back · the message is `Review: your changes were sent back with this comment:` + a blank line + the comment, sent as the developer's message (origin `user`; the normal path: queued while busy, a paused session resumed, a hooked session's mailbox).
- ASSUMED D79-tests · test-like = a test runner or a `test` script (`npm test`, `npm run test:*`, `npx vitest|jest|playwright test`, `pytest`, `go|cargo|dotnet test`, `make test|check`, …); the exit code is read from Claude Code's `Exit code <n>` error result (a non-error result is 0); a call without a result does not count.
- ASSUMED D79-commit-draft · the drafted message = the summary's first non-empty line without Markdown marks (≤ 72 characters) + a blank line + the rest of the summary (≤ 1,500 characters); without a summary `Changes from <session>`.
- ASSUMED D79-badge-place · the header badge leads the chips row (not the top row), so a long title and the header's actions keep their room; in the D74 *short* layout (chips hidden) the Inbox still has the card.
- ASSUMED D79-device-ui · on a paired device the card does not offer Discard / Clean up (the server refuses them anyway, 403 `local-only`).
- ASSUMED D79-push · the new push toggle `review` is on by default (stored toggles without it read as on, like D73's defaults); a card's later Clean up state does not notify again (same item).
- VERIFIED D79-hub-test · `tests/server/hub/hub.test.ts` › "a client that goes away is dropped" was already red on master (D75's reminder subscribes to the bus: 2 listeners, the test expected 1); it now counts the app's own subscribers first and checks the hub adds and removes exactly one.

## D80 · Undo a turn (2026-10-08)
- ASSUMED D80-which-turns · every message that goes to the process starts a turn and gets a checkpoint (the task, typed messages, service messages such as the todo reminder, "Continue."); the turn number is the message's place among the session's user messages (`events.payload.type = user`), so a message withdrawn by a Stop leaves a gap in the numbers. The chat's action is on every user bubble; one without a checkpoint is disabled with the reason.
- ASSUMED D80-trees · the working trees a session "uses" = its cwd's working tree, its worktrees, and (as the session diff does) the solutions of a workspace session it works on in place; a workspace folder that is no repo itself is covered only through those.
- ASSUMED D80-queued · a message written while a turn runs is checkpointed when it is sent (D44 queue), not when the agent takes it up; the running turn's later edits are inside that checkpoint. The action is hidden on a bubble still queued.
- ASSUMED D80-index · a revert also restores the staged state of the checkpoint (`index_tree`) when the branch was restored or never moved; with **Revert files only** the index is set to today's HEAD, so the reverted files show as unstaged changes. (The ruling only forbids touching the index while checkpointing.)
- ASSUMED D80-branch-rules · "not pushed" = none of the commits since the checkpoint is reachable from any remote-tracking ref (`rev-list --not --remotes`); a rewritten history (the recorded HEAD no longer an ancestor), a branch switch, a detached HEAD that differs, or a repo with no commit before the turn also refuse the branch move (files only offered). A detached HEAD that only moved forward is moved back like a branch.
- ASSUMED D80-redo · Redo exists for the newest revert only, until a message is sent after it (then the next turn's own checkpoint is the way back); Redo moves the branch forward again when today's HEAD is an ancestor of the one before the revert, else files only, without asking. Redo's own safety capture is kept (pruned like the others) but not offered as a second Redo.
- ASSUMED D80-note · the note goes into the outbox (`pending_messages`, kind `checkpoint-note`) and is prepended to the next message, as the restart note is; so the next user bubble shows it above what you typed. Redo withdraws it if it was not delivered yet, else queues a short "undid the revert" note.
- ANSWERED D80-close · (ruling 2026-10-08, D80-q2) a closed session keeps its checkpoints for the normal retention, so Reopen + revert works; only a deleted session's (at the next hourly prune: there is no delete event) and retention remove them.
- ASSUMED D80-safety-retention · safety checkpoints (before a revert / a Redo) follow the 7-day rule and are capped at the newest 20 per session.
- ASSUMED D80-header · (after ruling D80-q1) *Undo last turn* is always in the sidebar row's ⋯ menu once the session has a user turn: enabled for the newest turn's checkpoint, else disabled with the reason as its tooltip. The session header shows it only when it is usable (inline on wide screens, in the header's ⋯ menu below 1024 px): a disabled header button moved the prototype's Pause / Continue-in-terminal boxes in the visual oracle's header spec (demo sessions have no checkpoints), so per the ruling it went to the ⋯ menu (the sidebar row's, which exists on every width). Closed sessions show none (Reopen first). **Superseded** by the ruling of 2026-10-08 (*Integration 1.13* → int-header-overflow): never in the header row; the compact header's ⋯ menu shows it disabled with the reason too.
- ASSUMED D80-hooked · hooked terminal sessions are recorded as not supported: their `UserPromptSubmit` hook is `async` (D48 P4: hooks never delay the terminal), so a snapshot from it would race the turn's first edits. Making it synchronous would delay every prompt in the terminal; not done without a ruling.
- ASSUMED D80-gc · checkpoint commits keep the agent's commits reachable for as long as the ref lives (≤ 7 days), so `git gc` keeps them that long.
- ANSWERED D80-q1 · (ruling 2026-10-08) show *Undo last turn* disabled with the reason when the newest turn has no checkpoint; hidden only while the session has no user turn. Done as described under D80-header.
- ANSWERED D80-q2 · (ruling 2026-10-08) keep a closed session's checkpoints for the normal retention. Done; the take-over scenario test's snapshot is back to master's (it only differed because close deleted the refs).
- VERIFIED D80-takeover-transcript · `tests/server/takeover/conversation.test.ts` "byte for byte" failed on every run with D80 and passed on master. Evidence: on master with a 1.5 s pause before the comparison the target transcript is ~5 KB longer than the source (the take-over's first message, `This session moved from …`, that `supervisor.takeOver` sends to the resumed session, appended by its CLI); master with an artificial 150 ms delay in `#send` (no D80 code) fails the same way; D80 with `sessions.checkpoints` off on the target passes 3/3; D80 with `record` made fire-and-forget still fails (the delay is the capture before the write, ~150 ms of git on the target repo). So it is a race in the test (the whole-file compare only passed while the read beat the CLI), not a change in what is appended. The assertion now checks that the target starts with the source's bytes.

## D81 · Quick capture (2026-10-08)
- ASSUMED D81-idle · "idle" = the session's status is `idle` or `done` (not `run`, not `need`); `paused`, `fail`, closed and detached sessions are not asked. A `done` session without a live process is resumed by the message (as any message to it would be); the capture itself is what the developer asked for.
- ASSUMED D81-ask-once · each captured item is asked about at most once (`enrich_asked_at`); if the agent answers without calling `todo_update`, the card keeps "✎ waiting for the agent to fill in" and nobody is asked again (an Edit of the plan, description, priority or estimate clears it).
- ASSUMED D81-developer-edit · the developer's own edit of the description, plan, priority or estimate clears the mark too (they filled it in themselves, and an agent asked later would overwrite it); a title-only edit keeps it. The ruling names only the agent's `todo_update`.
- ASSUMED D81-open-only · only open items are asked about and show the note; an item started (▶ Start, `todo_start`, ⋯ → Mark in progress) or done before the agent was asked is not asked about (its mark stays stored and shows again if it is reopened).
- ASSUMED D81-setting-at-capture · the setting is read when an item is captured (off: stored unmarked, never asked) and again before each ask (off: nobody is asked; turning it on later asks for the items marked while it was on).
- ASSUMED D81-title-clip · a selection's generated title is clipped to 80 characters at a word (with `…`); the palette's text keeps up to 120 characters (the title limit) and, when cut, the whole text becomes the note; a share's title is the shared title, else the text's first line, else the link (120).
- ASSUMED D81-palette-rows · in a session, `todo <text>` lists this session first and the other open sessions after it (so it can still go elsewhere); outside a session it lists the open sessions, recent first (this machine's and the paired machines'); at most 10 rows.
- ASSUMED D81-selection-scope · the selection action is offered in the main conversation only (not in a subagent's chat, the timeline or the composer), and not for an unreachable machine's session.
- ASSUMED D81-share-ios · iOS Safari has no Web Share Target: no Switchboard entry in the iPhone / iPad share sheet (documented in `docs/devices.md`); nothing else to do in a web app.
- UNVERIFIED D81-share-origin · on a real Android phone the share-target POST is answered by the service worker (no request reaches the server); whether Chrome's POST would carry an `Origin` the device guard accepts (when the worker is not active) is not checked here (no real phone; manual checklist step 9 in `docs/devices.md`).
- ASSUMED D81-takeover · a take-over (D65) carries the captured item without its mark (it arrives bare on the new session, like an item from an older source); a small follow-up if wanted.
- ASSUMED D81-older-peer · capturing into a paired machine before D81 (no route: 403 / 404 without `not-found`) adds the item the plain way, bare and unmarked (its agent is not asked).
- ASSUMED D81-dev-db-order · 0035 is applied by version set, not by maximum: a development database that ran this branch's 0035 before lane A's 0032 would get 0032's table rebuild afterwards, which (if it does not carry the three columns) drops them. Released databases run 0032 before 0035; only a lane's own test database could see it.

## D82 · Model by task (2026-10-08)
- ASSUMED D82-cli-required · a rule that sets a model, an effort or an account must also set the CLI: the ruling validates "the model must exist for that CLI" and "the profile must be enabled for that CLI", which needs the CLI known when the rule is saved (the normal choice's CLI is not known then). The editor enables the model / effort / account selects only once a CLI is picked.
- ASSUMED D82-cli-change · a rule that moves a todo to another CLI starts from that CLI's defaults (model, effort and account `null`: the CLI default and the Settings → Accounts rule) before its own targets apply; the normal choice's model and account belong to the first CLI.
- ASSUMED D82-effort-drop · a rule that changes the model without setting an effort drops the effort to the CLI's default: the normal choice's effort may not exist for the new model (e.g. `xhigh` on Haiku). The same model keeps it.
- ASSUMED D82-unknown-list · while a CLI has not reported its model list, a rule may name the models the New-session form offers then (Claude Code: `default`, `opus`, `sonnet`, `haiku`; Codex CLI / OpenCode: `default`), and any of the CLI's effort levels (`low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`), as D31 / D42 check a start then.
- ASSUMED D82-stale-targets · rules are checked when saved, not when they apply: a profile disabled or a model gone later is passed on as it is (the start refuses it or falls back the way it would for any start with that choice). The editor then shows the stored value as an extra option and Save re-checks it.
- ASSUMED D82-explanation · the line reads `Routed by rule: <match> → <targets>`: the match is the priority (`any priority` when any) and the estimate (`≤30 min`, `>120 min`, `no estimate`; nothing for any; `any task` for a rule that matches everything); the targets are the CLI, the model's label, `effort <level>` and the account's name, joined with ` · `; a Claude Code rule that sets a model names only the model (`→ Sonnet`, the ruling's example).
- ASSUMED D82-no-peer · the rules are this machine's (settings are not on the peer API); a todo of a paired machine's session is routed by that machine's rules when lane A's run starts it there.
- OPEN D82-wiring · applying the rules to a todo run is lane A's `todoRunOptions` (`src/server/todos/run-options.ts`), which the orchestrator wires after both lanes merge; until then nothing in this lane starts a todo session, so the rules are stored and explained but not yet applied.

## D83 · Fresh session (2026-10-08)
- ASSUMED D83-bar-place · "a bar above the composer" = its own row between the todo strip and the composer (the composer's first row stays the context bar, D49); an empty todo list's "+ Todo" (which sits over the row above the composer) keeps clear of its buttons.
- ASSUMED D83-snooze-local · **Not now** is remembered per browser (`localStorage`, session id → percent), not stored by the service: it is a viewing preference like the usage-warning toasts; it is forgotten once the context drops below the threshold (a compaction), so the next fill offers it at the threshold again.
- ASSUMED D83-menu · "the session ⋯ menu" = the sidebar row's ⋯ menu (the header's actions sit in a ⋯ menu only below 1024 px); it offers the action at any percent, disabled with the reason for a hooked session, while a turn runs or a switch runs; hidden for a closed or moved session.
- ASSUMED D83-handover-fails · a handover that fails (the CLI ends, a usage limit, no reply within 10 min) does not fall back to an exported chat as the D62 switch does: nothing new is started, the old session stays open and as it was, and its chat says why ("Could not continue in a fresh session: …"); the developer can try again or close it.
- ASSUMED D83-names · the fresh session is named `<name>-<n>` and titled `<title> (<n>)` (n = 2, then 3 for a continued one's continuation; the next free name), so the sidebar, History and the links tell them apart; the task text is kept.
- ASSUMED D83-carried · carried over: folder, cwd (worktree), branch and branching, CLI, model, effort, account profile and its pin (`profilePinned`), solutions, work type / mode / phase / coordination / QA fields, ultracode, permission mode; the old session's worktrees become the fresh one's (its Diff tab). Not carried: Remote Control (off), the meter (new conversation), attachments, schedules.
- ASSUMED D83-header · the old (closed) session's header says "Continued in <title>" with **Open the fresh session**; the fresh session's way back is its first chat divider (no header note, to keep a working session's header unchanged).
- ASSUMED D83-reopen · a continued (closed) session can still be reopened from History (its conversation is its own); its todo list stays with the fresh session.
- ASSUMED D83-restart · a continuation running when Switchboard stops ends with it (like a CLI switch); nothing is kept of a half-started fresh session.
- ASSUMED D83-peer-place · for a paired machine's session the fresh session takes the old one's place in **this** machine's sidebar (where it was placed by its remote id); with the D71 shared layout the machine that ran it moves it there too, and the second move finds it placed already.

## Integration 1.13 (2026-10-08)
- ANSWERED int-routing-place · confirmed by the developer 2026-10-08 · the routed run's line ("Routed by rule: …") is shown in the Run toast (after the item's title and any worktree note), not in the run session's chat; the session's own model chip shows the routed model.
- ANSWERED int-routing-labels · confirmed by the developer 2026-10-08 · the server names models with the routed CLI's last reported list (else Claude Code's aliases) and profiles by name, like the editor's preview.
- ANSWERED int-fresh-actuals · confirmed by the developer 2026-10-08 · a fresh continuation moves the old list's `todo_actuals` history too (totals and the "this session" calibration continue in the fresh session). Tokens of an in-progress span are summed from the working session at the span's end, so turns of the closed old session in that span are not counted (approximate, as documented).
- ANSWERED int-fresh-run · confirmed by the developer 2026-10-08 · a run session continued in a fresh one hands its run over: the item's `runSessionId` becomes the fresh session (its review resolves the item, the D75 reminder goes there) and the fresh session takes its `todoLink`; the old one keeps its link as history.
- VERIFIED int-review-loop · tests/server/todos/run-integration.test.ts: run → agent done → review → merge / dismiss / discard / send back / changes gone / commit, through the real route, fake-claude, git, ReviewService and TodoReviewLink.
- FIXED int-checkpoint-send · lane C awaited the checkpoint row after writing each message, which made a scheduled Run now answer `ok` (tests/server/peers/schedules-loops.test.ts failed on feature/undo alone); stored in the background now, readers wait for it.
- FIXED int-stale-e2e · lane C's E2E fallout (each fails on feature/undo alone, passes on master): walkthrough.spec.ts waits for the agent's write (the status was already done before the turn and D80's checkpoint goes first); walkthrough-repo.spec.ts accepts the agent card's `app-repo/` (D14's placement after its write; the check used to win the race against that update).
- FIXED int-inbox-tests · lane B's E2E fallout (fails on feature/review alone with the file's earlier tests): tests/e2e/close-sessions.spec.ts and own-answers.spec.ts turn Review cards off for their server (they count questions only); tests/e2e/peers.spec.ts picks the permission item by its kind and waits for no permission left in A's Inbox (A's Review card is listed and forwarded too).
- FLAKY int-todo-run-count · tests/e2e/todos.spec.ts:546 (D76 Run) failed once in the full run and once alone in six runs: "~30m+" (an item saved without its estimate while the form is filled fast); passes otherwise. Not changed.
- ASSUMED D74-header-fit · (developer bug report) the header's actions fit the header's own width; the root path may shrink to nothing before anything moves; the ⋯ holder is 30 px; Move shortens before anything moves; the account picker goes before the CLI picker, Pause before the model picker. The paired-machines read is cached across mounts so the take-over action does not blink when it moves into ⋯. tests/e2e/takeover.spec.ts now expects "Move ▸" at 1440 with both panes open (the full label at 1920).
- ANSWERED int-header-overflow · ruling 2026-10-08: *Undo last turn* is not in the header row at all; it is in the sidebar row's ⋯ menu and the compact header's ⋯ menu (below 1024 px), disabled with the reason in both, plus ↶ Revert on each message. No new desktop header button. The header order checks in tests/e2e/model-effort.spec.ts / remote-control.spec.ts are master's again.
- FIXED int-stale-tests · tests/server/todos/capture.test.ts (0035 after 0032–0034, not straight after 0031), tests/server/peers/fresh-session.test.ts (waits for the close, which follows the first send and its checkpoint).

## D84 · Clean-up (2026-10-08)
- ASSUMED D84-where · Settings → Clean-up (last section), not a sidebar entry: the ruling allowed either; it is occasional.
- ASSUMED D84-adopted · worktrees the agent of a Switchboard session created and Switchboard adopted (D38) count as Switchboard's (they have a `worktrees` row); the developer's own hand-made worktrees have none and are never listed.
- ASSUMED D84-marker · "marked as Switchboard-created" is a new settings value `cleanup.createdBranches` written by the worktree manager on its own `git worktree add -b` (no migration). Branches from before this version are recognized by `session/` / `todo/` naming plus a `worktrees` row; older ticket-named ones per D84-old-task-branches below.
- ASSUMED D84-session-data · a branch "recorded in session data" means named in a `worktrees` row (which survives the session's deletion); `sessions.branch` alone names no repo, so it is not used.
- ASSUMED D84-open-session · an open (not closed) session's worktree is listed only when its PR is `MERGED` (ancestry alone would list every fresh worktree); a running session's worktrees and the session itself are never listed.
- ASSUMED D84-stale · "no changes for 14 days" = the newest of the last commit, uncommitted files' modification times, the session's last activity and its closing.
- ASSUMED D84-remote-read · remote branches are read from the remote-tracking refs of the last fetch (a scan never touches the network); the delete is pinned to the previewed sha with `--force-with-lease`, so a branch that moved on the remote is refused.
- ASSUMED D84-data · "previews / orphaned data files" = attachment rows past 30 days (normally already pruned at start), files in `attachments/` no row owns, folders of deleted sessions, `handovers/` exports untouched for 30 days, `takeover/` staging folders of no running operation untouched for a day. Nothing else in the data folder is touched.
- ASSUMED D84-limit-setting · the closed-session limit is its own key (`cleanup.closedSessionDays`, `PUT /api/cleanup/settings`), not part of `PUT /api/settings` (keeps it off the device-allowed settings write).
- ASSUMED D84-peers · this machine only (the ruling's fallback): proxying destructive clean-up to a paired machine was judged too costly for a rare task.
- ASSUMED D84-run-memory · runs are kept in memory (last ten); a restart during a run loses its progress view (each finished step already happened).
- ANSWERED D84-old-task-branches · developer ruling 2026-10-08: "list them if merged". A branch with neither Switchboard naming nor a created record is listed only when a `worktrees` row (live or removed) names it and it is merged into its base (ancestor of the base, or PR merged with every commit on a remote, as for other branches); unticked by default; its remote copy is never listed. An unmerged one, or one no row names, is never listed (tests/server/cleanup/service.test.ts → *older ticket-named task branches*).

## D85 · Tutorial (2026-10-08)
- ASSUMED D85-section · the replay lives in a new **Settings → Tutorial** section (after Updates): there is no "General" section, and adding rows to Claude Code would move what the visual oracle measures there.
- ASSUMED D85-existing · "an existing install" = the database had a session, a schedule, a saved folder or a finished setup when migration 0037 ran; decided once, in the migration, so it does not depend on when the UI first loads. Such an install starts from 1.12.0 (the last version without the tutorial) and gets every What's-new mini-tour after it (D76–D84), not the main tour (still replayable).
- ASSUMED D85-new-no-whats-new · a brand-new install gets only the main tour; the What's-new tours of the features already in its first version are not queued (Settings lists them as "not shown on this machine", each replayable).
- ASSUMED D85-replay · a replay (Settings, ⌘K) records nothing; only a tour that opened by itself is stored as completed or skipped.
- ASSUMED D85-esc · Esc = Skip tour (the current tour only); in a chain of What's-new tours the next one starts, and a **Skip all** button ends the chain (all remaining stored as skipped).
- ASSUMED D85-explain-only · clicks on the page are blocked while a tour runs (a transparent layer); the developer acts after the tour.
- ASSUMED D85-route · steps navigate (the newest open session, Todos, Inbox, a Settings section) and the tour returns to the page, drawer state and focus it started from.
- ASSUMED D85-version · the D76–D84 mini-tours carry version 1.13.0 while `package.json` still says 1.12.0 on this branch; the stored last version is the newer of the two, so nothing is offered twice when the bump lands.
- ASSUMED D85-undo-anchor · the Undo-a-turn step points at the per-message ↶ revert (`data-tour="undo-turn"`), never at a header button (the integration branch moves *Undo last turn* into the ⋯ menus); with no turn yet it is a centred card naming both.
- ASSUMED D85-phone-copy · the phone variant is the layout (drawer opened, bottom sheet), not different step texts.

## D87 · Quiet pushes (2026-10-08)
- ASSUMED D87-visible-not-focused · "in front" = the page is visible (`visibilityState`); focus is recorded but not required, on the server and in the service worker. A visible but unfocused page (a tablet's split screen, a desktop window behind another) can show the toast, so it gets no system notification.
- ASSUMED D87-toast-device-only · the new `notice` toasts show only on a paired device's pages, per that device's toggles (the defaults while it has no subscription); this machine's own UI keeps its toasts as they were (questions, usage warnings).
- ASSUMED D87-questions · a `questions` notice raises no extra toast: the M3.4 question toast (`questionBatch`, the paired machines' batches too) already covers question batches on every page.
- ASSUMED D87-test-push · the test notification (Settings → Devices → Send test) is always sent and always shown, even with the page open: it is asked for from the open page.
- ASSUMED D87-webkit · on WebKit (Safari, every iOS / iPadOS browser) the service worker always shows a push: WebKit may revoke a subscription whose pushes show nothing. The server's suppression applies there as everywhere.
- ASSUMED D87-no-whats-new · a bug fix, not a new feature: no What's-new mini-tour (`WHATS_NEW` untouched, it is the tutorial lane's file).
- UNVERIFIED D87-webapk · Chromium skips the userVisibleOnly notification while a *tab* of the origin is visible (`PushMessagingNotificationManager::EnforceUserVisibleOnlyRequirements`); not verified on a real Android phone whether an installed app's (WebAPK) window counts. Only the rare in-between push reaches the service worker while the app is open (the server already skips devices in front). Manual check: `docs/devices.md` → step 10.

- VERIFIED stabilize-stale-tests · stale tests, code as decided: `tests/core/stop-turn.test.ts` (D57: withdrawn entries carry `attachments`), `tests/server/inbox/inbox-list.test.ts` (D55: `update-available` label), `tests/web/model-picker.test.ts` (long messages: chat items carry `cut`), `tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts` (D62: `/api/clis` is a real route, no longer 501), `tests/e2e/visual/tools.spec.ts` (D61: the TOOLS rows compared with y relative to the TOOLS label, as visual/shell does)
- VERIFIED stabilize-peer-listener · regression (since D62 P5): `tests/e2e/peer-reconnect.spec.ts` failed every run (bisected: green at 2b625d6, red from ad836bb). Stopping the peer listener waited for open connections; a proxied read in flight (the session header's peer `/api/clis`) kept its keep-alive socket and the peer's hellos reused it and got 503 for ~20 s, past the grace. Fix: the peer app force-closes connections on close; regression test in tests/server/peers/reconnect-world.test.ts (fails without the fix)

  - RESOLVED 2026-09-29 (fix/timeline-flake, 346198b + 667d312) · cause 1 (the ❚❚ timeout): the test waited in wall time for playback, 125 `setInterval` ticks of 50 ms nominal, which take ~62 ms each on a quiet machine (sweep 7.5–8.2 s) and ~85 ms under load; the two failing traces from feature/stacked-branches stood at 952/1000 and just reached 1000 at the 10 s timeout.
